# Determinism: whose, how much, and what it bought

Measured 2026-08-06 on the Threadripper (24 cores, load 2–17 throughout, other
agents building). Seed 20260722, centre chunk (64,64), vanilla first in every
comparison. Every reference named here is generated by the **unmodified**
`server-26.2.jar`; nothing in this file is measured against a patched jar,
because no patched jar was built.

The question this file answers: worldgen parity is measured against a vanilla
reference that **disagrees with itself**, and at r=16 that disagreement had
grown to 83% of the overworld residual and 125% of the nether's. An instrument
that noisy cannot tell whether our own changes improved anything. So — can the
reference be made reproducible, and if not, what else sharpens it?

---

## The short version

1. **Vanilla's non-determinism is concurrency, and that is proved, not
   assumed.** Removing concurrency step by step removes the disagreement in
   proportion: 170,797 → 100,130 → 15,007 blocks on a fixed window.
2. **No configuration makes it zero.** Every lever the unmodified server
   honours was tried and the floor bottoms out around 15,000 on a 121-chunk
   nether window. **This is a clean negative and it is recorded so nobody
   repeats it.**
3. **Our own generator is already reproducible** — the overworld provably so
   under real concurrency, the End and nether trivially so because they run
   sequentially today.
4. **The ensemble idea does not work the way it was expected to**, and the
   reason is worth more than the numbers: the floor is not a noisy quantity
   that averages down, it is the size of a *set* that only grows as you
   sample. What an ensemble does buy is a sharper *residual*.
5. **The End is exact.** Our dump is byte-identical to an unmodified vanilla
   run over 1,089 chunks. Not masked, not modulo anything: 0.
6. **A published figure is refuted here** — the End's noise floor is not zero.
7. **The r=16 overworld floor is vanilla's decoration order and nothing else**
   — §11 tests five candidate sources and refutes four, including both harness
   ones, out of the pyramid's own bytecode and out of the floor's spatial
   profile. The one lever we control, `GEN_ORDER=perchunk`, was tried on the
   overworld and makes it 7.6% **worse**. It is irreducible.
8. **So the instrument changed, and the window now has a quotable figure
   again** — §12. Charge a position to us only when our block matches
   *neither* reference, mask nothing, and rule 6's disqualifier has no premise:
   **29,605 of 107,053,056, 99.972345%**. The blind spot the twin mask leaves
   is 29,560 positions wide and is measured at **115 blocks deep**.
9. **Every r=16 overworld number in this file was re-taken on 2026-08-09**,
   because the reference pair that produced the original ones did not survive
   the 2026-08-07 disk failure and a floor belongs to the pair of runs that
   produced it. The regenerated pair's floor is 29,560 where the destroyed
   pair's was 35,531. Sections whose numbers have **not** been re-taken say so
   at the top; §4's N=6 ensemble is the main one.
10. **§12's closing verdict — "do not use this window to adjudicate the next
    worldgen change" — is refuted in §14, and the instrument now has a floor of
    its own.** That verdict compared a charge to the width of a mask the charge
    does not use. Six more references were generated (70 s each, ensemble now
    **eight**) and an unmodified vanilla run substituted for ours is itself
    charged **16,932** at N=2. The verdict splits: this window cannot support a
    **level** claim below N=5 — at N=2 the innocent floor alone spans
    10,136..23,999, which is far worse than §12 feared — but a **delta**
    against a fixed reference set is exact at any N, down to one block, which is
    better. And the window's aggregate floor is not its per-kind floor:
    `grass_block -> dirt` is 646 against a floor of **1**. **A residual needs
    BOTH filters** — vanilla's noise *and* our own chunk-visitation order.
    94.0% of this one is order (853 of 14,270 survive all eight orders), and a
    per-kind vanilla floor is blind to that because it never varies our order.
    Of the 853, 646 are `grass_block -> dirt`.
11. **And §14's ladder was read the wrong way round, because every reference
    and every candidate in it was harvested in one chunk order** — §15. The
    instrument charges an *unmodified vanilla run* **117,628** for having the
    wrong chunk order and **3,804** for being a different run of the right one,
    against our whole residual of 14,270. Vanilla disagrees with itself by
    146,169 across orders and 32,127 within one: **4.6x, so vanilla is not the
    stable one.** Our 14,270 falls to **2,450** with the dump generated in the
    references' own harvest order, or **2,583** by putting both orders into the
    ensemble instead — a ninth same-order reference buys 307, two order-varied
    ones buy 11,687. With order controlled we are charged *less* than an
    innocent vanilla run at every N, which is not a fidelity claim but does mean
    **this window is finished as an adjudicator above ~2,500 blocks.** The
    order-invariant core is unmoved at 852..853.
12. **So the level was re-taken with the order set, and the window turned out
    to carry no exactness figure at all** — §16, 2026-08-10. `29,605 /
    99.972345%` is kept and marked unquotable with its condition named; the
    matched re-take is **2,450 of 107,053,056**, and that sits *inside* the
    **2,202..5,263** the same instrument charges a run that is genuinely
    unmodified vanilla. An exactness quoted against a floor it sits under is
    corroboration, not a measurement — what this window now says is that
    nothing here separates this port from a vanilla run of the same seed, which
    is real, checkable and not a percentage. Deltas remain exact.
13. **§4's ladder was re-taken with it** — §4.1, on eleven references, having
    been declared un-re-takeable when nobody had priced a reference at 70 s.
    The x-major residual ladder converges on ~13,500 and that is **not** a
    defect count: the same ladder with the order set converges on 2,444 five
    rungs earlier. **And §4's standing claim is half wrong** — §4.2. Floors
    really do not move with our code (0 at every N while the residual moves
    3.2x), but "only with the reference set" reads as "with N", and at fixed N
    the **harvest order is worth 4.62x** against 2.92x for the entire climb
    from N=2 to N=9. A floor belongs to its ensemble's N *and* its harvest
    order. The nether was re-taken too and stays corroboration-only: floor
    766,490 against residual 108,479 at N=6, and matching the order buys 27%
    there against the overworld's 83%.
14. **A SECOND SEED, MATCHED FROM THE FIRST MEASUREMENT, DOES ADJUDICATE —
    §17, 2026-08-10.** Seed 12345, same geometry, eight fresh stamped
    references, dump order read off `HARVEST-ORDER` before any level was taken:
    **69,288 of 107,053,056 — 99.935277% — against an innocent-vanilla floor of
    2,577..4,835 at the same N.** Fourteen times its floor, `quotable = true`,
    and the project's only quotable worldgen exactness. Two things it settles
    that entries 11 and 12 could have been over-read into: **chunk order is not
    a constant fraction of a residual** — 6.7% here against 83% on 20260722 —
    and a window with no exactness is a property of *that window*, not of the
    instrument. The residual is one nameable thing: this port writes **no
    `blue_ice` at all** where two vanilla runs write 17,718 and 17,741, and
    seed 20260722's window could never have found it because it has no frozen
    ocean in it.

---

## 1. The cause: concurrency, demonstrated rather than argued

`ChunkMap` dispatches every chunk status upgrade onto `Util.backgroundExecutor()`,
whose width is `Mth.clamp(availableProcessors() - 1, 1, getMaxThreads())` and
whose `getMaxThreads()` reads the system property `max.bg.threads` (accepted in
1..255, default 255). Read out of the bytecode of the pinned jar with `javap`;
`max.bg.threads` is the **only** system property in the whole server jar that
touches worldgen. The complete set the server reads is `max.bg.threads`,
`management.tls.keystore.password`, and the `java.*` / `os.*` / `user.dir`
fields it puts in a crash report.

Probe window: the nether corner that carries most of the r=16 floor — chunks
70..80 squared, 121 chunks, `--ybox 0 127`. Two independent harvests per
configuration; the number is their disagreement with each other.

| configuration | mismatches | exactness |
|---|---|---|
| `GEN_ORDER=rows`, default pool (23 workers) | 170,797 | 95.692305% |
| `GEN_ORDER=perchunk`, `-Dmax.bg.threads=1` | 100,130 | 97.474607% |
| the above, plus `taskset -c <one core>` | **15,007** | 99.621506% |
| the above, plus `-XX:ActiveProcessorCount=1 -XX:+UseSerialGC -Xbatch -XX:TieredStopAtLevel=1` | 20,141 | 99.492021% |

The disagreement falls by 91.2% as concurrency is removed and stops falling
when there is no more concurrency to remove. That gradient is the proof. A
non-deterministic *value* source — an unseeded RNG, a clock, a hash of an
address — would not care how many threads were running.

**The knob provably turned.** With `-Dmax.bg.threads=1` the live server has
**two** `Worker-Main` threads against the default 23 (`ps -T`), and the log
carries no `Wrong ... property value` warning, so the property parsed. (This
paragraph read "exactly one" until 2026-08-18. It is two, and the difference
is the whole finding of §46: the constructor argument is a `ForkJoinPool`'s
target *parallelism*, and such a pool spawns compensation workers, so the
clamp narrows the pool without serialising it.)

> ⚠ **CORRECTED 2026-08-18 — the knob turned, but it is not a thread cap and
> it does not clamp the harvest.** Read from the pinned jar's bytecode,
> `net.minecraft.util.Util.makeExecutor(String)` does this:
>
> ```
>  0: invokestatic  maxAllowedExecutorThreads:()I     <- the clamped value, n
>  5: ifgt          15                                <- n <= 0 -> direct executor
> 24: new           java/util/concurrent/ForkJoinPool
> 42: invokespecial ForkJoinPool."<init>":
>                     (ILjava/util/concurrent/ForkJoinPool$ForkJoinWorkerThreadFactory;
>                      Ljava/lang/Thread$UncaughtExceptionHandler;Z)V
> ```
>
> `n` is the constructor's **`parallelism`** argument, not a maximum thread
> count, and `asyncMode` is `true`. `ForkJoinPool` treats parallelism as a
> *target* level of concurrency and spawns **compensation workers** beyond it
> whenever a worker blocks, so a pool built with parallelism 1 is not a pool
> that runs one task at a time. `ps -T` showing one `Worker-Main` at rest is
> consistent with that and is not evidence against it.
>
> Measured in the nether, the clamped pairs came in at **34 / 438 / 460**
> against default-pool pairs at **75 / 134 / 151** — the clamped spread is
> *wider*, which is the opposite of what a clamp would do and is what settled
> it. **Every floor this project publishes is an UNCLAMPED floor**, carried
> with its N and never subtracted from a residual. The rows below stand as
> measurements of what the property does; what does not stand is reading them
> as measurements of a serialised harvest. The last row is
*worse* than the row above it, which is the honest signal that both are
sampling the same residual noise rather than tracking a trend.

**And the families that move are the ones the hypothesis named.** The
disagreements are `basalt ↔ blackstone ↔ netherrack` in both directions — the
`basalt_deltas` signature, where a `count_on_every_layer` placement finds its
ground y by reading a neighbour chunk that may or may not have decorated yet.
That is a feature that crosses a chunk border, which is exactly the class that
should be order-sensitive.

**It did not agree by generating less.** Census over the same 121 chunks,
control against the most-serialised run: basalt 1,774,424 vs 1,773,600,
netherrack 465,256 vs 463,721, blackstone 328,631 vs 330,485, air 898,294 vs
898,359, bedrock 185,898 vs 185,898, and `full=121` chunks in both. The
material is all still there and in the same quantity; what changed is where the
order-sensitive share of it landed.

## 2. The configuration answer is NO, and this is the record of it

`GEN_ORDER=perchunk` (added to `ci/gen-vanilla-region.sh`) issues one
`forceload add` per chunk in a fixed row-major order and does not send the next
until the previous chunk has reached FULL on disk. That is the strongest
ordering control available without touching the jar, and it is not enough.

The reason is structural: for a chunk to reach FULL its neighbours must reach
FEATURES, so forceloading one chunk decorates a small batch of chunks, and the
order *within* that batch is the scheduler's. Pinning to one core removes the
parallelism but not the interleaving — the main thread and the worker still
timeshare, and the kernel decides when.

**Do not try this again.** The levers are exhausted: the pool width, the
generation order, the CPU count the VM believes in, the collector, and the JIT
policy. What is left would have to change the jar.

## 3. Our own generator

Three dumps at the default worker count and one at `RUSTMC_WORKERS=1`, r=16,
compared with `cmp` on the region files and with `region-differ` on the blocks:

| dimension | region files differing | block mismatches |
|---|---|---|
| overworld | 0 of 4, every pair | 0 of 107,053,056 |
| the End | 0 of 4, every pair | 0 of 71,368,704 |
| nether | 0 of 4, every pair | 0 of 35,684,352 |

**The overworld result is the one that means what it looks like.** At r=8 the
overworld dump runs `wall=27.35 cpu=27.12` at one worker and
`wall=11.36 cpu=32.98` at 24 — a real 2.9x of concurrency — so three
byte-identical multi-threaded runs are evidence about thread order.

**The End and nether results are weaker and must be quoted as such.** For both
subcommands `RUSTMC_WORKERS` is a no-op today: `full-end` is
`wall=10.23 cpu=10.09` at one worker and `wall=10.58 cpu=10.43` at 24;
`full-nether` is `12.19/12.00` against `13.30/13.10`. Those pipelines are
entirely sequential, so the test ran the same sequential path four times. The
property a player cares about — same seed, same world, every time — **is**
established. Freedom from thread-order dependence is **not**, because no
threads ran. If either pipeline is ever parallelised, this measurement has to
be retaken before the claim carries over.

No non-determinism was found on our side, so none was fixed. That is the whole
of it, and it is recorded here so the audit is not repeated: no hash-ordered
iteration reached output, no RNG was seeded off anything but seed and position,
and no float accumulation ordering moved a block.

## 4. The ensemble, and why the metric it was meant to improve was the wrong one

`ci/floor-ensemble.sh` builds the twin mask from N references instead of two.
With the first reference as primary:

* `floor_N` — positions where the N runs are **not unanimous**, the union over
  j of the disagreements between ref1 and refj;
* `residual_N` — positions where they **are** unanimous and we differ, the
  intersection over j of `diff ref1 ours --twin refj`.

Six independent unmodified-vanilla references per dimension, all
`GEN_ORDER=rows`, r=16, seed 20260722. At N=2 both quantities reproduce the
published figures exactly, which is how the tool was checked.

> ⚠ **The overworld column of this table is stale, audited 2026-08-07.** It was
> taken before `village-savanna`, which moved `residual_N=6` from **37,471 to
> 24,402**, and before `df30334`, which moved `residual_N=2` by a further −37
> and was never re-run at N=6. **There is no current N=6 overworld figure**, and
> 24,402 − 37 is an inference rather than a measurement — do not publish it. The
> nether and End columns are unaffected: no merge since has touched either
> dimension's generation. The floors do not move with our code at all, only with
> the reference set, so all three floor rows stand.
>
> ⚠⚠ **AND THE REFERENCE SET ITSELF IS GONE — 2026-08-09.** The last sentence
> above was true of our code and false about this table's future. The r=16
> references were lost in the 2026-08-07 disk failure: `ls oracle-work` finds
> no surviving ensemble reference for any dimension, and the only r=16
> overworld pair on the box is the one regenerated on 2026-08-09, whose floor
> over this window is **29,560** rather than the 35,531 in the `floor N=2`
> row. **So the overworld `floor N=2` row is dead, and the N=6 ladder cannot
> be re-taken at all** short of generating six fresh references. Every row
> here is kept as the record of a measurement that was made and none of them
> is now an available instrument. What does survive is the *reasoning*: a
> floor is the size of a union and can only grow with N, and floor/residual is
> therefore the wrong measure of resolving power. Neither of those depends on
> which references seeded the ladder.
>
> ✅ **AND IT WAS RE-TAKEN — 2026-08-10. "Short of generating six fresh
> references" turned out to be seven minutes.** A reference costs **70 s**
> (`ci/order-vanilla-refs.sh`), which nobody had measured when the sentence
> above was written; the overworld ensemble is now **eleven** and the nether
> has **eight**. §4.1 below is the current ladder and this table is history.
> The prohibition that survives is narrower than the one written here and
> sharper: not *"the ladder cannot be re-taken"* but **"a floor may not be
> carried between ensembles"** — and, since ORACLE rule 29, not between
> harvest orders either, which is the larger of the two terms and is the one
> this table's own `floor N=2` row was silently varying.

| | overworld | nether | the End |
|---|---|---|---|
| floor N=2 | 35,531 | 317,725 | 0 |
| floor N=6 | **73,820** | **668,532** | **252** |
| residual N=2 | 43,041 | 254,799 | 125 |
| residual N=6 | **37,471** | **151,434** | **0** |
| denominator at N=6 | 106,979,236 | 35,015,820 | 71,368,452 |
| exactness at N=6 | 99.964974% | 99.567527% | **100.000000%** |
| floor/residual at N=6 | 1.97 | 4.42 | — |

**Quote the block count, not the percentage.** The denominator is the window
less whatever was masked out, so it moves with N, and the percentage improves
partly because the denominator shrank. The count does not have that problem.

Pairwise floors across all 15 pairs: overworld min 23,463, median 31,322, max
36,431 (1.55x); nether min 255,552, median 295,514, max 349,430 (1.37x); the
End min 0, median 125, max 252.

**The floor does not average down, and it never will.** It is not a noisy
estimate of a fixed quantity — it is the measured size of the set of positions
vanilla does not reproduce, and every extra reference can only add to that set.
Overworld 35,531 → 53,390 → 60,680 → 67,588 → 73,820, still climbing by ~6,000
per reference at N=6 — destroyed pair and destroyed ensemble, 2026-08-09; the
shape of the ladder is the finding, the rungs are no longer reproducible. So **floor/residual gets worse with N by construction**,
and asking "at what N does the nether become quotable by that ratio" has no
answer: at no N, and the ratio is not the right question. The nether at r=16
stays corroboration-only.

**What the ensemble does buy is a residual that means more.** Each reference
added exonerates positions where vanilla A and B happened to agree and a third
run does not:

```text
  overworld  43,041 → 38,706 → 38,007 → 37,901 → 37,471   (-12.9%)
  nether    254,799 → 176,220 → 162,414 → 158,837 → 151,434   (-40.6%)
  the End       125 →    125 →      0 →       0 →       0
```

The overworld's has nearly converged — the last reference moved it by 1.1% —
so ~37,500 is a defect count rather than a noise measurement, and 5,570 blocks
of the published 43,041 were never ours. The nether's is still moving by ~4.7%
per reference and has **not** converged; 151,434 is an upper bound that will
keep falling.

So the honest summary of the ensemble is: **it does not restore resolving power
by shrinking the noise, it restores it by removing spurious charges.** For the
End that was decisive. For the nether it removed 40% of the residual and still
left the window unquotable.

## 4.1 The ladder as it stands — 2026-08-10, with the harvest order on every rung

Everything in this subsection: seed 20260722, centre chunk (64,64), r=16, 1,089
chunks, bbox `48 48 80 80`, 107,053,056 blocks, full height, **vanilla first**,
Threadripper, branch `floor-ladder-retake`. Eleven overworld references:
**a..i harvested `GEN_ORDER=rows`, rr-a and rr-b harvested `rows_rev`.**

`ci/floor-ladder.py` computes every rung as a set operation on the
`<r1>-vs-<r2>.pos` matrix rather than by re-diffing, which is what makes a
nine-rung ladder cost seconds; the definitions are `ci/floor-ensemble.sh`'s,
rearranged. **It refuses unless it reproduces `ow-r16-floor-n2` — 29,560,
taken by diffing — and it does, to the block.** It deliberately does *not*
refuse on the twin-masked residual, because that number reads our dump and this
tree has moved since `main` 8e283f57: on the same reference pair it is now
**20,143 against 29,490, a delta of −9,347**, which is the branch working.

### Three things every rung carries, and one it did not before

* **N**, because a floor is the size of a union and can only grow with it.
* **`p_hide(N) = 1 − 0.05^(1/N)`** — the largest per-reference probability of
  a defect being hidden at which N references still have 95% power to catch
  it. N=3 hides 63.2%, N=6 hides 39.3%, N=10 hides 25.9%.
* **the harvest order of the references**, which is new and is the reason this
  is a re-take rather than a re-run.

```text
  references a..i, all harvested rows        our x-major dump (`ours`)
    N      floor   residual    denominator   p_hide
    2     29,560     20,143    107,023,496    77.6%
    3     46,260     16,314    107,006,796    63.2%
    4     55,640     15,615    106,997,416    52.7%
    5     64,749     14,948    106,988,307    45.1%
    6     70,583     14,440    106,982,473    39.3%
    7     79,317     13,914    106,973,739    34.8%
    8     82,599     13,837    106,970,457    31.2%
    9     86,240     13,535    106,966,816    28.3%

  references a..i, all harvested rows        our `zx` dump — MATCHED ORDER
    2     29,560      6,309    107,023,496    77.6%
    3     46,260      3,213    107,006,796    63.2%
    4     55,640      2,631    106,997,416    52.7%
    5     64,749      2,613    106,988,307    45.1%
    6     70,583      2,444    106,982,473    39.3%
    7     79,317      2,444    106,973,739    34.8%
    8     82,599      2,444    106,970,457    31.2%
    9     86,240      2,436    106,966,816    28.3%

  a..i then rr-a, rr-b — MIXED HARVEST ORDERS
   10    200,832      2,213    106,852,224    25.9%   (x-major)
   11    213,199      2,182    106,839,857    23.8%   (x-major)
   10    200,832      2,064    106,852,224    25.9%   (`zx`)
   11    213,199      2,021    106,839,857    23.8%   (`zx`)
```

**There is no exactness column, and its absence is deliberate.** The
denominator is the window less the floor, so it moves with N and any percentage
computed from it improves partly because the mask grew — which is §4's own
"quote the block count, not the percentage", one paragraph above the table it
was violated in. The first draft of this subsection printed nine of them and
`ci/check-figures.py` refused the document: nineteen unregistered percentages,
each of which a reader could have lifted. `ci/floor-ladder.py` no longer
computes the column at all, because a number that must not be quoted is best
not produced.

### What the re-take overturns in the table above it

The old table read its residual ladder — 43,041 → 37,471, the last step 1.1% —
as convergence, and concluded *"~37,500 is a defect count rather than a noise
measurement"*. Run the same argument on the x-major column here and you get the
same conclusion: 20,143 → 13,535, the last step 2.2%, apparently converging on
a defect count of about 13,500.

**It is not a defect count. It is mostly chunk order.** The `zx` column is the
identical ladder with one condition set, and it converges on **2,444 from
N=6** — a quantity 5.5x smaller that stops moving five rungs earlier.
Convergence of a residual under an uncontrolled condition means the condition
has stopped varying, which is not the same as the residual having stopped
containing it. The old conclusion was not wrong about the arithmetic; it was
reading a converged number as a clean one.

### The floor climbs, and now it is visible what it climbs with

```text
  floor  29,560 → 46,260 → 55,640 → 64,749 → 70,583 → 79,317 → 82,599 → 86,240
  step         +16,700  +9,380  +9,109  +5,834  +8,734  +3,282  +3,641
  then, adding two references of the OTHER harvest order:
        86,240 → 200,832 → 213,199
  step             +114,592  +12,367
```

**One reference of the other order adds more to the floor than the previous
seven of the same order put together.** That is the whole of §4's standing
claim in one line, and it is the next subsection.

## 4.2 §4's standing claim, tested rather than repeated

§4 has said since it was written: *"The floors do not move with our code at
all, only with the reference set."* It was quoted to justify keeping the floor
rows when the residual rows went stale. It is now testable — hold the reference
set fixed and vary our code, hold our code fixed and vary the reference set —
and the verdict is that **one half is true and trivially so, and the other half
is true and misleading, which is the worse failure of the two.**

### Half one — floors do not move with our code. TRUE.

True by construction rather than by luck: `floor_N` is a union over *reference
pairs* and never reads our dump at all. The measurement is still worth taking,
because "the floor did not move" and "the floor is not being recomputed" look
identical in a log. Holding the reference set fixed and swapping our dump
between two chunk orders — the largest swing our own code has available, the
two dumps differing from each other by ~200,000 blocks:

```text
   N    floor      residual, x-major dump    residual, `zx` dump
   2   29,560                     20,143                  6,309
   3   46,260                     16,314                  3,213
   4   55,640                     15,615                  2,631
   5   64,749                     14,948                  2,613
   6   70,583                     14,440                  2,444
   7   79,317                     13,914                  2,444
   8   82,599                     13,837                  2,444
   9   86,240                     13,535                  2,436
```

The floor is **identical at every N**; the residual moves by up to 3.2x. The
two are really being computed separately, and the first half stands.

### Half two — floors move only with the reference set. TRUE AND MISLEADING.

It credits *the reference set* and says nothing about which property of it, and
every reader — including this document, four paragraphs above — has taken it to
mean the count. The count is not the dominant term. Holding N fixed and varying
only whether the references share a harvest order:

```text
   N=2  one harvest order    min  24,526  med  32,500  max  39,085  (n=36)
        mixed harvest orders min 144,137  med 150,079  max 154,398  (n=18)
        ratio of medians                4.62x
   N=3  one harvest order    min  41,578  med  48,647  max  52,375  (n=60)
        mixed harvest orders min 160,004  med 165,582  max 170,204  (n=60)
        ratio of medians                3.40x
   N=4  one harvest order    min  53,935  med  58,371  max  62,152  (n=60)
        mixed harvest orders min 169,895  med 175,049  max 180,536  (n=60)
        ratio of medians                3.00x
```

**At fixed N, the harvest order is worth 4.62x and the whole climb from N=2 to
N=9 is worth 2.92x.** The 4.62 is not a coincidence and is not a re-quote: it
is ORACLE rule 29's 4.6 — 146,169 across orders against 32,127 within one —
arriving from a completely different direction, as a ratio of median floors
over 36 and 18 reference subsets rather than as a ratio of two diffs.

**So the claim as written licensed the operation that costs the most.** Read as
"floors move with N", it says a floor may be carried between two ensembles of
the same size — which is exactly the move that is worth several times the
residual the floor would be quoted beside. It is not retracted, because it is
not false. It is replaced by the form that does not license it:

> **A floor belongs to the ensemble that produced it: to its N, and to its
> harvest order. A rung without both beside it is not quotable.**

## 4.3 The harvest order of a..h, measured rather than assumed

§15 says every r=16 reference in this project was harvested `GEN_ORDER=rows`,
and says it because that is how the generator was always driven. An assertion
about how a tool was driven is the exact class of thing this project keeps
finding to be false — `gamerule randomTickSpeed 0` was "sent" for a week — and
it does not have to be an assertion. Two vanilla runs of the same order differ
by ~32,000 blocks on this window and two of different orders by ~150,000, which
is a gap with nothing in it, so the order can be **read off the pairwise
matrix**:

```text
  ref     median vs known-rows   median vs known-rows_rev   verdict
  a                    32,127                    148,520    rows      4.6x
  b                    34,813                    149,154    rows      4.3x
  c                    36,449                    147,498    rows      4.0x
  d                    32,838                    150,305    rows      4.6x
  e                    34,122                    150,384    rows      4.4x
  f                    35,526                    150,683    rows      4.2x
  g                    24,526                    151,296    rows      6.2x
  h                    39,085                    151,202    rows      3.9x
  rr-a                149,216                     36,810    rows_rev  4.1x
  rr-b                153,815                     36,810    rows_rev  4.2x
```

Ten references, ten unambiguous verdicts, minimum separation 3.9x. `harvest =
"rows"` in `ci/figures.toml` is now a measurement. The three references
generated on 2026-08-09/10 carry a real `HARVEST-ORDER` file; a..h predate the
stamp and this table is what licenses their entry in the ledger.

## 4.4 The nether column, re-taken — and it stays corroboration-only

`ci/nether-ladder-refs.sh`, eight fresh references: **a..f harvested `rows`,
rr-a and rr-b harvested `rows_rev`.** Seed 20260722, r=16, centre (64,64),
`--ybox 0 127`, 35,684,352 blocks, vanilla first, Threadripper, 2026-08-10.

**There is no registered nether floor for this window, and the tool says so
out loud rather than skipping quietly** — `ci/floor-ladder.py` prints a
`!! NO SELF-CHECK` line naming the block count, because an unchecked
reconstruction that looks checked is worse than one that admits it is not. The
first run of it here was *refused*, correctly and for the wrong reason: the
self-check was hard-coded to the overworld's 29,560 and fired at the nether's
377,949.

```text
  references a..f, harvested rows          x-major dump      `zx` dump
    N       floor       residual            residual
    2     377,949        226,945             180,252
    3     562,567        138,199             100,686
    4     659,792        117,685              89,042
    5     696,446        113,007              80,646
    6     766,490        108,479              79,334
  then rr-a, rr-b — MIXED HARVEST ORDERS
    7   1,095,497         90,146              76,354
    8   1,172,835         87,483              75,693
```

**The floor exceeds the residual by 7x at N=6 and the gap widens with N.** §4's
verdict on the nether — "it removed 40% of the residual and still left the
window unquotable" — survives a completely fresh ensemble. The r=16 nether
carries no level claim and asking at what N it would has no answer: the floor
grows faster than the residual falls.

**Matching the order helps here too, and much less.** 108,479 → 79,334 at N=6
is a 27% reduction, against the overworld's 14,440 → 2,444, an 83% one. So the
overworld residual was mostly chunk order and the nether's is mostly not — a
real difference between the two dimensions and not an artefact of the
instrument, since it is the same instrument, the same knob and the same day.

The standing claim tests the same way and weaker: the floor is identical under
both dumps at every N, and the harvest-order term at fixed N is **1.85x at
N=2** (medians 366,675 one-order against 679,055 mixed) against the overworld's
4.62x. Still the dominant single term; still not the count.

**The admissible instrument agrees, which is worth stating because it is a
different instrument.** Charging a position only where our block matches no
reference, with the order matched and the innocent-vanilla floor beside it:

```text
    N    charge      innocent floor  min      med      max
    2   197,250                  114,497  184,323  269,217
    3   110,947                   70,822  123,926  197,334   inside
    4    96,855                   49,102   94,226  157,381
    5    85,181                   37,951   80,241  111,015   inside
    6    83,681                        —        —        —   no floor at this N
```

Our charge is inside the innocent range at N=3 and again at N=5 and never far
outside it, so the two instruments reach the same verdict from opposite
constructions — one masks the disputed ground, the other adjudicates against
it. At N=6 every reference is in the ensemble and there is no candidate left to
be innocent, which the table says rather than printing a floor of zero.

## 4.5 What the other dimensions cost, at 70 s a reference

Written down because "we cannot afford it" was the reason §4's ladder sat
un-re-taken for a day, and nobody had priced it.

| dimension | r=16 references on the box | to re-take an N=6 ladder | verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| overworld | 11 (9 `rows`, 2 `rows_rev`) | done, 2026-08-10 | §4.1 |
| nether | 8 (6 `rows`, 2 `rows_rev`) | done, 2026-08-10 | §4.4 |
| the End | **0 at r=16**; one r=7 city pair | 8 x 70 s ≈ 10 min + a dump + 56 diffs | re-takeable, not taken |
| overworld seed 12345 | 2, harvest **unrecorded** | 6 more ≈ 7 min | re-takeable |
| overworld seed 31415926 | 0 | 8 x 70 s ≈ 10 min | re-takeable |

**Everything is re-takeable and nothing here is expensive.** A reference is 70 s
and a full ordered diff matrix over eleven of them is about 30 minutes of
`region-differ`; the whole overworld re-take above, references included, was
under an hour of box time. The thing that was actually scarce was not compute,
it was **a recorded harvest order** — which is now a required field
(`ci/check-figures.py`) and a stamped file (`ci/gen-vanilla-region.sh`).

**The End is the one worth taking next and it is not taken here**, for a reason
that is about what it would show rather than what it would cost: §5 records the
End's floor as 252 across six references and **not** the 0 that was published
off one lucky pair, and §6 closes the End at 100.000000% over the *r=7 city*
window against a twinned pair. Those two windows are different and the second
is the one the closure rests on. An r=16 End ladder would be a new instrument
on a dimension that already has a closed one, and it should be taken by whoever
next needs to move an End figure — with the p_hide of its N beside it, because
252-versus-0 is precisely what a low N buys you.

## 5. A published figure this refutes: the End's floor is not zero

`PARITY.md` §2, §6 and `WIDE-WINDOW-R16-ALL-DIMENSIONS.md` all state that the
End's r=16 noise floor is **exactly zero**, and draw a strong conclusion from
it: *"there is no position vanilla is unsure about, so there is nowhere for a
defect to hide."*

That conclusion is false. The floor of that particular **pair** is zero. Across
six references the End's floor is **252**, and the pairwise numbers are 0, 46,
81, 125, 171 and 206 among others. Two runs agreeing exactly was luck, and it
is precisely the shape §4 of `PARITY.md` warns about — *"two samples cannot find
every position a third order would move."* The End is very nearly
order-deterministic in vanilla, which is a different and much weaker statement
than "there is nowhere for a defect to hide".

Nothing about our own End numbers gets worse for this. They get better; see
below.

## 6. The End is exact

Our r=16 End dump against each of the six unmodified references, **unmasked**,
vanilla first, over 71,368,704 blocks:

```text
  van-end-20260722-r16-a    125
  van-end-20260722-r16-b    125
  det-ens-end-c             171
  det-ens-end-d               0        <-- byte-identical
  det-ens-end-e              46
  det-ens-end-f             206
```

**Our generator reproduces an unmodified vanilla run of this seed and window
bit for bit.** Not masked, not modulo chunk-visitation order, not to six
decimal places: zero disagreeing blocks in 1,089 chunks against a world the
real server wrote.

Consistently, `residual_N` against the six-reference stable set is **0**: every
position where we differ from reference A is a position vanilla itself does not
reproduce. And independently, `ci/decorate-order-intersect.sh` over the same
window gives an **order-invariant core of 0** — eight distinct traversal
digests, per-order totals 125, 125, 127, 1230, 1105, 168, 637, 125, 125, and no
position surviving all of them. All 125 blocks against reference A are
`chorus_plant`/`chorus_flower`, and the chorus totals bracket vanilla's
(14,068 against 14,083).

Three independent routes — one reference matched exactly, a six-reference
stable set at zero, and an order intersection at zero — agree. The End at r=16
is closed.

**And it is three seeds, not one.** Three fresh references per seed, same
window, ours unmasked against each, vanilla first:

```text
  seed 31415926     a  33      b   0      c   0     vanilla a:b 33, a:c 33, b:c 0
  seed 12345        a   0      b   0      c 287     vanilla a:b  0, a:c 287, b:c 287
  seed 20260722     see the six-reference table above; d is 0
```

On every seed our dump is byte-identical to at least one unmodified vanilla
run, and in each case to the **majority** answer — the two references that
agree with each other, never the odd one out. That is a stronger statement than
"we match a vanilla run": where vanilla is inconsistent we land on the answer
vanilla gives most often, and where vanilla is consistent we match it. There is
no seed here on which the End has a defect.

**And the control that says this is a property of the End and not of the
method.** The same comparison in the other two dimensions, ours against each of
the six references, unmasked, finds nothing near zero:

```text
  overworld   63,266  58,701  60,216  54,819  53,310  59,598
  nether     426,413 415,991 348,153 404,013 408,712 364,863
```

So "generate enough references and one of them will match" is not what
happened. The End matches because the End is right.

The zero is not a vacuous comparison either — the differ's own line is
`DIFF chunks=1089 (a=1369 b=1089) blocks=71368704 mismatches=0
biomes=1115136/0`, so 1,089 chunks and 71.3 million blocks were actually
compared and 1.1 million biome cells with them.

## 7. What a human sees

`region-differ topdown` renders the highest non-air block per column to a PNG.
Vanilla and ours, same seed, same square, r=16:

* **Overworld** — the same world. Coastline, islands, sand, tree distribution
  and the bay all land in the same places. With the residual overlaid and
  filtered to y ≥ 62, the visible disagreement is **two blobs and nothing
  else**: the two `village_savanna` starts this port does not place. Confirmed
  by census rather than by eye — vanilla has 250 `acacia_planks`, 35
  `hay_block`, 1 `bell` and 1 `composter` in the window and **we have none of
  any of them**, while `oak_planks` is 4,136 against 4,142. Zoomed in, vanilla
  has houses, a path and a farm plot; we have grass and trees.
* **Nether** — the same world. Crimson and warped patches, soul sand valley,
  lava lakes and the basalt deltas corner all in the same places; the
  differences are texture inside the deltas, which is the order-sensitive
  family.
* **The End** — indistinguishable, as it should be given §6.

**A missing village is very visible and a 0.013% move in the exactness figure.**
That asymmetry is the argument for keeping this renderer in the loop: the
percentage cannot see the one defect a player would notice first.

Images are in `~/det-logs/renders/` on the build machine and
`/home/sayer/grit/determinism-renders/` on the laptop.

## 8. What was not built, and the decision that belongs to the owner

**No patched jar was built.** The evidence above says one *would* work — the
cause is ordering, an ordering-only patch is therefore sufficient in principle,
and §1's gradient shows how far ordering alone gets. What stopped it is that
the case for it got weaker as the measurements came in, and the decision is
not mine:

* The End, the dimension nearest 100%, **did not need it**: it is already
  bit-identical to a real vanilla run.
* The overworld's remaining residual had converged to ~37,500 at the time of
  writing (24,402 after `village-savanna`, and not re-measured since) and is
  dominated by **structures that are not implemented** — chamber interiors and
  sixteen unbuilt structure sets; the two villages have since landed. A quieter
  reference does not build them. **The argument got stronger, not weaker,
  after this was written:** the N=2 residual has now fallen *below* the N=2
  floor, so the twin-masked instrument is out of resolving power on this
  window.

  ⚠ **The clause that used to end this bullet — "a deterministic reference
  is the only thing that would restore it. That makes the decision below more
  live, not less" — is wrong, and it is corrected here rather than deleted
  because it was the argument for building a patched jar.** §12 restores
  resolving power on this window with no patched jar, no new reference and no
  new generation at all: it stops masking. A deterministic reference would
  still be worth having — it would make deltas cheaper and put the End's kind
  of byte-identity within reach elsewhere — but it is no longer load-bearing
  for publishing an overworld figure, so the decision below is **less** urgent
  than this paragraph claimed, not more.
* The nether's residual has not converged and is dominated by unimplemented
  bastions plus order-sensitive basalt deltas. Same conclusion.

There is also a claim question that is the owner's and not an implementation
detail. This project's public sentence is that it measures against **unmodified
vanilla**, and that is currently true of every number it publishes. A
deterministic reference is a legitimate instrument and figures derived from it
can be published honestly — but only if they are labelled as such, kept
alongside the unmodified-vanilla figures rather than replacing them, and never
folded into the "unmodified vanilla" sentence. If the patch is built, that
separation has to be built with it.

## 9. Reproducing all of it

```bash
# the configuration gradient (two harvests per row, compare them to each other)
GEN_ORDER=rows      ci/gen-vanilla-region.sh 20260722 5 oracle-work/ctl-a 26111 75 75 nether
VANILLA_JVM_OPTS=-Dmax.bg.threads=1 GEN_ORDER=perchunk \
                    ci/gen-vanilla-region.sh 20260722 5 oracle-work/pc-a  26121 75 75 nether
VANILLA_JVM_OPTS=-Dmax.bg.threads=1 GEN_ORDER=perchunk \
  taskset -c 0      ci/gen-vanilla-region.sh 20260722 5 oracle-work/pin-a 26131 75 75 nether
region-differ diff oracle-work/pin-a oracle-work/pin-b --bbox 70 70 80 80 --ybox 0 127

# the ensemble
ENS_REFS="oracle-work/van-end-20260722-r16-a oracle-work/van-end-20260722-r16-b \
          oracle-work/det-ens-end-c oracle-work/det-ens-end-d \
          oracle-work/det-ens-end-e oracle-work/det-ens-end-f" \
ENS_OURS=/dev/shm/det-ours-end-1/region ENS_BBOX="48 48 80 80" \
ENS_OUT=/dev/shm/ens-end ENS_PAIRWISE=1 ci/floor-ensemble.sh

# the picture
region-differ topdown oracle-work/gen-wide-20260722-r16-ow-a \
    --bbox 48 48 80 80 --scale 2 --out /tmp/ow-vanilla.png
region-differ topdown /dev/shm/det-ours-ow-1/region \
    --bbox 48 48 80 80 --scale 2 --mark /tmp/res-y62.txt --out /tmp/ow-grit.png
```

## 10. One thing this run got wrong, kept because it is the house lesson

The first version of `ci/floor-ensemble.sh` filtered `--positions` output with
`awk 'NF>=5'`. `region-differ` writes position lines into the same stream as
its summary, its confusion table and its y histogram, and a summary line has
five fields too. A line that appears in *every* run survives *every*
intersection, so the script reported the End's residual falling to **1** at
N=6 — a spectacular result which was the string `DIFF chunks=1089 (a=1369`
intersecting with itself five times.

It was caught only because the differ's own counter said 125 and the filter
yielded 165 lines. The script now refuses to run unless those two agree, and
`ci/decorate-order-intersect.sh` had the correct anchored filter all along.
Rule 7 has now paid out fifteen times.

---

## 11. What the r=16 overworld floor is actually made of

> ⚠ **DESTROYED PAIR — 2026-08-09. Every number in this section is arithmetic
> on the 35,531 position lines of a reference pair that no longer exists.**
> `gen-wide-20260722-r16-ow-a` and `-b` were lost in the 2026-08-07 disk
> failure and regenerated from scratch, and a floor belongs to the pair of
> runs that produced it, so this decomposition cannot be reproduced from this
> tree. It is kept because its *conclusion* is the point of the section, and
> the conclusion was spot-checked on the regenerated pair, whose floor over
> the same window is **29,560**:
>
> ```text
>   floor positions                                29,560
>   involving sulfur / sulfur_spike / cinnabar     11,246   (38.0%)
>   largest families:  diorite vs granite   1,725
>                      cave_air vs sulfur   1,566
>                      granite vs diorite   1,552
>                      andesite vs diorite  1,527
>                      diorite vs andesite  1,519
>                      sulfur vs cinnabar   1,503
> ```
>
> Same story, different digits: overlapping stone-variant ore blobs and the
> sulfur cave, both of which read the world before they write it. The
> per-family arithmetic below has **not** been re-derived on the regenerated
> pair — read its figures as history and its argument as current.

Measured 2026-08-07, against the same two references every published r=16
overworld figure was taken against — `gen-wide-20260722-r16-ow-a` and its `-b`
twin, both from the unmodified `server-26.2.jar`. One `region-differ diff a b
--bbox 48 48 80 80 --positions` run, and every number below is arithmetic on
its 35,531 position lines. The filter is anchored and the parsed count is
checked against the differ's own `mismatches=` counter before anything is
computed (§10's lesson, enforced this time rather than remembered).

§1 proved the *cause* is concurrency, on a nether window. This section asks
the next question — **is any of the overworld floor ours?** — because a floor
built of harness artefact is reducible and a floor built of vanilla's own
thread order is not. Five candidate sources, tested rather than assumed. Four
are refuted and the fifth is the whole of it.

### Refuted: partially populated chunk edges

The obvious suspect is that a chunk at the edge of the saved square never
received the feature writes its neighbour owed it. **The bytecode says that
cannot happen.** In `ChunkPyramid`'s `GENERATION_PYRAMID`, `FEATURES` is the
**only** step of the twelve that declares a non-zero `blockStateWriteRadius` —
`javap -c` shows four calls to `ChunkStep$Builder.blockStateWriteRadius`, one
`iconst_1` and three `iconst_0` — so decoration is the only thing that writes
outside its own chunk, and it reaches exactly one chunk. And the promotion
order closes it: `LIGHT` calls `addRequirement(INITIALIZE_LIGHT, 1)`,
`INITIALIZE_LIGHT` adds no requirement of its own beyond its parent
`FEATURES`, and `ChunkStep$Builder.buildAccumulatedDependencies` composes
through `getRadiusOfParent` — so a chunk cannot reach `LIGHT`, and therefore
cannot reach `FULL`, until all eight of its neighbours have finished
`FEATURES`. Every chunk `region-differ` loads is `Status=full`. A full chunk
is never owed a write.

### Refuted: the window boundary

The floor per chunk by Chebyshev ring from the centre, against our residual
over the same rings:

```text
  ring   chunks    floor  per chunk     resid  per chunk
     0        1        0        0.0         0        0.0
     3       24     2710      112.9       131        5.5
     5       40     3593       89.8      1724       43.1
    10       80     4306       53.8      3066       38.3
    14      112     1436       12.8      4697       41.9
    16      128     1514       11.8      2773       21.7
```

**The outermost ring is the quietest part of the window** — 11.8 mismatches
per chunk against 112.9 in ring 3 — and the centre chunk has none at all. A
boundary artefact has the opposite profile.

Widening the compared box confirms it from the other side. Both references
hold **1,370** full chunks spanning chunks (4,3)..(82,82), so the halo two
rings beyond the forceloaded square is fully `FULL` in both. Comparing over
`--bbox 46 46 82 82` gives 1,369 chunks and **40,396** mismatches: the 280
halo chunks add 4,865, or **17.4 per chunk against the 32.6 per chunk inside**.
Extending the window past its own edge makes the floor *sparser*.

### Refuted: capture timing and region flush

`ci/gen-vanilla-region.sh` runs `tick freeze` before the first chunk loads, so
the game loop never turns; `docs/ORACLE.md` has the evidence chain, including
the ticks that were still queued at `t=0` in a finished reference. Both
references reached the same 1,370 full chunks over the same bounding box, so
neither was harvested early. And the two runs disagree about **no biome cell
at all** — `biomes=1672704/0` — which a truncated or half-flushed capture
could not produce.

### Refuted: structures

Not one of the 136 distinct block-pair families in the floor is a structure
palette. Forty-five distinct blocks appear in it and there is no plank, no
brick of any kind, nothing `chiseled`, no `hay_block`, no `vault`, no
`trial_spawner`. The only wood is `acacia_log` and `acacia_leaves`, which is a
savanna tree — a feature, not a structure — and the two `village_savanna`
starts this window is famous for do not contribute a single block. Structure
starts are chosen at `STRUCTURE_STARTS`, the second step of the pyramid, from
seed and chunk position alone, so there is nothing in a structure's placement
for a thread order to move — and the floor says so.

### Confirmed, and it is the whole of it: vanilla's decoration order

93.6% of the floor is twenty-five block-pair families and every one of them is
a decoration that reads the world before it writes:

```text
    4152  cave_air <-> sulfur          1039  coal_ore  <-> stone
    3918  cinnabar <-> sulfur          1015  kelp_plant<-> water
    3871  andesite <-> diorite          976  tall_seagrass <-> water
    3633  diorite  <-> granite          953  cave_air  <-> cinnabar
    2918  sulfur   <-> water            910  seagrass  <-> water
    2632  andesite <-> granite          724  deepslate <-> sulfur
    1498  air      <-> cave_air         582  cinnabar  <-> water
    1273  air      <-> sulfur_spike     467  cave_air  <-> deepslate
```

Three mechanisms, all of them read-then-write:

* **the stone variants.** `granite`, `diorite` and `andesite` are all in
  `stone_ore_replaceables`, so an ore blob from one chunk will happily
  overwrite a blob another chunk already placed. Which one survives is which
  one ran last. **10,136** blocks of the floor are these three swapping among
  themselves, in both directions.
* **the sulfur cave.** `sulfur_pool` is a `minecraft:lake` whose
  `can_place_feature` predicate *reads* the blocks already there
  (`not matching_blocks minecraft:sulfur_spike`), and `sulfur_spike_cluster`
  is a `speleothem_cluster` with `floor_to_ceiling_search_range: 12`. Both
  decide where to put a large blob by looking at a world that may or may not
  have been decorated yet. This family is the single largest share:
  **15,766** of the 35,531 have `sulfur`, `sulfur_spike` or `cinnabar` on one
  side or the other.
* **water-column vegetation**, **3,475** blocks. `kelp`, `kelp_plant`,
  `seagrass` and `tall_seagrass` against `water`, which is the shuffling
  family `tools/region-differ`'s own header warns a twin mask cannot fully
  catch.

And the distribution inside a chunk says this is *whole features moving*, not
merely two chunks contesting the blocks between them. By distance from the
chunk border, against what a uniform scatter would give:

```text
  d       cells  uniform     floor  observed  excess
  0 (edge)   60   23.44%      9340    26.29%   1.12x
  4          28   10.94%      3600    10.13%   0.93x
  7 (core)    4    1.56%       395     1.11%   0.71x
```

If the floor were only blocks two chunks both wrote, it would pile up at the
border; 1.12x is nearly flat. The order dependence is in *where a feature
decides to go*, and it lands anywhere in the chunk.

### So: is it reducible?

**Not by us, and not by anything short of changing the jar.** Nothing in the
harness is charged: the edges are complete by construction, the boundary is
the quietest part of the window, the capture is frozen and flushed, and the
families are vanilla's own decorations. §1's gradient already showed the
disagreement falling by 91.2% as concurrency is removed and stopping there,
and §2 records that no configuration reaches zero.

**And the one lever we control was tried on the overworld rather than argued
about, because §1 and §2 were measured on the nether.** Four fresh unmodified
references at one extent — seed 20260722, centre (59,66), r=5, 121 chunks,
chosen because that square holds ten of the r=16 window's worst floor chunks.
Two under `GEN_ORDER=rows`, the order every published reference was taken in,
and two under `GEN_ORDER=perchunk`, which forceloads one chunk at a time in a
fixed row-major order and does not send the next until the previous has
reached FULL on disk:

```text
  floor, rows a vs b            2,535
  floor, perchunk a vs b        2,728       +7.6%, i.e. no better
  control: rows-a vs pc-a       3,345       the knob did turn
```

**The strongest ordering control that is still pure configuration does not
reduce the overworld floor.** It is 7.6% worse, which is the same shape as
§1's last row being worse than the row above it: both are sampling one
residual rather than tracking a trend. The control matters as much as the
result — 3,345 blocks between the two orders is a real difference between two
worlds, so this is not a knob that failed to turn. §2's structural reason
holds here exactly: forceloading one chunk still decorates a small *batch*,
because its neighbours must reach FEATURES for it to reach FULL, and the order
inside that batch belongs to the scheduler.

**One observation from the same run that is worth more than the result, and it
is not clean.** Over those same 121 chunks the r=16 reference pair disagrees by
**12,185** blocks — 4.8x the 2,535 measured here at r=5. Extent and machine
load both differ between the two (`docs/DETERMINISM.md`'s header records the
r=16 references were harvested at load 2–17 with other agents building; this
run had an idle box), and this experiment does not separate them. What it does
establish is the thing that matters for every figure in this file: **a floor is
a property of the pair of runs that produced it, not of the seed and the
window.** It cannot be carried from one reference pair to another, and a
window's resolving power cannot be predicted from a floor someone else
measured. That is a second, independent reason to prefer an instrument that
does not need a floor at all — §12.

---

## 12. The instrument that restores resolving power: the admissible set

The floor is irreducible, so the instrument had to change. `ci/figures.toml`
registers the result and `ci/admissible-residual.py` computes it; this is the
argument for it.

### What was actually wrong with the twin mask

The mask is not wrong, it is *incomplete*. It removes every position the two
references spell differently and says nothing about them — so a genuine defect
of ours sitting on one of those positions is invisible, and rule 6's
disqualifier fires when that invisible region grows larger than what is
visible. On this window it did: a blind spot of 29,560 against a residual of
29,490. (On the destroyed reference pair, where this instrument was first
built, the same two numbers were 35,531 and 29,927 — the same verdict on
different digits, which is why regenerating the references changed what may be
published without changing what the instrument is for.)

But the mask throws away information it is holding. At a masked position the
two references give **two different answers**, and we wrote something. Whether
what we wrote is one of vanilla's two answers is a fact, it is measurable, and
the mask discards it.

### The change

For each position `p`, let `V(p) = {A(p), B(p)}` — the set of blocks an
unmodified vanilla run of this seed and window is known to produce there. It
has one element everywhere the two references agree and two where the mask
used to be. **We are charged at `p` if and only if `ours(p) ∉ V(p)`.**

Nothing is excluded, so the denominator is the whole window, there is no mask,
there is no floor, and rule 6's disqualifier has no premise to stand on. It is
not overridden — the thing it protects against is absent.

Three properties, and they are the whole case for the method:

1. **It can only over-charge us.** Two references under-sample vanilla's
   answers; a third run might well produce what we produced at a position the
   first two do not. So the count is an **upper bound on our defects** and the
   percentage a **lower bound on our exactness** — the direction a published
   figure has to err in.
2. **It improves with N where the mask degrades with N.** Adding a reference
   can only add answers to `V(p)`, so the charge is monotonically
   non-increasing. §4 measured the opposite for the mask: the floor is a union
   and grows, 35,531 → 73,820 from N=2 to N=6 (destroyed ensemble — §4), so
   floor/residual gets *worse*
   by sampling vanilla more. Under this instrument resolving power never
   degrades.
3. **It is the End's argument, generalised.** §6 closed the End not with a
   percentage but with *"there is no position on which vanilla is consistent
   and we are wrong"*, plus *"where vanilla is inconsistent we land on an
   answer vanilla gives"*. Those two sentences are exactly `ours(p) ∈ V(p)`
   for every `p`. **And the End is this instrument's own worked example of
   property 2**: against the `a`/`b` pair the charge is 125, because that
   particular pair happens to agree everywhere and the charge therefore equals
   the masked residual; against all six references it is **0**, because our
   dump is byte-identical to `det-ens-end-d`. Same dump, same window, more
   references, charge falls. That is the monotonicity, measured.

It needs no new generation: `|ours ∉ {A,B}|` is the positional intersection of
two unmasked diffs, `A vs ours` and `B vs ours`.

### The measurement

Seed 20260722, centre chunk (64,64), r=16, 1,089 chunks, **vanilla first**,
against the **regenerated** pair of unmodified references, our dump at `main`
8e283f57. Re-taken end to end on 2026-08-09: the references this instrument
was first demonstrated on did not survive the 2026-08-07 disk failure, and
because a floor belongs to the pair of runs that produced it, nothing from the
original demonstration could be carried across.

```text
  ours != reference A, unmasked        42,400
  ours != reference B, unmasked        46,255
  ours matches NEITHER                 29,605   of 107,053,056
```

**99.972345%**, no mask, and it is 115 blocks worse than the unquotable
twin-masked figure beside it. That is the point: a re-instrumentation that
made the number better would be a re-instrumentation nobody should believe.

**On the destroyed pair the same three lines read 50,149 / 45,588 / 30,279 —
99.971716%, 352 blocks worse than its masked companion.** The two sets are not
comparable and the second must never be quoted as a "before" for the first:
the references differ, and so does the tree. What *is* worth carrying across
is that the instrument behaved the same way on both pairs — it charged us more
than the mask did, by the positions inside the mask where we are wrong.

### The number that settles rule 6's worry

The blind spot is **29,560 positions wide. It is 115 blocks deep.**

```text
  positions the twin mask hides           29,560
  of those, we match reference A          16,650
  of those, we match reference B not A    12,795
  of those, we match NEITHER                 115   (0.39%)
```

At 99.61% of the positions vanilla could not reproduce, we produced something
vanilla itself produced. Rule 6 was right to refuse a figure that could not
see into the mask; what it could not know is that the thing inside was a
hundred-odd blocks, not thirty thousand. And the 115 are not diffuse: **73 of
them, 63.5%, involve `sulfur`, `sulfur_spike` or `cinnabar`**, the largest
single one being 29 positions where vanilla writes `cave_air` and we write
`sulfur`. What is left of the blind spot is the sulfur cave — §11's largest
floor family and the one place in this window vanilla is least sure of itself.

**And the arithmetic that makes this the same finding as the masked one:**
29,560 − 115 = 29,445 positions inside the mask where we give one of vanilla's
two answers, and 115 + 29,490 = 29,605, the masked residual plus the depth of
the blind spot being exactly the admissible charge. On the destroyed pair the
same identity ran 35,531 − 352 = 35,179 and 352 + 29,927 = 30,279.

### What the floor now says about this window

**29,605 against a floor of 29,560 is 45 blocks of headroom.** The admissible
instrument is still valid — it excludes nothing, so rule 6's premise is still
absent and the override in `ci/figures.toml` still holds — but validity is not
resolving power. Our disagreement with vanilla over this window is now within
0.15% of vanilla's disagreement with itself, so this window can no longer tell
a real improvement of a few hundred blocks from a different pair of vanilla
runs. **Do not use it to adjudicate the next worldgen change.** The honest next
instrument is a wider window, more references, or both; §4's ladder shows why
more references sharpen the admissible charge even as they inflate the mask's
floor.

> **THIS PARAGRAPH IS WRONG AND §14 REPLACES IT — 2026-08-09.** 29,605 and
> 29,560 are not measured the same way: the first is a charge under a rule that
> excludes nothing, the second is the width of a mask that rule does not use,
> so the subtraction has no meaning and "45 blocks of headroom" is not a
> quantity. Six more references were generated (70 s each) and the admissible
> instrument's **own** floor — an unmodified vanilla run charged against the
> others — was measured for the first time. The like-for-like comparison is
> **20,244 against 16,932**, and the verdict splits in two: this window cannot
> support a **level** claim below N=5 (at N=2 the innocent floor alone spans
> ~13,900 blocks, far worse than this paragraph feared), but a **delta**
> against a fixed reference set is exact at any N, down to one block, which is
> better than it feared. The last sentence above is the one that survives, and
> §14 puts a number on "more references". **Read §14 before quoting anything in
> §12.**

### What this does not do

* It does not make the window's *deltas* more or less sound; they were always
  sound and they still are.
* It does not prove we would match a third reference at the 29,445 positions
  it clears. It proves we produced something a real unmodified vanilla run of
  this seed produced there, which is a weaker and sufficient claim.
* It says nothing about block entities, entities, scheduled ticks or anything
  after generation — §6 of `docs/PARITY.md` still lists what no block figure
  covers.
* It does not retire the twin mask. For a **delta** between two commits the
  mask is still the right tool, because it holds the denominator fixed.

## 13. A determinism hazard that was not one: the biome palette's build order

This section exists because the project spent three days carrying a warning
that was wrong, and the way it was wrong is more useful than the fact.

### The claim

`surface::BiomeId` is a `u16` interned from the datapack's biome directory in
`BTreeMap` order. On 2026-08-06 a falsification sweep rebuilt that palette in
reverse — the same biomes, interning still injective, the climate table built
in the same loop so it stayed in step — as a **control that was expected to be
undetectable**. It changed **4 of 4 region files**. `docs/BENCH.md` recorded
it, three doc comments were corrected to say the order was load-bearing, and
the consumer responsible was never identified.

That is a latent determinism hazard of the worst shape: any future change to
palette construction — a different iteration, a parallel build, one added
biome — would silently move worldgen output, and nobody could say where.

### It does not reproduce

Re-measured 2026-08-09 on the same window the claim was made on. Seed
20260722, r=16, centre (64,64), `RUSTMC_WORKERS=1`, Threadripper:

```text
  forward vs forward     0 of 4    determinism control, same binary
  reverse                0 of 4    id values AND id order both moved
  shift 1                0 of 4    id values moved, order preserved
  shift 7                0 of 4
  poison (every id 0)    4 of 4    positive control -- must not be 0
```

Also `0 of 4` at r=7 on that centre, and `0 of 1` at r=7 centred on
**(-6000,-6000)** with poison at 1 of 1. That second centre is not decoration:
`docs/BENCH.md` establishes that (64,64) contains no frozen or cold ocean and
therefore cannot reach `cold_enough_to_snow` or the iceberg melt test at all,
so a window there is blind to the entire climate path. "A gate a window cannot
exercise proves nothing" — so the question was asked again where it can be.

### Three things the new instrument does that the old one did not

1. **One binary, both arms.** `RUSTMC_BIOME_PALETTE_ORDER` is read at run
   time, so the control and the injection are the same executable. The 2026-08-06
   method built a separate binary per injection and compared it against a
   pristine control — which establishes determinism across three runs of *one*
   binary and then compares *differently built* ones. Those are not the same
   claim, and nothing in that sweep could separate "the injection moved output"
   from "the build moved output".
2. **`reverse` and `shift` are a discriminator, not two spellings of one
   test.** `reverse` moves an id's numeric value and its position in the order
   together, so on its own it cannot name a consumer even when it fires.
   `shift N` interns N unreachable names ahead of the directory: biome *i* gets
   *i+N* and every `<` between two real biomes still answers what it did. Value
   moved, order preserved. `shift` red means a consumer reads the **value**;
   `shift` clean with `reverse` red means it reads the **order**.
3. **A positive control that must fire.** `poison` answers every biome query
   with id 0. Without it, a knob that silently failed to reach the generator
   would report every injection as undetectable and close the question with a
   measurement of nothing — rule 7, which this project has now paid out on
   fourteen times. Every run also prints an FNV digest of the whole name-to-id
   map, so a dead knob is caught a second, independent way even on a window too
   small to resolve a block.

### Why there was nothing to find, from vanilla's side

Vanilla has **no numeric biome identifier anywhere on the generation path**,
so there is nothing for an assignment order to be load-bearing *for*. From the
pinned jar:

* `SurfaceRules$BiomeConditionSource$1BiomeCondition.compute` is one call —
  `HolderSet.contains(Holder)`. For a direct set that is `Set.contains` over
  holder objects; for a named one it is `Holder.is(TagKey)`. Membership, not
  identity arithmetic.
* `BiomeManager.getBiome` and `getNoiseBiomeAtQuart` return `Holder<Biome>`
  throughout, and the fuzz tie-break `getFiddledDistance(J,I,I,D,D,D)` is
  seeded from **position** only — no biome takes part in it.

Ours is the exact analogue: `Condition::BiomeIds` is a `Vec::contains`, an
equality. The integer is an implementation device with no vanilla counterpart,
and our behaviour matches vanilla's precisely because neither side can ask
anything of a biome except "is it this one". **So the honest close is not "we
found the consumer and fixed it" — it is that the premise was false and the
architecture never permitted such a consumer.**

### What was killed as a null, so nobody re-runs it

* **The climate table going out of step with the palette.** The obvious
  explanation, and dead: the commit that built palette and climate in one
  lockstep loop landed 19:19 on 2026-08-06 and the sweep ran at 22:33. The
  in-loop assertion was already there.
* **"A rebuild moves output."** Two `cargo build --release` runs of one source
  into different target directories produce a **bit-identical** binary here, so
  a same-source rebuild arm is *vacuous* rather than passing — it dumps the
  same file twice. Forced apart with `RUSTFLAGS='-C codegen-units=1'`, a
  genuinely different binary from identical source leaves the r=16 dump
  unchanged.
* **The surface rules.** Every rule use of an id is an equality compare, so
  relabelling cannot change a verdict. Checked when the interning landed and
  not re-derived here; the T0 test now pins it.

### What guards it now

Nobody should have to rediscover this, so the finding is mechanised twice at
two very different costs:

* `ci/biome-palette-order.sh` — the dump-level gate. Five arms plus an optional
  rebuild arm, about twenty minutes at r=16. Exits 3 rather than 0 if the
  determinism control, the positive control or the digest check fails, so a
  broken instrument cannot report a green.
* `eval::relabelling_is_undetectable` — three unit tests, microseconds, run by
  every `ci/t0.sh`. Deliberately a `#[cfg(test)]` module **in the lib** and not
  a file in `tests/`, because T0 runs `--lib --bins` and an integration test
  would never run in any gate. One of the three asserts the verdicts are not
  degenerate, since 24 identical answers would compare equal under any
  relabelling whatever.

Either goes red if a `<`, a `min`/`max`, a `binary_search`, a hash of an id, or
an id indexing a table built outside the palette's own loop ever appears.
---

## 14. The admissible instrument's own floor — measured 2026-08-09, and §12's last paragraph was wrong

§12 ends by saying this window is spent: *"29,605 against a floor of 29,560 is
45 blocks of headroom … Do not use it to adjudicate the next worldgen change."*
After the beard fix the same instrument reads **20,244**, further below the
same 29,560, and the natural reading is that the window is now finished
outright. **That reading is wrong, and so was the paragraph it comes from.**
Both compare two numbers that are not measured the same way.

### What was actually being compared

* **29,560** is `|A ≠ B|`: the width of the **twin mask** — the region the
  masked instrument *excludes*.
* **20,244** is `|ours ∉ {A,B}|`: a **charge** under a rule that excludes
  nothing.

The second instrument does not use the first one's mask, so the mask's width
is not its floor. §12 knew nothing better was available — there were two
references of this window and a floor needs a third — and said "there is no
floor" on the strength of "there is no mask". **No mask means no blind spot.
It does not mean no noise.** Two references under-sample the set of blocks
vanilla can put at a position, so part of the charge falls on *any* run that
is neither A nor B, and an unmodified vanilla run is such a run.

### The measurement: substitute an innocent candidate

Six further unmodified references of this exact window were generated —
`oracle-work/gen-wide-20260722-r16-ow-{c,d,e,f,g,h}`, new directories, same
seed 20260722, centre (64,64), r=16, `GEN_ORDER=rows`, the same invocation the
`-a`/`-b` pair came from — bringing the ensemble to **eight**. About 70 s each;
this was never expensive, only unattempted. Then run the admissible rule with
a **known-innocent** candidate in our place:

```text
  |ours ∉ {a,b}|   20,244     the brief's figure, reproduced exactly
  |c    ∉ {a,b}|   16,932     c is unmodified vanilla. THIS is the floor.
  |a ≠ b|          29,560     the twin floor, reproduced exactly
```

**No percentage is printed beside those counts, deliberately.** A percentage is
the form a level claim gets published in, and the finding below is that this
window cannot support a level claim at N=2 — so quoting one here would refute
the section it appears in. `ci/check-figures.py` enforces the same thing from
the other direction and caught this section doing it on the first draft: an
exactness percentage needs a ledger entry, a window and an artefact, and the
right time to mint one for this window is when the headline is re-taken at
N >= 5.

The arithmetic closes exactly both ways, which is what says the measurement is
sound rather than merely new:

```text
  29,560 = 13,739 (c matches b not a) + 15,589 (c matches a not b) + 232
  29,560 = 12,864 (we match b not a)  + 16,595 (we match a not b)  + 101
```

So the like-for-like comparison is **20,244 against 16,932**, not against
29,560. The headroom is 3,312 blocks, not 45 — and not negative.

### The number nobody knew: what this window can resolve

`ci/window-resolving-power.py` runs every candidate against every subset.
`ours` is charged against N-subsets of the eight; the floor is every one of the
eight charged against N-subsets of the other seven.

```text
  N   ours                                floor (innocent vanilla)              sep
  1   33,108..37,828  (med 36,857, n=8)   26,547..38,778  (med 31,949, n=56)  +4,908
  2   18,073..23,313  (med 20,278, n=28)  10,136..23,999  (med 16,063, n=168) +4,215
  3   15,491..18,273  (med 16,442, n=56)   6,223..17,591  (med 10,919, n=280) +5,523
  4   14,780..16,724  (med 15,448, n=70)   4,048..13,917  (med  8,375, n=280) +7,073
  5   14,518..15,862  (med 14,958, n=56)   3,214..11,679  (med  6,713, n=168) +8,245
  6   14,286..15,057  (med 14,695, n=28)   2,635.. 9,135  (med  5,516, n=56)  +9,179
  7   14,272..14,709  (med 14,414, n=8)    2,471.. 6,626  (med  4,511, n=8)   +9,903
```

Read the two columns against each other rather than either alone. **Our charge
asymptotes and the floor does not.** Ours falls 36,857 → 14,414 and is flat
from N=5; the floor falls 31,949 → 4,511 and is still falling. That is the
signature of a residual that is mostly real: more sampling of vanilla dissolves
the floor and leaves ours where it was. If our 20,244 had been noise, the two
columns would have converged.

**And the twin floor was never a single number.** Over the 28 pairs available
now it runs **26,547..38,778, median 31,949**. The published 29,560 is one draw
from that. `docs/AGENT-BRIEF.md` already says a floor belongs to the pair that
produced it; this is how wide "belongs" is.

### So: two different resolutions, and only one of them is bad

The brief that commissioned this asked for "the N below which this window
resolves nothing". There is no single N, because *level* and *delta* are
limited by different things, and conflating them is what produced the wrong
verdict in the first place.

* **A LEVEL — "our residual on this window is X" — needs N ≥ 5.** At N=2 the
  innocent floor alone spans 10,136..23,999. Our 20,244 sits *inside* that
  band, so at N=2 no residual may be quoted: pick a luckier reference pair and
  an innocent vanilla run outscores us. **This is the sense in which §12's
  verdict was right, and it is much worse than §12 thought — the unusable
  span is ~13,900 blocks, not 45.**
* **A DELTA — "this change improved us by k" — is exact at any N, including
  N=2, and k can be 1.** The references are fixed files and our generator is
  deterministic, so `|ours ∉ V|` is a deterministic function of our output:
  before and after against the *same* reference set, the difference has no
  noise in it at all. The −9,331 the beard fix is quoted for is sound, and so
  is any future delta taken the same way.

**What a delta cannot do by itself is generalise**, and that has a positional
test rather than a threshold. Let `U` be the union of every pairwise
disagreement among the references — the region where vanilla is not unanimous.
Over eight references `U` is **82,599 positions, 0.077% of the window**.
Outside `U` all eight runs spell the position the same way, so a block we move
there is moved against vanilla itself; inside `U` it may only be moved between
two answers vanilla already gives. So:

> **Intersect your changed positions with `U`. The part outside is your
> result; the part inside is a coin-flip you happened to win.** One command,
> and it replaces the unanswerable question "is my delta bigger than the
> floor".

For our current tree that intersection is already decisive: of the **14,270**
positions charged at N=8, **13,837 lie outside `U`** — positions where eight
independent unmodified vanilla runs agree and we differ — against **433**
inside it. The blind spot §12 measured at "29,560 wide, 115 deep" is, with
eight references, 82,599 wide and 433 deep.

### The finding that makes the window usable again

The window's aggregate floor is large because a few block families are large
and noisy, not because the window is noisy everywhere. Ranked by our N=8
charge, with each pair's floor being the **worst** any innocent run is charged
for that same pair:

```text
  vanilla -> ours                          charged   floor  verdict
  diorite -> andesite                        1,642     764  CLEAR
  diorite -> granite                         1,116     396  CLEAR
  andesite -> granite                          957     344  CLEAR
  granite -> andesite                          940     562  marginal
  andesite -> diorite                          912     542  marginal
  granite -> diorite                           830     574  marginal
  grass_block[snowy=false] -> dirt             646       1  CLEAR
  kelp_plant -> water[level=0]                 481     299  marginal
  water[level=0] -> kelp_plant                 433     370  marginal
  sulfur -> cave_air                           420     380  marginal
  cave_air -> sulfur                           408     362  marginal
  sulfur -> water[level=0]                     383     385  FLOOR
  seagrass -> water[level=0]                   294     130  CLEAR
  water[level=0] -> seagrass                   280     159  marginal
  cinnabar -> sulfur                           265     518  FLOOR
  water[level=0] -> sulfur                     262     124  CLEAR
  water[level=0] -> tall_seagrass[upper]       217     108  CLEAR
  tall_seagrass[upper] -> water[level=0]       188     110  marginal
  sulfur -> cinnabar                           158     437  FLOOR
  diorite -> tuff                              153      16  CLEAR
```
