quoted as a scalar range, `2,577..4,835 at N=5`, for as long as it had
existed. A range means the six innocent readings disagree with each other by
~2,258 blocks, and **nobody had ever asked where**. ORACLE rule 9 forbids
opening an investigation on a residual before it has been censused positionally
and by block; a floor is a measurement in exactly the same sense, and it had
never been given the same treatment.

`ci/floor-census.py` gives it that treatment. Same window, same ensemble, same
matched dump order as §17 and §17.1: seed 12345, r=16, centre chunk (64,64),
1,089 chunks, 107,053,056 blocks, references `gen-wide-12345-r16-ow-{c..h}`,
`gen_order=rows` / `dump_order=zx` read off each reference's own
`HARVEST-ORDER` stamp, vanilla first, Threadripper. It reproduces
`2,577..4,835` exactly from the existing 72-diff matrix before it says
anything else, and it costs **nine seconds and no vanilla run**.

### 18.1 The floor is per-run noise, not a fixed set — and that decided the item

This is the finding everything else follows from.

```text
  union of the six innocent charges     21,650 positions
  charged to more than one candidate         2 positions  (0.01%)
```

Six unmodified vanilla runs, each adjudicated against the other five, are each
charged on their **own private set of positions**. There is no shared hard
core: no named list of places where vanilla is reliably unsure of itself.

That reading kills one of the three candidate moves outright and licenses
another, which is why the census came before the box was spent:

* **"Find and pin whatever is nondeterministic in vanilla" has no target.** It
  was the outcome worth the most — if the 2,258 were one or two named sources,
  the floor would be mostly removable rather than irreducible. It is not one or
  two sources with fixed positions. It is a diffuse per-run resampling.
* **"More independent vanilla runs" has a mechanism.** Noise that is
  independent per run is precisely what a larger ensemble thins, because the
  admissible set `V(p)` can only gain answers as references are added. The
  charge is monotonically non-increasing in N by construction; what the census
  adds is the reason to expect the *rate* to be worth paying for.

### 18.2 It is three blocks, and they are not where our residual is

```text
  vanilla block     floor   median   runs charged   share of floor
  diorite           1,398    1,219       6 of 6         19.6%
  andesite          1,377      877       6 of 6         19.3%
  granite           1,187      623       6 of 6         16.6%
  air                 648        6       5 of 6          9.1%
  snow[layers=1]      240        3       4 of 6          3.4%
```

`diorite`, `andesite` and `granite` are **55.5% of the floor between them**,
and the six largest ordered pairs are the six permutations of those three
substituting for each other. That is the overlapping stone-variant blob
mechanism §11 named out of the `ChunkPyramid` bytecode — blobs that read the
world before they write it — now measured from the ensemble rather than read
off the code. §11 said not to re-open it and this does not: it quantifies it.

**Only 64 distinct vanilla blocks carry any floor at all.** Every other block
in the window's vocabulary was never charged to a single one of the six
innocent runs. That complement is the usable half, and it is emitted as
`ci/floor-tables/ow-r16-12345-rows-n5.toml` so a later item can look its block
up rather than clear the aggregate.

And the floor is shallow and clustered: 484 of 1,089 chunks hold any of it,
half of it lives in 58 chunks, and 15,893 of the 21,650 positions are in
`y 0..47`.

### 18.3 The key of the table is the vanilla block ALONE, and that is not a detail

The obvious key is the ordered pair `(vanilla, ours)`, which is what
`ci/window-resolving-power.py --kinds` uses — correctly, for ranking *our*
charge. As a **lookup** key it is wrong, and wrong in the unsafe direction.
Our port writes `blue_ice -> packed_ice`; an innocent vanilla run at those
same positions writes `blue_ice -> water`. Keyed on the ordered pair,
`blue_ice -> packed_ice` has a floor of 0 and our charge is adjudicated
against nothing, while vanilla is demonstrably unsure about `blue_ice` there.

**And one table is not enough, which the first version of this section got
wrong.** Keying on the vanilla spelling floors a **miss** — vanilla puts `B`
here and we do not. A **surplus** is the opposite claim, "we put `B` where
vanilla does not", and its floor is keyed on what the innocent candidate
*placed*. Looking a surplus up in the miss table is a category error that
returns a number rather than an error, so `ci/floor-census.py` emits both and
says which is which. On `blue_ice`, the two directions read **151** (miss) and
**175** (surplus) at N=5, and **100** and **0** at N=15 — near enough to be
mistaken for each other and different enough to change a verdict.

So the emitted table marginalises over what the candidate wrote. The cost of
that choice is paid in the right direction and is worth stating as a number:
**summing the per-block floors gives 7,132, which is half again the 4,835 any
single innocent run is actually charged.** The table is *more* conservative in
aggregate than the scalar floor and only sharper per block. It cannot be used
to manufacture a smaller total, and any future reader tempted to add it up
should get a bigger number, not a smaller one.

### 18.4 Widening the ensemble ACROSS harvest orders makes the floor worse

The brief for this item asked for both effects of a larger N to be measured
rather than assumed, and this is the one that does not go the expected way.
Pooling the six `rows` references with the two `rows_rev` ones gives a pool of
8 and a leave-one-out N of 7 — more references, higher N, and:

```text
  pool                       N     floor min    median    max
  6 rows                     5       2,577      3,387    4,835
  6 rows + 2 rows_rev        7       1,476      2,903    6,128
```

The median falls and **the maximum rises by 1,293 blocks**. The six `rows`
candidates are each charged less, as expected; the two `rows_rev` candidates
are charged 4,421 and 6,128, because a minority-order candidate is adjudicated
mostly against the majority order and is charged for its harvest order rather
than for being a different run. A floor is quoted as its worst innocent
reading, so this pool is a worse instrument despite being larger.

Registered as `ow-r16-12345-floor-mixed-order`, **1,476..6,128** at N=7,
`quotable = false`, disqualifier **mixed harvest order** — it is a real
measurement of a worse instrument, kept so that the move it rules out stays
ruled out, and it must never be quoted as this window's floor.

**A floor belongs to the ensemble AND the harvest order that produced it, and
widening across orders is not widening.** This is the same quantity §15 and
ORACLE rule 29 measure from the other side; it is recorded here because the
tempting move when a margin is thin is to raise N with whatever references
exist, and on this window two of the ten available ones would have made it
worse.

### 18.5 The headline, adjudicated per block instead of in aggregate

The registered 7,940 split by the vanilla spelling and put beside each block's
own floor, same ensemble, same N:

```text
  vanilla block                        charged   floor   ratio   verdict
  air                                    1,561     648    2.4x   CLEAR
  packed_ice                               864      59   14.6x   CLEAR
  water[level=0]                           617     203    3.0x   CLEAR
  spruce_leaves[distance=2,...]            513      81    6.3x   CLEAR
  sulfur                                   483     115    4.2x   CLEAR
  cave_air                                 463     128    3.6x   CLEAR
  snow[layers=1]                           460     240    1.9x   marginal
  deepslate[axis=y]                        453      31   14.6x   CLEAR
  ...
  diorite                                  127   1,398    0.1x   AT/UNDER FLOOR
  andesite                                 112   1,377    0.1x   AT/UNDER FLOOR
  ---------------------------------------------------------------------
  CLEAR — over twice its own block floor              6,854
  marginal — above it but not twice                     509
  at or under its own block floor                       577
```

**6,854 of the 7,940 — 86.3% — clears its own block's floor by more than
twice**, and only 577 sits at or under it. Nothing is subtracted anywhere:
the three totals partition the charge, they do not reduce it.

The aggregate ratio of 1.6x and the per-block picture disagree because **the
floor and the residual are in different places**. 55.5% of the floor is stone
variants; only 359 of our 7,940 is. Thirty-six block kinds in our residual,
171 blocks in total, have a measured floor of exactly zero. The window is
spent as an *aggregate* adjudicator long before it is spent as a *named* one,
and the aggregate number was hiding that.

### 18.6 A charge belongs to its subset exactly as a floor belongs to its pair

Found while reproducing the registered figure, and it is a small correction to
how §17.1 states it. The registered 7,940 is the charge over references
`c,d,e,f,g` — one particular 5-of-6 subset. The other five subsets read:

```text
  cdefg 7,940   cdefh 7,944   cdegh 7,881
  cdfgh 7,886   cefgh 8,418   defgh 7,871
```

**7,871..8,418, median 7,913, n=6.** The floor beside it has been registered
as a range since it was first taken; the charge was registered as a scalar,
which quietly asserts that the choice of which N of the pool to adjudicate
against does not matter. It moves the headline by up to 547 blocks — about
11% of the floor's own maximum — and it is now printed by
`ci/floor-census.py --adjudicate` beside every charge it reports. The
registered 7,940 is not wrong and has not been changed; it is one reading of
six and it now says so.

Registered as `ow-r16-12345-charge-subset-spread`. Note its baseline: this
spread is measured against our own post-iceberg matched-order dump, not against unmodified vanilla —
it is the wobble in OUR charge under the choice of reference subset, and it is
not a floor, not an innocent reading and not comparable to one.

### 18.7 The sub-window is a mirage, the per-block table is not — and one held-out test separates them

§18.2 says the floor is clustered: 484 of 1,089 chunks hold any of it. The
obvious next move is a sub-window that avoids them, and the arithmetic looks
extraordinary. Cutting the 100 worst chunks — 9.2% of the window — takes the
floor from 4,835 to 1,461 while losing only 14.1% of our residual, and the 605
chunks in which no innocent run was charged at all hold **4,315 of our 7,940
against a floor of exactly zero**.

**That zero is an artefact and the sub-window must not be built.** It is
selected using the very six readings it is then validated against, which is
rule 7 wearing a new hat: a chunk with a real but small floor reads zero at
n=6 by luck, and there are 1,089 chunks for it to happen in.

The test that separates a real zero from an unsampled one is to estimate the
floor on half the pool and measure it on the other half. Over all twenty
3-and-3 splits:

```text
  PER-BLOCK arm — the table this section emits
    charge called CLEAR on the selecting 3     6,854..7,402  (median 7,128)
    still CLEAR against the held-out 3         6,854..6,854
    survival                                   92.6%..100.0%

  PER-CHUNK arm — the sub-window the same census suggests
    chunks reading zero-floor on the 3            805..842
    HELD-OUT charge INSIDE those chunks         2,893..4,694  (median 4,166)
    our charge inside them                      4,908..6,652
```

Registered as `ow-r16-12345-floor-subwindow`, `quotable = false`, disqualifier
**selected on the same runs that validate it**. A fresh innocent vanilla run is
charged **2,893..4,694 blocks inside the region
that had no floor at all** — essentially the whole of the window's floor
reappears the moment it is measured by references that did not choose the
region. The sub-window buys nothing and would have manufactured a 4,315-block
claim against a floor that is not zero.

The per-block table is the opposite result on the identical test. Every split's
CLEAR total converges on the same **6,854**, and no split loses more than 7.4%
of what it selected. The asymmetry has a reason worth keeping: a per-chunk
floor is estimated from a handful of positions in each of 1,089 buckets, while
a per-block floor is estimated from thousands of positions in each of 64, and
the three blocks that carry over half of it are charged in **6 runs out of 6**.
Estimator variance, not geometry, is what makes one usable.

**So the answer to "a sub-window or a per-block floor" is: per-block, and the
sub-window is registered as a null so nobody re-derives it.** The held-out
check is `ci/floor-census.py --holdout` and it runs in
`ci/floor-census-12345.sh`, because a validation that is performed once by hand
and then described in prose is not a validation.

### 18.8 The floor re-taken on sixteen references — 4,835 -> 2,241, and 3,387 -> 590

The census in §18.1 said the floor is per-run noise rather than a fixed set,
and per-run noise is the one thing a larger ensemble removes. That was the
argument for spending the box, and it was made before the box was spent. Ten
new `rows` references — `gen-wide-12345-r16-ow-{i..r}`, ports 25951..25960,
`ci/refs-12345-widen.sh` — take the pool from 6 to 16 and the leave-one-out N
from 5 to 15. Each took **56..63 s**; the corpus lock was held as role `run`
for the ten vanilla boots and handed back before the diff matrix, which is 210
new `region-differ` calls at **2.6 s** each and needs no lock.

```text
  pool   harvest   N     floor min   median      max   p_hide   union   >1x
     6   rows      5         2,577    3,387    4,835    45.1%  21,650     2
    16   rows     15           141      590    2,241    18.1%  11,465     0
```

**The max fell 53.6% and the median fell 82.6%**, `p_hide` went from 45.1% to
18.1%, and the number of blocks carrying any floor at all went from 64 to 52.
Not one of the 11,465 positions in the union is charged to two candidates:
sixteen unmodified vanilla runs are each wrong in their own private places, and
§18.1's reading holds harder at sixteen than it did at six.

#### The prediction, and the one part of it that was wrong

§18.1's within-pool fit was `17,779 * N^-0.81` on the max and `16,270 *
N^-0.98` on the median, registered before the references were generated. At
N=15 it predicted **1,988** and **1,160**. The median came in at **590**, a
long way better than predicted; the max came in at **2,241**, 12.7% worse.

The max being the sticky one is not noise and it is worth keeping, because it
is the same effect the brief warned about wearing its third disguise. **A floor
quoted as a maximum is an extreme-value statistic over the pool, so a bigger
pool gets more chances to draw a bad candidate.** The like-for-like control is
to read the max over the same six candidates as before:

```text
  max over candidates c..h only, N=15                    1,210
  max over all sixteen candidates, N=15                  2,241
  max over candidates c..h,      N=5                     4,835
```

On the identical six candidates the floor's worst reading fell **4,835 ->
1,210, a factor of four**. The registered 2,241 is larger than that and is the
number this window will be quoted against, because candidate `m` is a real
unmodified vanilla run and its 2,241 is a real reading — it simply did not
exist to be drawn from before. Widening an ensemble genuinely improves the
instrument and *simultaneously* makes its headline statistic look worse than
the improvement really is. Quote the median beside the max, and never compare a
max taken over 6 candidates with a max taken over 16 as though they were the
same quantity.

#### What the margin is now

The same matched-order dump `zx`, unchanged, adjudicated against the widened
ensemble:

```text
                        N      charge              floor                margin
  before (pool 6)       5   7,940 (7,871..8,418)   2,577..4,835 med 3,387   1.64x
  after  (pool 16)     15   7,755 (7,755..7,827)     141..2,241 med   590   3.46x
```

Its baseline is the same matched-order dump the 7,940 was taken from, unchanged — no
code moved between the two rows, only the ensemble.

**The window is at 3.46x its worst innocent reading and 13.1x its median**,
against 1.6x before. The charge itself fell only 185 blocks, which is what it
should do — adding references can only remove positions from an intersection —
and **no code changed, so this is not a fidelity improvement and must not be
read as one.** It is the instrument getting sharper.

Two side effects worth having. The charge's own subset wobble (§18.6)
collapsed from **547 blocks to 72** — 7,755..7,827 over the sixteen 15-of-16
subsets — so the headline no longer depends materially on which references it
is adjudicated against. And the per-block adjudication improved with the floor
under it: **CLEAR 7,088 / marginal 226 / at-or-under-floor 441**, so 91.4% of
the residual now clears its own block's floor by more than twice, against
86.3% before.

#### The held-out test still says the same two things

Re-run on the widened pool, floor estimated on 8 candidates and tested on the
other 8, 40 splits sampled deterministically from 12,870:

```text
  PER-BLOCK   called CLEAR 7,088..7,332, still CLEAR 7,088..7,088,
              survival 96.7%..100.0%
  PER-CHUNK   908..978 chunks read zero-floor on the 8, and the held-out
              candidates are charged 790..2,239 inside them
```

The per-block table generalises better than it did at six references and the
sub-window still does not — but note the sub-window's leak fell from
2,893..4,694 to 790..2,239. That is the estimator-variance explanation in
§18.7 predicting its own improvement: more references sample each chunk bucket
better, so fewer of them read zero by luck. It is still 790 blocks of floor
inside a region claiming none, so the construction stays dead.

## 19. The 20260722 window, re-adjudicated per block — one defect comes back, the window does not (2026-08-10)

§16 re-took this window's level with the chunk order matched and got **2,450 of
107,053,056**, and §17's floor work then disqualified it: an innocent unmodified
vanilla run adjudicated against the same references is charged
**2,202..5,263**, so 2,450 sits inside its own floor. The window has carried no
exactness figure since. §18 did something different on seed 12345 — measured
the floor **per block** rather than per window — and found that a window whose
aggregate is spent can still be sharp for named claims.

This section points that construction at 20260722. The question was whether it
resurrects this window too. **It does not, and it resurrects one defect inside
it, which is a smaller and more useful result than either extreme.**

Everything below is re-run by `ci/floor-census-20260722.sh` in one command. No
vanilla reference was generated for it: the nine that exist were enough, and
the 132-diff matrix `ci/order-level-retake.sh` left behind was the whole input.

### 19.1 First, the harvest order on this window was an assertion

`ci/figures.toml` records `harvest = "rows"` for every 20260722 r=16 overworld
figure. **Eight of the nine references carry no `HARVEST-ORDER` stamp at all.**
Only `-i` does, and `-rr-a` / `-rr-b`, all three stamped by
`ci/order-level-retake.sh` when it promoted them out of a scratch directory.
ORACLE rule 30 is exactly this case: an unstamped reference's order is
*recoverable* but not *known*, and the anchor of an ensemble must be stamped.

So it was recovered rather than asserted. `ci/classify-20260722-order.sh` diffs
each unknown against a stamped reference of each order and reads which
population it falls in, against this window's own scales:

```text
  controls    i    vs rr-a   (rows     vs rows_rev)  149,216   cross-order
              rr-a vs rr-b   (rows_rev vs rows_rev)   36,810   same-order

  a  vs i 32,127   vs rr-a 146,169   sep 114,042  -> rows
  b  vs i 34,813   vs rr-a 145,771   sep 110,958  -> rows
  c  vs i 36,449   vs rr-a 144,137   sep 107,688  -> rows
  d  vs i 32,838   vs rr-a 149,113   sep 116,275  -> rows
  e  vs i 34,122   vs rr-a 146,369   sep 112,247  -> rows
  f  vs i 35,526   vs rr-a 149,188   sep 113,662  -> rows
  g  vs i 24,526   vs rr-a 149,298   sep 124,772  -> rows
  h  vs i 39,085   vs rr-a 148,517   sep 109,432  -> rows
```

**The assertion was true.** All eight are `rows`, and none is close: the eight
separations span **107,688..124,772** against a same-order scale of 36,810, so
no verdict here is a coin toss wearing a number. That is a null worth the cost of
killing — had even one of the eight been `rows_rev`, every figure on this
window would have been measuring the order gap rather than the run gap.

It is recorded rather than forgotten. The classifier writes a stamps tree whose
files carry a `classified_from=` line holding the two distances that decided
each verdict, `ci/floor-census.py` records them in the emitted table as
`harvest_classified`, and it **refuses** a pool in which every member is
derived — because a classification read against no stamped anchor is an
assertion again.

The classifier also carries a negative control the seed-12345 one does not.
Every real input here is expected to answer `rows`, so the discriminator is
never exercised by the data; `rr-a` and `rr-b` are therefore run back through
the unknown path and must come back `rows_rev`. They do. Without that, a
classifier that answered `rows` unconditionally would have produced this exact
output.

### 19.2 The floor, per block

Nine references, leave-one-out N=8, `p_hide` 31.2%. The aggregate reproduces
the registered range exactly — **2,202..5,263, median 3,281, n=9** — which is
the positive control that the matrix and the construction are the ones §16 and
§17 used.

```text
  union of the nine innocent charges : 30,574 positions
    charged in 1 of 9 : 30,281      charged in 2 of 9 : 282
    charged in 3 of 9 :     11
  73 distinct vanilla blocks carry any MISS floor
  84 distinct blocks were PLACED by any innocent run
```

The floor is per-run noise rather than a fixed hard set: 99.0% of the union is
charged to exactly one of the nine. The top of it is the same family seed 12345
showed — `granite` 891, `diorite` 718, `andesite` 554 — plus `sulfur` 705 and
`cinnabar` 553, and `water[level=0]` 612 with `kelp_plant` 232 from the ocean
columns this window has and 12345's does not.

### 19.3 The item: the 2,450, adjudicated in both directions

The charge is adjudicated against the **same eight references** the floor is a
leave-one-out over, because a charge taken at a different N is not comparable
to a floor taken at this one — more references can only shrink an
intersection. At N=8 it is **2,450 to the block**, which reproduces
`ow-r16-admissible-matched` exactly and is the positive control on everything
below: the matrix, the parser and the construction are the ones §16 used. Over
all nine it is 2,443, and the nine 8-of-9 subsets span **2,443..2,514**, which
is the charge's own sampling wobble and is quoted for the reason the floor's
is.

Both directions matter and they are different tables:

```text
  MISS direction — keyed on the VANILLA spelling ([[block]])
    grass_block[snowy=false]     649       1   649.0x  CLEAR
    water[level=0]               422     612     0.7x  AT/UNDER FLOOR
    diorite                      189     718     0.3x  AT/UNDER FLOOR
    andesite                     174     554     0.3x  AT/UNDER FLOOR
    seagrass                     168     149     1.1x  marginal
    ...
    CLEAR 803   marginal 449   at/under floor 1,198

  SURPLUS direction — keyed on what WE placed ([[placed]])
    dirt                         648      71     9.1x  CLEAR
    water[level=0]               538     746     0.7x  AT/UNDER FLOOR
    granite                      279     551     0.5x  AT/UNDER FLOOR
    short_grass                  123       8    15.4x  CLEAR
    ...
    CLEAR 824   marginal 394   at/under floor 1,232

  BOTH — each position takes the WEAKER of its two verdicts
    CLEAR in both directions               650
    marginal in the weaker direction        96
    at or under a floor in one direction 1,704
```

**Quote the join.** A position is only evidence of a defect if vanilla
reproduces both what it put there and what we put there; if either block is one
an innocent run is itself charged for at this rate, the position is consistent
with being a different run rather than a different generator. The two single
directions read 803 and 824, and the join is 650 — smaller than either, which
is the point of taking it. **And the join is 650 at N=8 and 650 at
N=9**: only the unadjudicable bucket moves with N, which is what a robust
headline looks like and is not something the aggregate reading could show.

**So 650 of 2,450 — 26.5% — clears its own floor in both directions, 96 is
marginal, and 1,704 (69.6%) does not clear and is corroboration only.**

Both of the figures this section registers — the join, and the one pair that
carries it — are baselined this way:

> measured against our own order-matched dump, not against unmodified vanilla — the floor beside it is the innocent-vanilla one

That is what makes them a decomposition of our own charge rather than a new
reading of the window, and it is why no exactness percentage may be computed
from 650.

### 19.4 And the 650 is one defect, not a spread

`grass_block[snowy=false]` is 649 of the miss side against a miss floor of
**1**, and the block we put there instead is `dirt`, 648 of the surplus side
against a placed floor of **71**. Both directions clear; the binding one is the
surplus, and the matched pair reads **649 against a matched floor of 71** —
**9.1x**. Everything else in the join is noise around it.

This is not a new defect — §14 measured `grass_block -> dirt` at 646 on this
window with a floor of 1, and it is 76% of the order-invariant core §15 named.
**What is new is that the whole of the rest of the 2,450 is now known not to be
adjudicable.** Before this, "2,450 below a floor of 2,202..5,263" was a single
verdict over the window; the port could have been wrong in 2,450 places or in
649 of them and no instrument said which. It is 649, plus a marginal tail.

### 19.5 The zeros survive; the sub-window does not

A floor of zero is an estimate from nine innocent runs, not a proof that
vanilla never varies there, and this table has 803 blocks of CLEAR resting on
small ones. Held out — floor estimated on 4 of the 9 and tested against the
other 5, 40 splits:

```text
  PER-BLOCK   called CLEAR 803..1,232, still CLEAR 803..803,
              survival 65.2%..100.0%
  PER-CHUNK   793..850 chunks read zero-floor on the 4, and a held-out
              innocent run is charged 2,219..4,102 inside them against our
              1,968..2,218
              [disqualifier: selected on the same runs that validate it]
```

The per-block table **generalises**: whichever half the floor is estimated on,
the same 803 survive. The excess above 803 is what an unlucky split invents,
and it never survives.

The per-chunk sub-window **does not**, and on this window it fails harder than
on 12345: a held-out innocent run is charged *more* inside the "clean" chunks
(median 2,847) than our dump is (median 2,087). A sub-window chosen to avoid
the floor on the runs that define it has no floor by construction, and here the
construction would have handed us a region where vanilla out-charges us.

### 19.6 The nulls, so nobody re-runs them

* **Widening across harvest orders is much worse here than on seed 12345.**
  Pooling the nine `rows` references with the two `rows_rev` ones takes the
  floor from 2,202..5,263 to **1,066..13,273** — the maximum rises 8,010 blocks,
  against 1,293 on 12345. **Disqualifier: mixed harvest order**, and it is
  never this window's floor. The whole rise is the two minority members, charged
  9,086 and 13,273 because each is adjudicated mostly against references of the
  other order. Do not do this.
* **The sub-window construction is dead on this window too**, and for the
  reason §18's is: a per-chunk floor gets a handful of positions in each of
  1,089 buckets where a per-block floor gets thousands in each of 73.
* **The harvest order of `a..h` is not a live question.** It is `rows`, measured
  with controls, and re-running the classification costs nothing but proves
  nothing new.

### 19.7 What this window would need to state a level again

It cannot, and more references will not fix it — which is the useful half of
the answer. The aggregate floor is 2,202..5,263 against a charge of 2,450, and
**1,704 blocks of that charge sit on blocks vanilla itself does not reproduce
on this window**: `water[level=0]` at a floor of 612, the stone variants at
554..891, `kelp_plant` at 232. Those are not sampling noise that a tenth
reference thins — §18.8 took the 12345 floor 4,835 -> 2,241 by going from 6
references to 16, and even a proportional win here leaves the ocean-column and
stone-variant floors above our charge on them, because our charge on them is
already *below* what an innocent run is charged.

The three things that would change the answer, with the numbers behind them:

1. **A different geometry, not more references.** 508 of 1,089 chunks hold any
   floor and the ocean columns are most of the top of it. A window centred away
   from water would drop `water[level=0]` 612, `kelp_plant` 232 and the
   seagrass pair 149+103 out of the floor — but the held-out test in §19.5 says
   a sub-window of *this* window chosen that way is selection on n=9, so it has
   to be a **new** window with its own references, not a crop of this one.
2. **A different seed.** Seed 12345 on the identical instrument and geometry
   resolves at 3.46x its floor on the widened pool (§18.8) where this one
   resolves at none. The project already has its quotable overworld window and
   it is not this one.
3. **Nothing at all, and use it for what it is good at.** This window carries
   the project's sharpest single named defect — `grass_block -> dirt` at 649
   against a matched floor of 71 — and deltas on it remain exact against a
   fixed reference set and a fixed pair of orders. That is what it is for now.

**The recommendation is 3.** Ten more references cost about five hours of
vanilla boots and would buy a smaller number to still not be able to quote; the
one defect the window does adjudicate is already named, already located, and is
worth more than the window's aggregate ever was.

## 20. The defect §19 named, closed — 2,436 -> 1,786 and the item to zero (2026-08-10)

*(Branch `worldgen-carver-topmaterial`, off `floor-20260722-perblock`
cef0f06b. Seed 20260722, r=16, centre (64,64), bbox 48 48 80 80, 107,053,056
blocks, eight unmodified vanilla references `gen-wide-20260722-r16-ow-a..h`,
matched chunk order, VANILLA FIRST, Threadripper. `ci/grassdirt-retake.sh`
re-takes every number here in one command.)*

§19 ended by recommending option 3 — stop trying to rescue this window's
aggregate and spend it on the one defect it adjudicates. That is what this
section does.

**The measured result.**

| | charge | `grass_block[snowy=false] -> dirt` | CLEAR in both directions |
|---|---|---|---|
| base cef0f06b | 2,436 | 646 | 650 |
| with the fix | **1,786** | **0** | **4** |
| delta | **-650** | -646 | -646 |

**Newly charged: 0.** Every one of the nine references improved by *exactly*
650 positions and not one by a different number. **This is a delta and not a
level** — this window is still registered `quotable = false`, disqualifier
`below floor`: 1,786 sits inside the innocent-vanilla floor registered as
`ow-r16-20260722-floor-n8` (median 3,281) at the same N over the same
ensemble, exactly as 2,436 did. No
exactness percentage may be computed from either number.

### 20.1 The item is 646, and the brief's 649 and 648 are its two marginals

`ci/floor-census.py` keys its two directions separately, each marginalised
over the other side, and that is correct — but it means the same "pair" has
three sizes:

| keyed on | count | decomposition |
|---|---|---|
| MISS, the vanilla spelling | 649 | 646 dirt + 3 clay |
| SURPLUS, what we placed | 648 | 646 grass_block + 2 coal_ore |
| **the ordered pair itself** | **646** | — |

They agree. `ci/confusion-pair-census.py` reports all three and its
`--selftest` injects the failure of computing the joint as either marginal.
Both floors were looked up through the full-state key and neither reads zero:
miss floor 1 (runs_charged 2), placed floor 71 (runs_charged 9), so 9.1x on
the binding side, as §19 published.

### 20.2 The census located it before any class was opened

Rule 9, and it named the suspect on its own: the 646 are **one block deep in
all 646 of their distinct x/z columns** — a skin, not a volume — spread over
**y 50..85** with 349 in y48..63 and 281 in y64..79 and peaks at y=62 (157)
and y=63 (127), touching only **60 of 1,089 chunks** with half of it in 8, in
**191 six-connected clusters** whose median size is 1 and largest is 42. The
chunk-border share is **11.6% against 12.5% for a uniform scatter**, which
kills the chunk-seam hypothesis on the positions alone.

A one-block skin at sea level, in many small rings, in a minority of chunks,
off the seams. That is the lip of a cave mouth.

### 20.3 Two defects, and the first made the second unreachable

From the 26.2 bytecode, `WorldCarver#carveBlock`: at offsets 8..27 it arms a
`MutableBoolean` from `BlockState#is(Blocks.GRASS_BLOCK)` and
`is(Blocks.MYCELIUM)` — `is(Block)` compares the **block** and ignores
properties — and at offsets 109..176, when that flag is true and
`getBlockState(pos.below()).is(Blocks.DIRT)`, it calls
`CarvingContext#topMaterial` and writes the result. It reads as a **rim**
because the interior of a cave mouth exposes stone, which the DIRT test
rejects; only the edge exposes dirt.

1. `carve_block` armed `has_grass` with `existing == "minecraft:grass_block"`
   — a **state** string against a **block** name — and the overworld surface
   rule's own result state carries `snowy=false`. The arm could never fire in
   the real generator.
2. `generate_decorating` then stubbed the closure itself to `None`.

Either one alone hides the other, which is why the caveat said "stubbed"
while `golden_carver_chunk` asserted `topMaterial was asked for 0 times` and
both were green. Rule 7: the measurement had stopped looking.

`SurfaceSystem#topMaterial` itself is cheap and is not the surface stage in
miniature: a one-shot `SurfaceRules$Context`, the RuleSource compiled,
`tryApply(x,y,z)` called once, `stoneDepthAbove` and `stoneDepthBelow` both
**hardcoded to 1** at offsets 66..67, and the biome `Set` argument
`aconst_null`. The fluid flag has exactly one use — `waterHeight = y + 1`
when the carved block holds fluid, else `Integer.MIN_VALUE` — and that single
bit is what picks `grass_block` over `dirt`.

### 20.4 Three controls, because a green number is not evidence on its own

* **A poison control that fires.** Reverting *only* the arm to the
  state-string compare, leaving the whole closure in place, returns the
  charge to 2,436 and the pair to 646, and `RUSTMC_CARVER_TOPMAT=1` reports
  **0 asks** where the fixed build reports **4,897 (632 under a fluid, 29
  declined for an unfilled biome container)**. That distinguishes "the
  closure is routed through the measured path" from "the closure exists".
  The 632 matter specifically: the fluid flag is topMaterial's only use of
  its fourth argument, so a window that never carved into water would not
  have exercised the branch that chooses between the two blocks at all.
* **The instrument cannot move a block.** `RUSTMC_CARVER_TOPMAT` gates an
  `eprintln!` only; the counters increment unconditionally.
* **Worker count is not a confounder, measured rather than assumed.** These
  dumps were taken at `RUSTMC_WORKERS=6` because the box is shared, while the
  stored ladder dump was taken at the default pool. The base tree at 6 and at
  the default are **byte-identical over this window — `mismatches=0` across all
  107,053,056 blocks**, which is what licenses the comparison.

### 20.5 The baseline is 2,436 on this tip, and that is not this branch's number to fix

`ow-r16-admissible-matched` publishes **2,450**. Dumped from the unmodified
base cef0f06b in the matched order the charge is **2,436**. The stored
`dump-zx` region that figure was measured on differs from a dump of this tip
by **14 blocks** — all stronghold brickwork (`stone_brick_stairs`,
`mossy_stone_bricks`, `cracked_stone_bricks` and their mossy stairs), all in
y32..47, clustered at (771..774, 44, 1035) — so **that dump was not made from
this tip**. AGENT-BRIEF's rule applies exactly: a restack moves a branch's own
baseline, and two numbers are not a contradiction until you check which tip
each measured.

**The item is unaffected either way.** On the stored dump and on a dump of
this tip alike, the miss is 649, the surplus 648, and the join 650 — because
the 14 sit in the unadjudicable buckets. Whoever owns
`ow-r16-admissible-matched` should reconcile which tip produced its dump.

### 20.6 What is NOT a remaining gap

`generate_nether` still stubs the closure to `None` and that is **correct**:
`NetherWorldCarver` overrides `carveBlock` and its override holds **zero**
references to `topMaterial` (javap, 26.2). Named here so it is not chased.

`golden_carver_chunk`'s assertion is inverted from `== 0` to `> 0`, but it is
an integration test and `ci/t0.sh` runs `--lib --bins`, so **T0 does not reach
it**. The control that adjudicates this change is the measured one in §20.4,
not that assertion.

### 20.7 The same fix on seed 12345 — 7,940 -> 7,704, and this half IS quotable

The brief that opened this item asked for a re-measurement on seed 12345 on
the grounds that fixes generalise and 12345 is the window that can state a
level. It does generalise, and this is the more quotable half of the result.

*(Seed 12345, r=16, centre (64,64), same bbox and denominator, references
`gen-wide-12345-r16-ow-c..g` (N=5) — the same ensemble
`ow-r16-12345-admissible-iceberg` was taken over — matched order `zx` read
off `-c`'s stamp, VANILLA FIRST.)*

| | charge | exactness | floor (max, N=5) |
|---|---|---|---|
| base cef0f06b | 7,940 | 99.992583% | 4,835 |
| with the fix | **7,704** | **99.992804%** | 4,835 |
| delta | **-236** | | unmoved |

**Newly charged: 0.** The base tip reproduces the registered 7,940 *exactly*,
split 6,854 CLEAR / 509 marginal / 577 at-or-under — the same three numbers
`ow-r16-12345-admissible-iceberg` records — which is the positive control on
this arm. **The floor did not move**, because it is a property of the
reference ensemble and this change did not touch it; the whole of the
improvement is in the charge, which is what distinguishes a repair from a
luckier ensemble. The window stays quotable: 7,704 still clears its N=5 floor
of 4,835.

**The confusion table here is the strongest single piece of evidence in this
section**, and it is not the one the 20260722 window gave:

| | count |
|---|---|
| `grass_block[snowy=true] -> dirt` | 213 |
| `grass_block[snowy=false] -> dirt` | 19 |
| refs split between the two spellings | 3 |
| `grass_block[snowy=false] -> clay` | 1 |

**213 of the 236 come back `snowy=true`.** A stub cannot produce that and
neither can a hardcoded "put grass here": the `snowy` property is set by the
surface rule reading the block above, so the re-grassed cave lips in the snowy
biomes of this seed are being decided by an actual evaluation of the rule tree
at the exposed block. `RUSTMC_CARVER_TOPMAT=1` reports **1,503 asks, 174 of
them under a fluid, 3 declined** on this window.

**A note on which floor to quote.** §17.1 says an item closing a *named* pair
on this window should quote that pair's own floor rather than the aggregate
4,835. That guidance is not used to inflate anything here: the figure
registered for this arm is the aggregate charge and the aggregate floor, which
is the conservative reading, and the delta is quoted separately.

## 21. The order-inverted row: `sulfur -> cinnabar` is ours, and the instrument is cleared (2026-08-10)

Measured on branch `worldgen-sulfur-cinnabar-order`, off
`worldgen-carver-topmaterial` **8c7e024c**, Threadripper. Seed 12345, r=16
overworld, centre chunk (64,64), 1,089 chunks, 107,053,056 blocks, bbox
`48 48 80 80`, full height, `RUSTMC_WORKERS=1`, **VANILLA FIRST**, adjudicated
against the sixteen stamped `gen_order=rows` references
`gen-wide-12345-r16-ow-{c..r}` at N=15 leave-one-out.

**State the tip, because a restack moves a branch's own baseline.** Every
figure in this section was taken on 8c7e024c and on no other commit. The
aggregate level on this window is registered `quotable = false` and none is
quoted here; what follows is a per-block row, its own floor, and a delta.

### The item, and what makes it different from every other row on the board

`ci/floor-census.py --adjudicate` against
`ci/floor-tables/ow-r16-12345-rows-n15.toml`:

```text
  row                          charged   miss floor   surplus floor   binding   ratio
  sulfur -> cinnabar               312            3               3         3    104x
```

Neither floor reads zero, so this is not the pooled-to-a-bare-name failure
that silently reports a floor of zero. The miss floor is keyed on the vanilla
spelling `sulfur` and the surplus floor on the block we placed, `cinnabar`;
both come from the same sixteen-reference pool that produced the row.

**And the row is order-INVERTED, which is the finding rather than a nuisance.**
The same instrument, the same references, the same tip, our dump taken twice
and differing in nothing but chunk visitation order:

```text
                              x-major (default)      zx (the stamped order)
  sulfur -> cinnabar                          0                         312
  vanilla = sulfur, marginal                 82                         483
  whole-window admissible charge          13,164                       7,528
```

At every one of the 312 positions our x-major dump writes `sulfur` and agrees
with all sixteen references; our `zx` dump writes `cinnabar`. The two sets do
not overlap at all — `in both 0`, `zx only 312` — so the row does not *move*
under the other order, it **vanishes**.

### Do not open the feature's bytecode on that. Decompose the order first

ORACLE rule 9, and rule 29 is directly on point. `ci/order-pair-decompose.py`
was written for this and takes three cuts.

**By chunk, and it is one place:**

```text
  272  (77, 48)      25  (78, 48)      8  (79, 48)      6  (79, 49)      1  (80, 50)
```

**By y band:** 194 in `0..15`, 103 in `16..31`, 13 in `-16..-1`, 2 below. A
body, not a scatter.

**And by what vanilla does at the same positions under a different harvest
order — the control that decides whose defect this is.** It needs no new
reference: a position that `c-vs-rr-a.pos` does not list is one where the
`rows_rev` run wrote what the `rows` run wrote.

```text
  rr-a   vanilla's other order differs at 2 of 312   (0.64%)
  rr-b   vanilla's other order differs at 2 of 312   (0.64%)
```

**Vanilla writes `sulfur` there whichever way it walks the window. We write
`sulfur` one way and `cinnabar` the other.**

### The three suspects, and which one the measurement leaves standing

The brief that opened this named three and asked for a measurement, not a
reading.

1. *a feature whose iteration order differs from vanilla's, so the same blocks
   land in a different sequence and only one order's reference agrees* —
   **STANDS.**
2. *a reference harvested in an order that does not match its own
   `HARVEST-ORDER` stamp, so the charge is an artefact of the instrument* —
   **KILLED, three ways.** All sixteen `rows` references carry a stamp and all
   sixteen agree (`gen_order=rows dump_order=zx`); no unstamped reference is in
   the pool and `ci/sulfur-order-decompose.sh` refuses on a mixed one (ORACLE
   rule 30). Matching the stamped order *improves* the whole-window charge
   13,164 -> 7,528, so the stamp is not naming the wrong knob. And vanilla's
   own cross-order runs agree with each other at 310 of the 312, so there is no
   harvest-order ambiguity left at these positions for an artefact to hide in.
3. *a genuine placement defect that happens to correlate with order because the
   feature is chunk-border-sensitive* — **not the shape of it.** A placement
   defect puts the block somewhere wrong; here the block is not placed at all.
   `RUSTMC_TRACE_WRITE=1233,22,770` under the two orders:

```text
  x-major   WRITE (1233,22,770) <- minecraft:stone      by ? (no feature running)
            WRITE (1233,22,770) <- minecraft:cinnabar   by ? (no feature running)
            WRITE (1233,22,770) <- minecraft:sulfur     by minecraft:sulfur_spike from chunk (77, 48)
  zx        the third line is ABSENT
```

`minecraft:cinnabar` is written by the surface rule alone — no feature in this
tree writes it — so `sulfur -> cinnabar` means *a feature that should have
painted sulfur over the surface rule's cinnabar did not run or did not reach*.
Under the stamped order `minecraft:sulfur_spike` never writes.

**So the correct outcome here is NOT a withdrawn charge.** If it had been
suspect 2 it would have been, and that would have been a perfectly good result;
it is not, and saying so is the point of having measured it.

### The vanilla-side prior this had to clear, and did

`AGENT-BRIEF.md` is explicit that "we are order-dependent where vanilla is not"
has to clear vanilla's own order floor first. On this row vanilla's floor is
large — measured on the existing sixteen-reference matrix, no dump involved:

```text
  two runs of the SAME order      c vs d      150   c vs e   29   c vs r   27
  two runs of DIFFERENT orders    c vs rr-a   426   c vs rr-b 506
```

So `sulfur -> cinnabar` is genuinely order-variant terrain in vanilla, by
roughly 4x across orders, and a hypothesis that ignored that would have been
worthless. What survives it is the leave-one-out intersection: the 312
positions are exactly the ones where **all sixteen** references agree and we do
not, and at those the cross-order control says vanilla is stable to 0.64%.
The aggregate prior is real and it does not reach these positions.

### A repro that costs seconds, not a full window

`worldgen-dump full 12345 6 <out> 78 49` under the two orders, differenced
inside bbox `72 43 84 55`: **3,937 blocks move, 1,023 of them
`sulfur -> cinnabar`.** Iterate there; confirm on the window.

### What is registered, and what is not

Nothing here is a level. The row and its floor are a per-block adjudication and
they stand on their own; the whole-window charge is quoted only as the
before/after of a knob, which is a delta against a fixed reference set and does
not depend on the level being quotable.

### The control that nearly overturned all of it, and did not — ORACLE rule 14

The positional footprint of the whole inversion looks exactly like a
context-boundary artefact, and it would have been dishonest not to chase it.
Charged under `zx` and **not** under x-major: 1,097 positions, 25 chunks,
76.1% of them sulfur-cave family — and **every one of those chunks sits at
z = 48, 49 or 50**, which is the low-z edge of the scored bbox:

```text
  (77,48) 387   (79,49) 300   (79,48) 186   (80,48) 41   (78,48) 38   … 20 more
```

The window is chunks 48..80 and `DECORATION_MARGIN` is 2, so under `zx` — z
outer, ascending — a chunk at z=48 has only two decorated rows beneath it,
where under x-major it has a fully decorated column beside it. A charge that
appears only at the leading edge of a sweep is the shape of a window that is
too small for its own decoration order, and if that were the cause the correct
outcome would have been a corrected instrument and a **withdrawn charge**, not
a code change.

It is not the cause. Rule 14 says widen the context until the answer stops
changing and quote the converged one. Re-dumped at **r=20** — chunks 44..84,
six decorated rows below z=48 instead of two — under the same `zx`, scored over
the identical bbox `48 48 80 80` against the same sixteen references:

```text
                              r=16 dump      r=20 dump
  whole-window charge             7,528          7,505
  sulfur -> cinnabar                312            312
  chunk (77,48), all rows           387            387
  the row by chunk        (77,48) 272, (78,48) 25, (79,48) 8, (79,49) 6, (80,50) 1
                                     — identical, block for block
```

**Zero movement.** The row is not our margin and the low-z concentration is
where this seed's sulfur cave is, not where our sweep starts. The charge is
converged and it is ours.

### Eight orders, two populations, and no common minimum — the row is not reachable by this knob

If the row were *only* a matter of walking the window the way vanilla walked
it, one order would put it at zero and leave everything else at its best too.
Eight of ours, same tip, same sixteen references, same bbox, N=15, one dump
each:

```text
  order        total charge   sulfur -> cinnabar   stone-variant rows   chunk (77,48)
  x-major            13,164                    0                5,741               0
  zx                  7,528                  312                  203             387
  spawn               9,226                    0                2,437               0
  zx_rev             23,878                  522                7,245             398
  spiral             28,201                   79               16,404             120
  spiral_in          34,538                  337               21,496             387
  zx_zrev            82,823                  246               59,776             120
  reverse            92,591                  693               61,149             518
```

**No order minimises both populations.** The stone-variant ore blobs — the
classic order-sensitive family, and the one that is under its own floor
everywhere — want `zx` and are 28x worse under x-major. The sulfur cave wants
x-major or `spawn` and is charged 312 under `zx`. The best order for one is
never the best order for the other, and the gap is not marginal in either
direction.

That is the finding. **The residual is not reachable by turning this knob**, so
"our chunk order is wrong" cannot be the explanation for both, and at least one
of the two populations is disagreeing with vanilla for a reason that is not
chunk order at all. The stone variants sit under their own per-block floor and
are vanilla disagreeing with itself; the sulfur cave clears its floor 104x. So
the one that is left is the sulfur cave, and it is code.

**What the orders that zero it have in common, stated as a lead and not as a
conclusion.** x-major and `spawn` both decorate (76,49) and (76,50) *before*
(77,48); `zx` does not, and neither does vanilla's own `rows`. Two orders break
the clean split — `spiral` also runs (76,49) first and still charges 79, and
`zx_zrev` charges 246 — so the neighbour in the −x/+z quadrant is necessary and
not sufficient, and the honest statement is that chunk (77,48)'s decoration in
this tree depends on chunks vanilla's does not. `RUSTMC_TRACE_WRITE` puts the
missing write in `minecraft:sulfur_spike` **from chunk (77,48) itself**, so
what is crossing the border is a *read*, not a write.

### The cross-chunk influence is DESTRUCTIVE, not enabling — and it flips the obvious reading

The natural reading of "the spike does not write under `zx`" is that it is
waiting on something a neighbour has not yet produced. **It is the other way
round, and one command settles it.** `RUSTMC_DECORATE_ONLY=77,48` decorates
that chunk and no other, so no neighbour has decorated at all:

```text
  RUSTMC_DECORATE_ONLY=77,48   WRITE (1233,22,770) <- minecraft:sulfur
                                   by minecraft:sulfur_spike from chunk (77, 48)
  x-major, whole window        …identical line…
  zx, whole window             …absent…
```

The spike fires **with no neighbours whatever**. So nothing is missing under
`zx`: a chunk that decorates *before* (77,48) under `zx`, and does not under
x-major, is **breaking** it. Under `zx` the chunks decorated before (77,48)
that x-major has not yet reached are exactly the +x/−z quadrant — (78,47),
(79,47), (80,47) and the rest of z<48 at x>77.

**And vanilla has the same precedence.** Vanilla's `rows` also decorates z=47
before z=48, so vanilla's (78,47) runs before its (77,48) too — and vanilla
still writes sulfur at all 312. So the interfering write is one vanilla's
neighbour does not make, or our scan is fragile to a write vanilla's scan
tolerates.

### Correcting one sentence above: the feature runs, it is reshaped

Earlier in this section the row is glossed as "a feature that should have
painted sulfur did not run". The full confusion breakdown inside chunk (77,48)
under `zx` says that is too strong, and the stronger claim is the wrong one:

```text
  272  sulfur -> cinnabar                          (we miss sulfur)
   23  cinnabar -> sulfur                          (we ADD sulfur vanilla does not have)
   23  sulfur_spike[tip,up]     -> air             (spikes we do not place)
    6  air -> sulfur_spike[tip,up]                 (spikes we place that vanilla does not)
   15  sulfur_spike[tip,down]   -> air
    5  air -> sulfur_spike[tip,down]
   …  every one of the 387 is sulfur / cinnabar / sulfur_spike, and nothing else
   y bands: 263 in 0..15, 122 in 16..31, 2 in 48..63
```

Surplus in both directions. The cluster **runs and is displaced**, which is
what a floor/ceiling scan returning a different answer looks like — not what a
feature that never fired looks like. And no third block family appears among
the 387, so whatever the interfering neighbour writes is itself in agreement
with vanilla and is invisible to the charge; it is the *scan's* reading of it
that diverges.

### The mechanism: an RNG stream shift, not a rejected placement

`RUSTMC_DECORATE_PREFIX=78,48,1` with `RUSTMC_TRACE_ATTEMPT=minecraft:` prints
59 `ATTEMPT` lines for chunk (77,48). **The first 39 features are identical
under the two orders.** Only lines 40-41 differ:

```text
  - ATTEMPT minecraft:sulfur_spike_cluster chunk (77,48) reached-feature=21 draws=2721 rejected: biome=68
  + ATTEMPT minecraft:sulfur_spike_cluster chunk (77,48) reached-feature=20 draws=1081 rejected: biome=69
```

With `RUSTMC_TRACE_ATTEMPT_POS=1` the cluster's emitted positions agree for
nine entries and part at the tenth: last common `REACHED at (1245,24,771)
draw=317`, then `(1236,13,769) draw=938` against `(1235,17,779) draw=919`.
**The same position, reached at a different draw count** — so the divergence is
a world read *inside the cluster body*, at
`crates/rustmc-worldgen/src/feature/speleothem_cluster.rs:252-284`
(`scan` / `scan_direction`, i.e. `is_empty_or_water`, one call per column of the
±radius disc). `sulfur_spike`'s own chain is `count, in_square, height_range,
count, random_offset, biome` — no world-reading modifier — so its own
2,843 -> 2,888 shift is purely downstream of the cluster's.

**Which neighbour: (78,47), and not (76,49).** Isolated by holding the
decorating set fixed at four chunks and swapping only the order of (78,47)
against (77,48). On that small window the eight orders split cleanly into
exactly two draw counts, and membership is predicted by "(78,47) first" alone.
`spiral` runs (76,49) *after* (77,48) and is still good, which kills the −x/+z
lead recorded above — **that lead was wrong and this supersedes it.**

`RUSTMC_DECORATE_ONLY=77,48 RUSTMC_TRACE_SPELEO='*'` gives the cluster 407
columns, max disc offset ±8, spread `228 (77,48) / 100 (78,48) / 64 (77,47) /
15 (78,47)`. The 15 columns in (78,47) are x 1248..1253 × z 766..767 — the
corner where the disc crosses the border.

### The reach null, and it is clean

```text
  RUSTMC_READ_REACH=1        DECORATION READ REACH (max Chebyshev chunk distance) 1
  RUSTMC_READ_REACH_TRAP=0   panic: read reach 1 > 0: decorating chunk (70,41)
                             reached block (1131,652) in chunk (70,40)
```

The trap proves the probe is live rather than silent. The largest read on the
speleothem path is **1 chunk — the same as the feature write radius**, and a
±8-block disc from an in-chunk origin cannot exceed it. **Our decision reach is
not wider than our write reach**, so "we read further than vanilla" is dead.

### What is NOT established, and it is the next move

The only difference found inside those 15 columns is `air -> oak_planks` and a
`rail` at **y −44..−43**, from a mineshaft-shaped feature in (78,47). **The
causal link from that block to the cluster's scan is not established, and there
is a reason to doubt it:** the cluster's origins here are at y 13..24 and
`floor_to_ceiling_search_range` is 12, so a flip at y −44 is ~57 blocks outside
the range the scan is supposed to look in. Either the scan is reaching further
than its own range says — which would be the defect, and a `javap` read of
vanilla's cluster scan bounds is now the right first move rather than the wrong
one — or the real interfering write is elsewhere in those columns and was not
found.

**And the mineshaft itself cannot be adjudicated from this ensemble.** Chunk
(78,47) is at z=47, outside the scored bbox `48 48 80 80` **and outside the
r=16 references entirely**, so nothing here says whether our planks match
vanilla's. Establishing that needs a reference that covers z=47; the existing
ones must not be regenerated.

### The bytecode read of the scan bounds — the 57-block gap cannot be bridged, and branch (A) is dead

The move above named two branches: either the scan reaches further than its own
range says, or the interfering write is elsewhere and was not found. **A `javap`
read of the bounds kills the first one outright.** Read from the pinned jar,
`net/minecraft/world/level/levelgen/Column.class`:

`Column.scan(reader, pos, range, columnPredicate, tipPredicate)` takes `pos`'s
own `getY()` as the anchor — `aload_1; getY; istore 6` — and hands that same
integer to both `scanDirection` calls, `UP` first and `DOWN` second. There is
no other anchor in the method: no heightmap read, no `WorldGenerationContext`,
no min/max build-height clamp, and no offset applied to `pos` before the walk.

`scanDirection` opens `mutable.setY(startY)`, so the shared `MutableBlockPos`
is **re-anchored at the origin for the second call** rather than continuing
from wherever the first walk stopped, and its x/z are never touched because
`move(Direction.UP|DOWN)` moves only y. The loop is `i = 1; while (i < range &&
isStateAtPosition(mutable, columnPredicate)) { move(dir); i++ }`, then a single
`isStateAtPosition(mutable, tipPredicate)` on whatever it stopped on. So the
walk takes **at most `range - 1` steps from the origin, in each direction, with
no clamp of any kind.**

Our `scan` / `scan_direction` at `speleothem_cluster.rs:252-284` are
byte-for-byte this: same anchor, same `i < range`, same UP-before-DOWN, same
`create(down, up)` argument order, same "an end is only recorded if the block
the walk stopped on satisfies the tip predicate".

**Therefore the reachable y extent of a cluster whose origins are y 13..24 with
`floor_to_ceiling_search_range: 12` is y 2..35, and it is exactly that in
vanilla too.** A block at y −44..−43 is 57 blocks outside it and **no
arithmetic in the reference brings it inside**. The mineshaft `oak_planks` and
`rail` recorded above are **not** the mechanism, and the reason they looked
like a lead is the marker: they are simply the only thing that differed in the
part of the column a whole-column census printed.

`SpeleothemClusterFeature.place` and `placeColumn` were read in the same pass
and match ours throughout — the guard before any draw, the
`height/wetness/density/xRadius/zRadius` order, the x-outer/z-inner loop over
`origin.offset(x, 0, z)`, the `Math.min(height, ceiling - floor)` cap, the
`withFloor(floor - 1)` pool, the merge split
`randomBetweenInclusive(lo, hi + 1)` with `lo = max(ceiling - stalactite,
floor + 1)` and `hi = min(floor + stalagmite, ceiling - 1)`, and the
**unconditional `nextBoolean()`** spent before the merge conjunction can be
evaluated. So the draw-count difference is not arithmetic in the feature body:
it is driven entirely by which columns the world lets `scan` return an end for.

**What this leaves.** The interfering write is inside x 1248..1253, z 766..767
at **y 13..35** — the intersection of the divergent cluster's disc with chunk
(78,47), restricted to the band the scan can actually see. That is a much
smaller search than the column census that found the planks, and it is the next
measurement.

### The mechanism, measured end to end: a cross-border `sulfur_spike` deletes a column before it can draw

`ci/sulfur-read-trace.sh`, both arms on tip 710f9604, seed 12345, centre
(64,64), r=16, `RUSTMC_WORKERS=1`, four chunks decorating
(`RUSTMC_DECORATE_PREFIX=78,48,1`), traversal digests `1afc539f71b91805` (zx)
and `8632f512e1327a85` (default) — distinct, so the knob turned.

Decoration order, read off the trace rather than asserted:

```text
  zx       (77,47)  (78,47)  (79,47)  (77,48)
  default  (77,47)  (77,48)  (78,47)  (79,47)
```

Diffing the **read position sequences** of `sulfur_spike_cluster from chunk
(77,48)` — positions, not states, so a differing *state* is not mistaken for a
differing *decision* — the arms are identical for 477 reads and then fork at
column **(1247,24,766)**:

```text
  zx        1247,24,766  1247,24,766                      -> next column
  default   1247,24,766  1247,24,766  1247,25,766 x4
                         1247,24,766  1247,23..13,766  1247,25,766
```

Two reads and out, against a full scan. Two reads is `is_empty_or_water`
answering **false**: it costs one canvas read when the block is air and two
when it is not, and the second read is the `== "minecraft:water"` test. So in
the `zx` arm `Column.scan`'s opening guard fails, `scan` returns empty, and
`placeColumn` returns **before spending a single draw**.

What is in that block, and who put it there — identical writer in both arms,
only the timing moves:

```text
  FEATWRITE 1247,24,766 = minecraft:sulfur_spike[thickness=tip,
      vertical_direction=down,waterlogged=false]
      [minecraft:sulfur_spike from chunk (78, 47)]

  zx       cluster reads  minecraft:sulfur_spike   -> column abandoned, 0 draws
  default  cluster reads  minecraft:air            -> column scanned, draws spent
```

**That is the RNG stream shift.** x 1247 is in chunk 77 while the writer is
chunk (78,47), and that is legitimate in principle: `sulfur_spike`'s placement
chain ends in `random_offset` with `xz_spread` a `clamped_normal(mean 0,
deviation 3, min −10, max 10)`, so a spike from (78,47) may legally land ten
blocks to −x. Under `zx` that write lands *before* (77,48) decorates; under
the x-major raster it lands after, and the cluster never sees it. Each column
deleted this way costs the cluster its per-column draws, which is the
1,081-against-2,721 shift §21 measured, and everything downstream in (77,48)
moves with it.

**And the row is a covering failure, not a placement error.** Across all four
chunks, in the whole traced box, **nothing in decoration writes `cinnabar` at
all** — 0 of 624 in-box writes under `zx`, 0 of 757 under `default`. Cinnabar
is already in the world when decoration starts, from the noise stage. So
`sulfur -> cinnabar` can only ever mean *vanilla covered this block with
sulfur and our reshaped cluster left it exposed*. No amount of reading the
cinnabar-writing code was ever going to explain this row, because there is no
such code in this stage.

### What this does NOT establish — the unadjudicated part, stated plainly

Whether **vanilla** puts a spike at (1247,24,766) is **not measured and cannot
be measured from anything this project currently holds.** That block is at
z=766, i.e. chunk z=**47**, outside the scored bbox `48 48 80 80` and outside
all sixteen r=16 references. Nothing adjudicates it.

The argument that it is ours rather than vanilla's is an **inference, and it is
recorded as one**: if vanilla's cluster also abandoned that column, vanilla
would carry the same stream shift and the `zx` charge would be near zero
rather than 312. It is not, so vanilla's world at (1247,24,766) must have been
empty-or-water when its (77,48) cluster scanned. That chain is only as strong
as §21's attribution of the 312 to this cluster's stream, which is measured —
but the final step is reasoning, not a reading of vanilla.

**Closing this needs a new reference covering z=47, in a new directory, never
a regeneration of an existing one — and rule 6 needs it twinned, so two
vanilla runs, not one.** Until then the correct statement is: the mechanism is
localised to one block and one feature, and the defect is not yet attributed
to a specific arm of `sulfur_spike`'s placement chain.

### The `isWaterAt` fix, and the poison control that says the window cannot exercise it

Read from the pinned jar: `LevelReader#isWaterAt` is
`getFluidState(pos).is(FluidTags.WATER)`, and
`data/minecraft/tags/fluid/water.json` holds **`minecraft:water` and
`minecraft:flowing_water`**. Our tree had that predicate written three times
and they did not agree:

```text
  speleothem_cluster.rs  canPlacePool path      water + flowing_water   right
  stage/decorate.rs      SpeleothemWorld        water only              WRONG
  simple_block.rs        SimpleBlockWorld       water only              WRONG
```

Both wrong ones are live. The second reaches `growSpeleothem`'s per-block
`waterlogged`, so a speleothem crossing *flowing* water was left dry while a
pool two blocks away was refused correctly, in the same feature, from the same
fluid. The third reaches `copy_waterlogged_from`, which is
`DoublePlantBlock#copyWaterloggedFrom` and calls `LevelReader.isWaterAt` in the
bytecode. All three now share one `is_water_fluid` in `feature/speleothem.rs`.

**There is a fourth `is_water_at` in `stage/decorate.rs` and it is CORRECT as
the narrow one. Do not widen it.** It feeds the freeze path, and
`Biome.shouldFreeze` is `getFluidState(pos).is(Fluids.WATER)` — fluid
*identity* against the source fluid, not `is(FluidTags.WATER)`. A
`flowing_water` state's type is `Fluids.FLOWING_WATER`, so vanilla answers
false there. Four methods with one signature, three of one kind and one of
another; unifying them on sight is a regression and this was checked against
the bytecode rather than assumed.

**The poison control: `ci/waterlog-poison-control.sh`, two dumps of ours
differing only by a revert of the fix commit.**

```text
  seed 12345, r=16, centre (64,64), RUSTMC_DECORATE_ORDER=zx,
  RUSTMC_WORKERS=1, 4 region files each, box threadripper

  pc-fixed  vs  pc-reverted     IDENTICAL   (diff -rq, exit 0)
```

**So the fix is byte-inert on this window: the delta is 0, in either
direction, over the identical position set.** That is the honest reading and
it must not be dressed up — the `sulfur -> cinnabar` row is **unchanged at
312 against a binding floor of 3**, and that baseline is the previous agent's,
measured on tip `8c7e024c`, not re-taken here.

A gate a window cannot exercise proves nothing, and this window cannot
exercise this fix. What the control does establish is that it fires *correctly*
rather than vacuously: each arm was required to produce region files, `diff`'s
exit code was read rather than its output length, and two independently built
and dumped runs of the reverted arm are themselves byte-identical, so the
IDENTICAL verdict is a real tie and not two failures agreeing.

> **The first version of this control reported `FIRES` off its own failure,
> and that is worth more than the fix it was testing.** It wrote both dumps
> *inside* the tree it rsyncs with `--delete`, so the second arm's sync
> deleted the first arm's dump; `diff` then wrote `No such file or directory`
> into the result file, and the verdict was "is that file non-empty". A
> control that manufactures a positive result from a missing input is the
> exact defect class this project keeps paying for, reproduced inside the
> instrument built to catch it. Both are fixed: dumps live outside the rsync
> target, `diff`'s exit status decides (0 identical, 1 differing, >1 refuse),
> and an arm with no region files is refused rather than compared.

#### The whole-branch comparison, which is the figure to quote

The per-commit control above isolates one fix. This one asks the blunter
question — **does anything on this branch move a block on this window?**

```text
  seed 12345, r=16, centre (64,64), RUSTMC_DECORATE_ORDER=zx,
  RUSTMC_WORKERS=1, 4 region files each side, box threadripper

  pc-base (a17daf5d, the branch point)  vs  pc-fixed  (instrument only)
      diff -rq exit 0 — IDENTICAL
  pc-base (a17daf5d, the branch point)  vs  pc-final  (instrument + both
                                                      isWaterAt fixes)
      diff -rq exit 0 — IDENTICAL
```

Two things follow, and the first matters more than the second.

**`RUSTMC_TRACE_READS` is output-inert as a measurement, not as an argument.**
It is inserted into `Canvas::block_at`, the hottest read path in the
generator, and restructures that method's control flow. Had it perturbed
anything, every trace taken today — including the read-position diff that
located the mechanism at (1247,24,766) — would have been measuring the
instrument. The first comparison rules that out on the same window the
mechanism was found on.

**The delta from this branch is 0**, byte-exact, in either direction, over the
identical position set. `sulfur -> cinnabar` is therefore **unchanged at 312
against a binding floor of 3** — a figure measured by the previous agent on
tip `8c7e024c` and *not* re-taken here; what is measured here is that this
branch does not move it.
## 22. The seed-12345 window on a tip carrying BOTH open fixes, and the lake that iced its own footprint — 4,140 -> 3,113 (2026-08-10)

*(Branch `worldgen-12345-next-items`. **Tip measured: `worldgen-carver-topmaterial`
c1654bcc plus a cherry-pick of the spruce canopy fix `3262cf3c`, plus the fix in
§22.4 — NOT `main` 21e25c48**, which had not been cut when these dumps were
taken. Seed 12345, overworld, r=16, centre chunk (64,64), 33x33 = 1,089 chunks,
bbox 48 48 80 80, y full, 107,053,056 blocks, no mask, VANILLA FIRST, sixteen
stamped references `gen-wide-12345-r16-ow-{c..r}`, `gen_order=rows`,
`dump_order=zx` read off each reference's own `HARVEST-ORDER`, leave-one-out
N=15, `RUSTMC_WORKERS=1`, Threadripper. `ci/next-12345-measure.sh` re-takes it.)*

### 22.1 The brief's base branch did not contain what the brief said it did

The brief opening this item said `worldgen-carver-topmaterial` c1654bcc "is the
highest tip and contains the spruce fix, the per-block floor tables both
directions, and this morning's carver `topMaterial` close". It contains the
second and third and **not the first**. `worldgen-spruce-leaves` and
`worldgen-carver-topmaterial` are two lines off a common ancestor `1b2994a6`,
each of which ports `IcebergFeature` separately; neither is an ancestor of the
other. `git merge-base --is-ancestor 3262cf3c c1654bcc` is false.

This is not bookkeeping, because **the two lines' published numbers were taken
against different bases and neither predicts a tip carrying both**: the spruce
line measured 7,490 -> 4,111 from a base that includes a blue-ice re-take, and
the carver line measured 7,940 -> 7,704 from one that does not. The tip here was
built by cherry-picking `3262cf3c` — two source files, no tooling dependency —
rather than by merging the two doc-heavy lines, which conflict over ~3,200 lines
of `docs/` and `ci/figures.toml` and are a restack somebody else is doing.

### 22.2 The re-measured charge, and the two rows that a marginal would have promoted

| | charge | exactness | floor (N=15, n=16) | margin on max / median |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| tip, both fixes | 4,140 | 99.996133% | 141..2,241, median 590 | 1.85x / 7.02x |
| with §22.4 | **3,113** | **99.997092%** | unchanged | 1.39x / 5.28x |
| delta | **-1,027** | | | |

**Fixed 1,027, newly charged 0**, taken as the set difference of the two N-way
intersections rather than as a difference of totals. The floor did not move
because it is a property of the reference ensemble and this change did not touch
it. As in §18.8's re-take, **the percentage improved and the resolving power
fell** — a smaller charge against a fixed floor is a narrower margin, and saying
so is the honest form.

`ci/next-12345-joint.py` keys the charge on the **ordered pair**. That matters
here for a reason the aggregate cannot show: the two rows a marginal ranking
would have put near the top are **not items at all**, because `air` carries a
large floor in both directions.

| row | count | miss floor | surplus floor | **binding** | ratio |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `packed_ice -> air` | 400 | 31 | **406** | 406 | **0.99x — under** |
| `air -> packed_ice` | 310 | **579** | 36 | 579 | **0.53x — under** |

### 22.3 The item: a lake iced its whole footprint, not its water

`ci/next-12345-joint.py` named the suspect before any class was opened (rule 9).
Every `-> ice` row was **exactly 1.00 blocks deep in every one of its columns** —
a flat sheet, not a volume — at **one y per chunk** varying between chunks,
across ~20 chunks, with a chunk-border share of 21..23% against 12.5% for a
uniform scatter. Chunk (60,67) held 131 of them, all at y=-11, over `deepslate`
(51), `cave_air` (48), `air` (24), `sulfur` (7) and `tuff` (1) alike. A single
flat plane that does not care what it overwrites is not a lake body and not a
freeze of the world surface.

From the 26.2 bytecode, `LakeFeature#place` offsets 1246..1347: the ice pass
walks the **entire 16x16 footprint at one fixed y**, `origin.offset(i, 4, j)`,
and never consults the lake's own shape mask. **Ours does the same, and vanilla
does too** — so the shape was never the difference. The only things standing
between that plane and the world are `Biome#shouldFreeze(level, pos, false)` and
`config.canReplaceWithAirOrFluid`.

`Biome#shouldFreeze` is five tests (offsets 0..89) and
`stage/decorate.rs`'s `LakeWorld` impl had **one**:

```text
   0  warmEnoughToRain(pos, seaLevel)                 -> false     WE HAD THIS
  16  !isInsideBuildHeight(pos.getY())                -> false     missing
  29  getBrightness(LightLayer.BLOCK, pos) >= 10      -> false     always taken
  53  !getFluidState(pos).is(Fluids.WATER)            -> false     missing
  73  !(getBlockState(pos).getBlock() instanceof
        LiquidBlock)                                  -> false     missing
  84  mustBeAtEdge == false                           -> TRUE
```

The two fluid tests are what kept the plane inside the lake. Without them a
water lake in a cold biome laid a one-block sheet of `ice` across its whole
footprint. `Fluids.WATER` is the **source** fluid, so flowing water fails, and
`instanceof LiquidBlock` excludes a waterlogged block — which has a water fluid
state and is not a water block; together they are why the port asks for
`minecraft:water` at `level=0` and not for anything merely wet.

**The lake that reaches this underground is 26.2's `sulfur_pool`** — fluid
water, barrier `minecraft:sulfur` — which is why the ice landed in the same
chunks as this window's sulfur rows. The lava lakes were already gated out one
level up by `fluid_is_water`, which was correct and is not the defect.

### 22.4 Controls

* **The poison control fires.** Reverting *only* the water arm and leaving the
  build-height arm in place returns the charge to 4,140 — the pre-fix figure to
  the block, with newly-charged 0 in that direction too. That distinguishes
  "this predicate did the work" from "the predicate exists", and it proves the
  arm is routed through the measured path rather than being dead code a green
  number would have flattered.
* **The reused reference matrix is controlled, not assumed.** A
  `<r1>-vs-<r2>.pos` for two vanilla references is a function of the two
  references only, so the ref-vs-ref half of the 342-diff matrix was reused
  rather than re-derived. That is an argument and not a measurement, so
  `ci/next-12345-measure.sh control` re-derives `c-vs-d` from the references and
  `cmp`s it against the reused copy; it is byte-identical, and the stage exits 3
  rather than letting anything stand on it if it is not.
* **The order was read off the stamps, not asserted** (ORACLE rule 30), and the
  dump's own `decorate-order:` line is checked to report the order it was asked
  for — until 2026-08-10 an unrecognised name ran the default raster and still
  printed the name it had not used.

### 22.5 Order stability, measured on both sides and on both tips

Dumped again x-major and scored against the same sixteen references:

| tip | matched `zx` | x-major | stone-variant rows in x-major |
|---|---|---|---|
| before the fix | 4,140 | 9,777 | 5,669 |
| after the fix | **3,113** | **8,729** | 5,669 |

The whole of the ~5,600 difference is stone-variant blob confusion
(`andesite`/`diorite`/`granite` in both directions, six rows), which is the
known order artefact and not any item here. **The `-> ice` rows this section
closed were order-stable** — 453 -> 466 and 284 -> 287, about 1..3% — which is
part of why they were safe to open. Read all of it with §17's warning: chunk
order is 6.7% of this window's residual and 83% of 20260722's, and neither
estimates the other.

**And one row is not merely order-unstable, it is order-INVERTED.** Measured on
the fixed tip, in both orders, over the same sixteen references:

| | matched `zx` | x-major |
|---|---|---|
| chunk (77,48) charged | **387** | **0** |
| positions where vanilla is `sulfur` | **406** | **0** |
| positions where we placed `cinnabar` | **312** | **0** |

The x-major dump is not short a chunk — it charges 8,729 overall — it is
*exactly right* in (77,48), where the matched-order dump is 387 wrong. The
global preference for `zx` is not in doubt (3,113 against 8,729), so this is not
an argument for changing the order; it is a statement about where the suspect
is. A feature whose output flips with the traversal is reading state a
neighbouring chunk's decoration writes, and a `lake` is exactly that shape — its
footprint is anchored at an origin that straddles chunk borders. **So the first
move on item 3 in §22.6 is an order decomposition, not a bytecode read of how
`cinnabar` is chosen** (`ci/decorate-order-intersect.sh`,
`ci/order-charge-sweep.py`; AGENT-BRIEF, "decompose a residual by order before
opening any investigation on it"). Two agents have each lost a turn to a lump
that the intersection deletes in one command.

### 22.6 The next three items, ranked by what clears its BINDING floor

A miss floor is keyed on the vanilla spelling, a surplus floor needs the
`[[placed]]` table, and the binding floor is the larger. All three rows below
were looked up through the full-state key and **none of the six floors reads
zero**, so none of them is the 2026-08-09 missed-lookup artefact.

| # | row | count | miss floor | surplus floor | **binding** | ratio |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `water[level=0] -> packed_ice` | 493 | 39 | 36 | **39** | **12.6x** |
| 2 | `packed_ice -> water[level=0]` | 419 | 31 | 65 | **65** | **6.4x** |
| 3 | `sulfur -> cinnabar` | 312 | 3 | 3 | **3** | **104x** |

**Items 1 and 2 are one suspect, and it is the iceberg.** Both sit at y 46..62
— sea level — 2.1..2.6 blocks deep per column, in 17 and 25 chunks, and both
