rather than an inference.** `608`'s measured click and `607`'s are the *same
action on the same rig* — a `container_click` on a chest menu. Nothing in the
actions, the rig, the `observe` list or the recording separates them, because
the difference is not in what the scenario does but in what it claims, and
that is information only the author has. A harness that guessed would be
inventing the check's subject, which is the one thing this corpus never does.

**The polarity is the safety argument.** Absent means "must speak". Every
scenario written before the field existed is therefore opted in to the strict
reading, and a new check that forgets it goes red, never green: an omission
cannot manufacture a pass. That is the opposite failure direction from the
hand-written manifest `compare::PROJECTION_SOURCES` warns about, where the
branch that adds the tenth axis is the branch that forgets to declare it.

And it exempts nothing from comparison. A `silent` scenario still compares its
measured step on both sides; a subject that speaks there still `Diverge`s,
which is what keeps the four able to fail. All the declaration suppresses is
`Mute`.

### The poison — `ci/mute-falsify.py`

A control that cannot fire is not a control, and this section is *about* that
sentence. The script re-compares recordings that already exist, in seconds,
with no server started. Against the `rustmc-cov2` pair (2026-08-10 13:16, 87
scenarios of this corpus), **16 cases, all green**:

| case | what is poisoned | required |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | nothing — the baseline | `mute` = `{607}` and nothing else |
| 2 | a victim's last step silenced in **both** traces (3 victims) | that victim goes `mute` |
| 3 | " | **no other scenario's verdict moves** |
| 4 | `measured_step = "silent"` stripped from each of the four | each goes `mute` |
| 5 | the declaration added to a poisoned victim | back to its baseline verdict |
| 6 | a victim's **step 0** silenced instead of its last | no new `mute` anywhere |

**Case 4 is the one that matters.** Without it, "the four stay green" is
indistinguishable from "the verdict never fires". With it, the four are green
because something is declared and true.

A false start worth recording: the first three victims the script chose were
one-step scenarios, and silencing their only step emptied the whole reference
projection — which is `vacuous`, decided inside `verdict_for` before `Mute` is
reached. That is the verdicts agreeing, not disagreeing, and it fixed the
selection rather than the verdict: a victim must keep speaking *outside* its
measured step, which is `607`'s shape and the only shape `Mute` is for. The
second false start was reading that condition off the raw trace instead of off
the comparator's own `reference_obs` / `measured_reference_obs`, and a
scenario whose earlier steps are loud on the wire and silent under its own
`observe` list duly slipped through. Only the projection can predict the
projection.

### Two smaller things the same reasoning forced

* **The measured step is counted before `collapse_repeats`.** That filter's
  stated job is to forget which step an observation landed in, because where a
  free-running timer's edge falls against a step boundary is a property of
  this harness's sampling. A filter designed to forget step attribution must
  never be what decides that a step was silent — it would report a silence its
  own disclaimer had created. Every container check in the corpus collapses
  `block_event`, so the day one of them adds it to `observe` this ordering is
  what stands between that edit and a corpus-wide false `mute`.
* **A scenario with no `[[step]]` is refused at load.** It has no measured
  step for the verdict to be about, and refusing makes "every scenario has a
  measured step" a fact the comparator may rely on rather than a coincidence
  it re-checks.

### `607` itself

Rewritten, and the bytecode says why the old one could never have worked.
`AbstractContainerMenu.doClick`'s `PICKUP_ALL` arm (`javap -p -c -constants`,
offsets 1611 onward) enters the gather at 1667 on three tests:

```text
  1641  carried.isEmpty()       -> ifne 1873   bail on an empty cursor
  1649  slot.hasItem()          -> ifeq 1667   EMPTY SLOT: straight to the sweep
  1657  slot.mayPickup(player)  -> ifne 1873   has a takeable item: bail
```

The old file clicked a slot that still held a matching, pickupable stone, so
1651 and 1661 were both true and 1664 jumped out of the arm. **The sweep runs
on a clicked slot that is empty** — which is exactly what a real double-click
produces, because the client sends `PICKUP` first, emptying the slot into the
cursor, and `PICKUP_ALL` against the slot it has just emptied. The old
sequence was one no client sends.

It now puts three stone into three chest slots, lifts one onto the cursor with
a plain `PICKUP` (leaving that slot empty), and `pickup_all`s the empty slot.
Three donors and not two: one donor cannot distinguish a gather from an
ordinary pickup. And it observes `packet:0x60` — a record with one component,
`ItemStack contents`, no window id, no state id, no sequence, so its digest is
a pure function of the cursor and there is no counter in it to flap.

### The run — what the corpus actually said, 2026-08-11

Everything above this line was written before the branch had a corpus verdict
of its own. This is that verdict, and one of the three acceptable answers about
`607` came back.

**The run.** `ci/t2-gameplay.sh` in `~/rustmc-verdict`, Threadripper,
2026-08-11, corpus **87** — the size `water-spreads-cascade` left in `main`
when it took 88 -> 87, counted from the run and not from a brief. Ports
:26584/:26585, picked by the script's own probe. Rebased onto
`doc-collision-class` (`1f8f3813`), which is `main` (`31e4615f`) plus the
five-branch stack of 23 commits.

```
CORPUS  declared  67/87 match   (15 diverge, 5 absent, 0 vacuous, 0 mute, 0 inert, 0 error)
CORPUS  strict    63/87 match   (22 diverge, 2 absent, 0 vacuous, 0 mute, 0 inert, 0 error)
```

**`0 mute` is the result, not the absence of one.** The corpus this branch
exists to fix reports zero unfalsifiable scenarios because the one it had was
fixed and the four that assert a silence declare it. `561`, `571` and `581`
came back `match`; `608` did not, for a reason that is not this verdict's and
is set out below.

**`607` CONVERTS.** `match` -> `diverge`, and the diverge is the gather:

```
at 13: vanilla {"kind":"container_set_slot","slot":1,"state":9,"window":1,...} step 8
       ours    {"kind":"packet:0x60","rest":"67414a774c80560f",...}            step 8
```

Vanilla empties donor slot 1 into the cursor. We answer with the cursor packet
whose digest is the same `67414a774c80560f` the old file spent its life
comparing against itself — the resync of an unchanged cursor. The check now
sees the thing it is named for and fails on it. `pickup_all` remains
unimplemented in `crates/rustmc-server/src/click.rs`; that is `click.rs`'s
item and this branch does not open it.

`ci/checks/gameplay-baseline.toml` therefore moves `607` **down**, `match` ->
`diverge`, which is the second deliberate downward move in that file after
`602`. A `match` no server behaviour earned is worse than an honest red.

### The control that separates this branch from the tree under it

**This branch changes no server code at all** — `git diff 1f8f3813..HEAD --
crates/` is empty — so the subject binary in that run is the stack tip's. The
comparator is the only thing this branch could have moved a verdict with, and
`compare::project` is untouched: the `Mute` arm is guarded on
`out.verdict == Verdict::Match` and can only relabel, never convict.

Measured rather than argued. The **stack-tip** comparator, built from
`1f8f3813` in `~/rustmc-verdict-base` and carrying no `mute` verdict at all,
was run over **the same two recordings**:

| comparator | projection | declared | diverge | absent |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| this branch | `proj:1e154ef8765b7525` | 67/87 | 15 | 5 |
| stack tip `1f8f3813` | `proj:b385528330fee646` | 67/87 | 15 | 5 |

**Identical.** The third verdict moved zero verdicts on this corpus. Whatever
`608` is, it is not this.

### `608` — a red this branch inherits and must not fix

> **RETRACTED 2026-08-11 — this whole subsection is wrong. See "The `608`
> finding above is wrong" at the end of this section.** It is left standing
> rather than deleted because the way it was wrong is the finding.


`container-click-with-an-out-of-range-slot-changes-nothing` came back
`diverge`, against a `match` baseline, and it is one of the four by-design
negative checks. That is the outcome Job 1 says must not happen — so it
matters exactly what happened, because the shape is nearly the shape of a
self-refuting deliverable.

It is not. The four are required to stay green **against the new verdict**,
and `608` is not `mute`; the census records `0 mute`. It diverges on wire
content at **step 0**, its rig step, three steps before the one it asserts a
silence at:

```
at 1: vanilla {"kind":"container_set_content","rest":"b4b699a3a20338fc","state":1,"window":1} step 0
      ours    {"kind":"container_set_content","rest":"8aac16faed2ec626","state":1,"window":1} step 0
```

Its declaration is still doing its job: the stack-tip comparator scores it
`diverge` on the same recordings, and `ci/mute-falsify.py` reports its case 4
UNRUNNABLE rather than failed, because `Mute` only relabels a `Match` and a
subject that is already diverging cannot exercise that case however well the
verdict works. Three of the four subjects remain and all three pass.

`ci/corpus-floor.sh` over six vanilla recordings including this run's puts
`608` in the **recorder-suspect** bucket, not the reference bucket: 5 of 15
pairs disagree and **0 of the 10 pairs inside the largest homogeneous subset
do**. Every disagreeing pair contains this run. The ensemble holds two
distinct recorders and this run is the only one taken on the stack tip, so the
reading is that the recorder moved under it, not that vanilla is
irreproducible there. That belongs to whoever gates the stack; it is not
`ci/checks/gameplay/607` and this branch owns nothing else in that directory.

### The floor is stale, and it was stale before this branch touched it

> **Partly retracted** — the recorder-suspect reading of `608` below is wrong
> for the same reason. The stale figures themselves stand.


`ci/checks/gameplay-floor.toml` declares `projection = "proj:58380c9e4cbeae81"`
and `scenarios = 88`. Both are wrong now, and the projection was already wrong
at the stack tip: `1f8f3813`'s own comparator is `proj:b385528330fee646`. The
floor's quotability is withdrawn by the tool, correctly, and re-stamping is not
this branch's to do.

What a re-stamp should say, measured here so the next agent does not have to
re-derive it — `ci/corpus-floor.sh` over six vanilla recordings
(`rustmc-verdict`, `-cov2`, `-blueice`, `-grassdirt2`, `-batch2`, `-coreend`),
2026-08-11:

* `scenarios` **87**, not 88;
* `floor` **8**, not 7 — but see the caveat below before writing that down;
* `projection` whatever the merged tip's `oracle-runner projection-id` prints,
  which is neither of the two ids above.

**Do not stamp the 8 from this ensemble.** It is heterogeneous — two recorders,
largest homogeneous subset 5 — and two of its eight members are `607` and
`608`, both recorder-suspect rather than reference. Worse for `607`
specifically: this branch *rewrote that scenario*, so comparing this run's
`607` against the other five compares two different experiments sharing a
name, which is not a flap. The honest floor for the merged tip needs a
homogeneous ensemble recorded on it. **Who stamps it: whoever merges the
stack, or a dedicated re-stamp item after it lands** — not a branch scoring
against another branch's recordings, and not this one.

### The `608` finding above is wrong, and the way it was wrong is the finding

**Retracted 2026-08-11, on the orchestrator's reading and then on bytes.** Two
independent readings of the stack tip carrying this branch are byte-identical
— `~/rustmc-stack` at `428ce367`, ports :26782/:26783, and this branch's own
run — so `608` is a **deterministic** disagreement, not recorder instability,
and ORACLE rule 6 is satisfied against it rather than for it. The
recorder-suspect bucket was the wrong reading.

**And the control that produced it was varying the wrong thing.** Holding the
recordings fixed and swapping the comparator proves only that the comparator
is innocent. It cannot see a change that lands in the *recordings*, and that is
where this branch's change landed. The stack tip's gate scores `608` `match`
on **its own** recordings and this branch's comparator scores it `diverge` on
**these** — same verdict logic, different traces.

`rest` settles it: it is a digest **baked into the trace by the recorder**, not
computed by the comparator at compare time (`{"kind":"container_set_content",
"fields":{"rest":"…","state":1,"window":15}}` is the whole of what is stored).
So no projection and no verdict could have moved it. `compare::project` is
untouched on this branch and the `Mute` arm only ever relabels a `Match`.

**The bytes, over every gameplay recording on the box (39 pairs, 2026-08-11):**

| side | old `607` (`max step 6`) | rewritten `607` (`max step 8`) |
|---|---|---|
| vanilla | `8aac16faed2ec626` — **37 of 37** | `b4b699a3a20338fc` — 2 of 2 |
| ours | `8aac16faed2ec626` | `8aac16faed2ec626` — **39 of 39** |

Corpus sizes 86, 87, 88, 92 and 93 are all in that left column, so the value is
not a function of the corpus. It is a function of one thing: whether `607` is
the old file or the new one.

**Read the second row.** *Our* server's `608` never moves — not once in 39
recordings. **The rewritten `607` moved vanilla and left us where we were.**
That is not a defect in the instrument; it is the defect `607` now reports,
arriving one scenario later.

### The mechanism, and why it is the 552/553 lesson again

`607` sorts immediately before `608` and every scenario in a run shares one
connection — `record.rs` logs in once and reuses the party, which is why that
file already resets position, held keys and any open menu before each
scenario, and says in its own comment that **the corpus must not be
order-dependent**.

The chain:

1. `607`'s measured `PICKUP_ALL` gathers three stone onto the cursor **on
   vanilla**. On this server `do_click` does not implement `PICKUP_ALL`, so the
   cursor keeps the one stone the preceding `PICKUP` put there. That
   asymmetry is `607`'s entire finding and it is correct.
2. The per-scenario reset closes the open menu. Vanilla's
   `doCloseContainer` runs `removed`, which **drops whatever is carried**. We
   do not — which is not a new defect either: it is
   `611-closing-a-crafting-table-returns-the-grid-contents`, already baselined
   `diverge`, and `611` fails with the identical signature
   (`container_set_content`, step 0, differing `rest`).
3. `608` opens a chest. Its `container_set_content` carries the player
   inventory, vanilla's now three stone lighter and ours not, and the digests
   part.

So the answer to "did the projection change, or is our step-0 content really
different" is **neither, and both are the wrong question**: our step-0 content
is really different, but it did not become different at `608` and it was never
being projected away. `608` is reading `607`'s defect through a reset that
depends on the server under test behaving correctly. **A reset that only works
if the thing being measured is already right is not a reset** — the same shape
as the check that could not fail, one level up, which is the class this whole
section is about.

`record.rs` has been here before and recorded the ruling: `552` sneaks, `553`
does not, and run together vanilla produced identical traces for both because
`552` left the sneak flag set. The comment on that fix says explicitly that
fixing it *in the scenario* "is the version that works until somebody writes
the next scenario, so it is fixed here" — in the recorder. That precedent
rules out every scenario-local dodge available to `607`: renumbering it past
`608` only moves the leak to whatever sorts next, and no rig ending in a
`PICKUP_ALL` can leave both sides carrying the same thing, because carrying
different things **is the measurement**.

### What closes it, and why this branch is not the one to close it

The fix is a **server-independent inventory reset in `record.rs`**, beside the
position and sneak resets that are already there — the player's carried item
and hotbar restored from the client rather than inherited from whatever the
last scenario's server did with them. `record.rs` is the recorder and is not
part of the projection identity, so it moves no verdict by construction.

It is not done here for one reason: **it changes every recording in the
corpus, so validating it costs a full corpus run**, and the box is running the
stack's gate. Landing an unvalidated recorder change would be exactly the
"commit message is not evidence" failure this project has already paid for.

Until it lands, `608`'s red is honest and is left red. **Its baseline is
deliberately not moved** — recording `diverge` there would publish `607`'s
defect under `608`'s name and would flip back the day `PICKUP_ALL` or
drop-on-close is implemented, which is a baseline that describes the corpus's
alphabetical order rather than the server.

## 30. A window cannot be widened past what was generated, and this pool was never generated as wide as it looks (2026-08-11)

Branch `sulfur-cinnabar-r19`, off `main` **31e4615f**, Threadripper. Written to
close `sulfur -> cinnabar`, which stood at **312 against a binding floor of 3**
(104x) on seed 12345, r=16 overworld, bbox `48 48 80 80`, pool 16, N=15. The
brief for it asked for a re-diff of the existing references on `45 45 83 83`,
on the reasoning that each reference holds `r.1.1 .. r.2.2.mca` and therefore
already covers chunks 32..95 in both axes.

**It does not, a region file is not a chunk, and the re-diff as specified would
have returned the number it started with.**

### 30.1 What the pool actually holds

44 directories match `gen-wide-12345-r16-ow-*`. Two (`a`, `b`) carry no
`HARVEST-ORDER` at all and two (`rr-a`, `rr-b`) are `gen_order=rows_rev`, so
the usable one-order pool is **40**, which is the set `ci/refs-12345-widen2.sh`
already names. Reading the chunk statuses back out of the region files
(`ci/ref-chunk-extent.py`, new here):

```text
    every one of the 44 is minecraft:full over chunks 46..82 and no further
    the ring at x or z in {45,83} is minecraft:initialize_light
    all 44 share the IDENTICAL 152-chunk non-full set
    largest full square centred (64,64):  r=18,  window 46 46 82 82
```

`gen-vanilla-region.sh 12345 16` decorates 45..83 and completes 46..82: it
generates a ring beyond what it finishes, because that is what finishing the
rest requires. So the r=19 window is not one the references can be scored on,
and the widest they admit is **r=18**.

### 30.2 Why the re-diff would have been silent rather than wrong

`region-differ` drops, without a word, every chunk that is absent from either
input (`tools/region-differ/src/main.rs`, the `b.contains_key` filter) and
every chunk whose status is not `minecraft:full`. Our own charge dump is
tighter than the references: `worldgen-dump full 12345 16` prints
`chunks=1369 first=(46,46) last=(82,82)` and then `wrote 1089 chunks`, so it
**decorates a 2-chunk margin and writes only 48..80**, and 432 of the r=19
window's 1,521 chunks are absent from our side entirely.

A diff of the existing pair on `45 45 83 83` therefore scores exactly the
chunks it scored on `48 48 80 80`, byte for byte, and reports no change. This
is the shape the project keeps paying for: not a wrong number, a number that
cannot move, presented as a widened measurement. `ci/refs-12345-widen2.sh` now
refuses it — `WIDEN2_BBOX` is a parameter defaulted to the historical window,
the window is stamped into `$OUT/WINDOW` so two windows cannot share a matrix,
reused pair and charge diffs are taken only at the window and dump they were
computed at, and every reference plus our dump must be `minecraft:full` across
the window before a diff is taken.

### 30.3 DECORATION_MARGIN of 2 does not converge, measured three ways

Widening our own generated extent moves blocks inside the window we already
publish. Three dumps at the same tip, same seed, same centre, all
`RUSTMC_WORKERS=1 RUSTMC_DECORATE_ORDER=zx`:

```text
    r=16   decorates 46..82   writes 48..80
    r=18   decorates 44..84   writes 46..82
    r=20   decorates 42..86   writes 44..84

    r=16 against r=18, over 48 48 80 80        23 blocks, ALL in chunk (48,54)
    r=18 against r=20, over 46 46 82 82     2,713 blocks, in chunks x=46 and
                                            x=47, plus 3 at (79,46)
```

The 23 are `snow[layers=1]`, `water -> ice`, `spruce_leaves` and
`grass_block[snowy]` — read-then-write surface decorations, the same family
§18.2 found the floor is made of. So contamination propagates **at least three
chunks inward** from the outermost decorated ring, not one: each contaminated
ring contaminates its neighbour when that neighbour decorates and reads it.

`crates/rustmc-server/tests/tiled_world.rs::a_chunk_does_not_depend_on_how_big_
the_area_around_it_was` asserts precisely what these numbers contradict, and it
passes. It compares chunk (0,0) at tile radius 0, 1 and 2 — distances 2, 3 and
4 from the outermost decorated ring, so it is pointed at the right thing — but
at one chunk of one seed where nothing straddles the border. The effect is rare
and localised (23 blocks in one of the 33 chunks of a rim column), so a
single-chunk probe misses it by construction. **This is a gate that cannot fail
at the size it is run**, and it is a separate item; nothing here changes the
margin.

### 30.4 What this leaves for a widened window

Our side is fixable by dumping wider — chunk (77,47) is identical between the
r=18 and r=20 dumps, so it is converged from r=20 on. The references are not:
they were all harvested at r=16, their outermost decorated ring is 45, and
chunk (77,47) sits two rings inside it. The contamination is a deterministic
function of the generation extent and is therefore **identical in all forty
references**, which means the leave-one-out innocent floor cannot see it: it is
a systematic the instrument is blind to, not noise the pool averages out.

Two new references at r=18 (`gen-wide-12345-r18-ow-x1`, `-x2`, decorating
43..85, which puts chunk (77,47) four rings in) are the control that settles
it, and rule 6 needs two rather than one. Until they are read, no charge on
chunk (77,47) is quotable.

### 30.5 The pool, not the window, is what moved this row — and it moved it to zero

While the widened window was being made possible, the cheaper question turned
out to be the one that mattered. The published `312 / binding floor 3 / 104x`
was taken at **pool 16, N=15**; the pool has been 40 since §25 and the row had
never been re-read on it. `ci/chunk-charge-floor.py` (new here) adjudicates one
chunk with its own floor beside it, which neither `floor-census.py` nor
`confusion-pair-census.py` can do, and it is the right denominator by §18.6 —
a charge belongs to its subset as a floor belongs to its pair.

Pool 40 stamped `gen_order=rows` references, N=39 leave-one-out, window
`48 48 80 80`, our r=16 dump at tip 31e4615f, **vanilla first**:

```text
  chunk     charge   of which sulfur->cinnabar   innocent floor  min/med/MAX
  (77,48)      387                        272                     0 / 0 / 0
  (78,48)       38                         25                     0 / 0 / 0
  (79,48)      186                          8                     0 / 0 / 0
  (79,49)      312                          6                     0 / 0 / 0
  (80,50)        5                          1                     0 / 0 / 0
                                        -----
                                           312
```

The row reproduces to the block, and **every one of its 312 positions has a
unanimous vanilla spelling across all forty references**. The binding floor on
each of those chunks is 0 at pool 40 — and at pool 16 and pool 5 alike, so this
is not the pool buying the zero, it is those chunks being places where vanilla
is block-for-block deterministic across every reference this project owns.

**Unanimity is not the argument, and must not be read as one.** §23.5 is
unchanged: an innocent unmodified vanilla run scores up to 2,169 unanimous on
this window, and `ci/admissible-floor.py` and `ci/window-resolving-power.py`
would have convicted 1,327 positions for free on exactly that reasoning. What
is quoted above is not unanimity, it is the leave-one-out innocent charge —
substitute a real vanilla run for our dump and read what the instrument charges
it. On these chunks it charges zero.

Controls, because rule 7 bites hardest when a measurement clears everything:

```text
  chunks (64,64), (70,70), (55,60)        our charge 0        floor 0
  chunk  (56,69)   pool 5                 our charge 0        floor MAX 350
  chunk  (56,69)   pool 40                our charge 0        floor MAX  57
```

The instrument reports a large floor where one exists and watches it fall with
the pool, so the zeros are a property of the sulfur chunks rather than of the
tool. `ci/chunk-charge-floor.py --selftest` carries the two failures that
matter as fixtures: a pair whose floor lives under the *other* key (§18.3 —
ours writes cinnabar where the references say sulfur and the innocent run
writes water, so the miss floor of `sulfur` is 1 while the pair floor is 0),
and a stated block that must not be pooled with its bare name.

**So the ratio is not 104x.** It is 312 against a measured floor of 0 on the
chunks that carry it, and the row was never as close to its floor as the
aggregate window figure made it look. The 3 came from pooling the whole
window's `sulfur` miss and `cinnabar` surplus at N=15; localised to the chunks
the charge actually occupies, and at the pool the project has owned since
2026-08-10, there is nothing under it.

### 30.6 The widened window, and why most of what it adds is its own rim

Pool 40, N=39, window `46 46 82 82`, our side the **r=20** dump (written extent
44..84, so the window is two rings inside our own outermost written chunk and
chunk (77,47) is identical between the r=18 and r=20 dumps), vanilla first:

```text
    charge over 46 46 82 82                              10,697
    charge over 48 48 80 80 (same pool, §25)              1,279

    sulfur -> cinnabar, by chunk
      (77,48) 272   (74,46) 253   (73,46) 145   (79,46)  99   (76,46) 99
      (77,47)  35   (75,46)  28   (78,48)  25   (79,48)   8   (79,49)  6
      (78,47)   3   (80,50)   1                              total    974
```

**662 of the 974 are new, and 624 of those 662 are in the single ring z=46** —
the outermost written ring of the widened window, one chunk inside the
references' outermost decorated ring. The other top rows behave the same way:
`air -> snow[layers=1]` 854, `snow -> air` 563, `water -> bubble_column` 477,
`air -> spruce_leaves` 464 and 446, which is §30.3's read-then-write family
again and in the same place.

So the widened window is **not quotable as a window**. Widening bought 9,418
blocks of charge and the great majority of it is the instrument measuring its
own margin, exactly as `DECORATION_MARGIN` was supposed to prevent and
§30.3 shows it does not at this width. This is ORACLE rule 14 arriving as a
bill rather than as a caveat: the answer had not stopped changing, so the wider
number is not the better one.

What survives is the per-chunk reading, which is the right denominator anyway:

```text
  chunk     charge   sulfur->cinnabar   innocent floor min/med/MAX   ring
  (77,47)       49                 35                   0 / 0 / 0    z=47
  (78,47)       12                  3                   0 / 0 / 0    z=47
  (76,47)        4                  0                   0 / 0 / 0    z=47
```

Chunk (76,47) charges 4 and all four are `granite -> diorite`: the z=47 ring is
not uniformly charged, and (77,47) is not a rim effect smeared along a row. It
sits directly north of (77,48), which carries 272 of the original 312, and it
carries the same block pair. The natural reading is one body crossing a chunk
border, which is what the item said from the start — but one ring further out
than the window could see, and **not** at (1247,24,766).

### 30.7 The named position is not a mismatch

The diagnosis this brief inherited names `(1247,24,766)` — a cross-border
`sulfur_spike` written by chunk (78,47), landing before (77,48) decorates,
failing `Column.scan`'s `is_empty_or_water` guard and abandoning the column
before it spends a draw. Scored at last:

```text
    chunk (77,47), y=24, census across six references and ours
      references c,d,e,f,g,h    56 sulfur   51 cinnabar   3 sulfur_spike  (all six identical)
      ours                      54 sulfur   53 cinnabar   3 sulfur_spike

    chunk (77,47), y=24, ours against reference c, positions
      1246 24 759   air                             -> sulfur_spike[thickness=middle,...]
      1237 24 762   sulfur                          -> cinnabar
      1238 24 764   sulfur_spike[thickness=frustum] -> air
      1235 24 767   sulfur                          -> cinnabar

    the same against reference d
      1237 24 762   sulfur -> cinnabar
      1235 24 767   sulfur -> cinnabar
```

**We agree with vanilla at (1247,24,766).** It is not charged against any
reference, so no part of the 312 or of chunk (77,47)'s 49 is there. Our
`sulfur_spike` count in that chunk at y=24 is 3, which is what all six
references have, so "our spike is one vanilla does not place" is false at that
y — the two spike *positions* that do differ against reference c do not differ
against reference d, which puts them inside vanilla's own run-to-run
variation, and the census confirms it: references disagree with each other
about the spike total in that chunk (58 in c and e, 29 in d, f and g).

The two `sulfur -> cinnabar` misses that ARE common to both references sit at
**(1237,24,762)** and **(1235,24,767)**. The covering failure is real and it is
where the census puts it; the mechanism narrated around (1247,24,766) is not
supported by the blocks.

## 31. `DECORATION_MARGIN` converges at no width, and the gate that said otherwise was one seed wide (2026-08-12)

Branch `worldgen-decoration-margin`, off `main` **d049ef5a**, Threadripper.
§30.3 measured that widening our own generated extent moves blocks inside the
window we publish, said so, and then said the thing that made this an item:
`crates/rustmc-server/tests/tiled_world.rs::a_chunk_does_not_depend_on_how_big_
the_area_around_it_was` **asserts the opposite and passes**. Nothing here
changes the generator. What changes is the gate, and what is measured is the
question the gate was asked and could not answer: is there a margin width that
converges?

There is not, and the reason is structural rather than a matter of finding a
big enough number. Both halves below are measurement.

### 31.1 §30.3 reproduces, and so does the hazard §30.2 warned about

The generator is byte-identical between `31e4615f`, where §30.3 was measured,
and this branch's base: `git diff 31e4615f..d049ef5a -- crates/rustmc-worldgen/`
is empty, and the only change under `crates/rustmc-server/src/world.rs` is
comment text. So §30.3's three dumps are dumps of this tip's generator, and
they were re-diffed rather than re-taken. **Both sides of every diff here are
ours** — this is our extent against our extent, not a parity figure — so the
vanilla-first rule has no sign to fix; the narrower extent is named first
throughout.

```text
  r=16 vs r=18  over 48 48 80 80      23 mismatches   chunks=1089 (a=1089 b=1369)
                                      worst chunk (48,54): 23
  r=18 vs r=20  over 46 46 82 82   2,713 mismatches   chunks=1369 (a=1369 b=1681)
```

§30.3 reproduces to the block.

**And the third diff is the control, because §30.2's hazard had never been
witnessed firing.** The same r=16 dump against the same r=18 dump, over the
*wider* window it does not cover:

```text
  r=16 vs r=18  over 46 46 82 82      23 mismatches   chunks=1089 (a=1089 b=1369)
```

Identical to the narrower window, line for line — the mismatch count, the
worst chunk, the per-block breakdown and the `EXACTNESS` percentage all come
back unchanged, which is what makes it convincing and is exactly the trap.
(That percentage is deliberately not repeated here: it is an our-against-ours
extent comparison, not an exactness claim about anything, and a four-decimal
number in this document is a figure a reader may act on. `ci/figures.toml`
has nothing to register.)
280 of that window's 1,369 chunks are absent from our r=16 dump and
`region-differ` dropped every one of them without a word. A widened window
returned the narrow window's number and called it a measurement. The only
thing that separates the two is the `a=1089` in a header nobody has to read.

The refusal that exists for this does fire, and it discriminates:

```text
  ci/ref-chunk-extent.py --window 46 46 82 82 <r16>/region
      REFUSED window 46 46 82 82: 280 of 1369 chunks are not minecraft:full
      statuses seen: <absent>                                        exit 3
  the same call on the r=18 and r=20 dumps                           exit 0
  the same call on all three at 48 48 80 80                          exit 0
```

So the instrument is sound and the discipline is the load-bearing part: run
the extent check on **both** sides before believing any figure taken on a
window you have widened. `region-differ` alone will not tell you.

### 31.2 The ladder, and the variable that actually governs it

`tools/worldgen-assembly/tests/probe_margin_context.rs`, which grew a
`RUSTMC_MARGIN_MAX_RADIUS` knob here because capping at radius 4 is why the
ladder's shape had never been read. Each rung generates a square, then the
next square out, and compares **every chunk the narrower run delivered** —
the part it claims is finished.

The governing variable is not the margin and not the radius, it is a chunk's
**depth below the outermost decorated ring**: `depth = radius + margin -
distance-from-centre`. A margin of *m* is exactly the depth of the delivered
square's rim, at every radius, which is the whole of §31.3. The centre chunk
of a radius-*r* tile sits at depth *r + m*, so the centre column of this
ladder — printed on every line now — reads off what each depth is worth:

```text
  seed        depth 2   3    4    5    6    7    8    9      (blocks moved by
  12345            40   56   41   29   33   33    8    0       chunk (0,0) when
  0                 2    0    0    0    0    0    0    0       the area grows
  20260722          0    0    0    0    0    0    0    0       by one ring)
```

Through `Through::Carvers` every one of those rungs is **0** at all three
seeds, out to a 10-ring context and 225 delivered chunks. Terrain is
per-chunk pure and the instability is decoration alone — the same control
this probe recorded in 2026-07-28 and the same answer.

**And the centre column is not one instrument's opinion.**
`crates/rustmc-server/tests/margin_probe.rs` asks the same question from the
other side of the server — `World::block_at` on a fully assembled `World`
rather than the assembly crate's canvas through `Through::Decoration` — and
at seed 12345 it returns **40, 56, 41, 29, 33** on the same five rungs. Two
instruments, two crates, one number. It also re-runs seed 0 at **2, 0, 0, 0,
0** where its own header records 24 to 334 blocks oscillating on 2026-07-28,
so that header is a stale reading of a generator that has since gained
icebergs, lakes and leaf litter — it is corrected in place rather than
deleted. The 2026-07-28 conclusion drawn from it, *"it oscillates rather than
converging"*, does not survive: today's seed-0 column settles immediately and
the top block at (8,8) is **74 at every context**, constant while hundreds of
blocks move underneath it. A column is a poor detector of a sparse effect,
which is the same mistake as one chunk and one seed, one dimension down.

The aggregate over the whole delivered square, which is what a tile actually
has to be right about:

```text
  seed     delivered chunks   1    9    25    49    81   121   169  225  289   361   441   529
  12345                      40 1397  3406  6501 11127 11797 17194  309   11  1005  5514  1937
  0                           2   81  2736  7383 14719 20217 45349 45145    -     -     -     -
  31415926                    0    0     0     0   833  1917  2916 2439    -     -     -     -
  20260722                    0    0     0     0     0     0     0    0    -     -     -     -
```

**The 31415926 row is the one to read twice.** This probe's own header has
recorded that seed as `0, 0, 0, 0` since 2026-07-28, alongside 20260722, as
evidence that the parity seeds are byte-stable. It is `0, 0, 0, 0` — and the
ladder stopped at four rungs. The fifth is 833. A ladder that stops before the
effect starts reports a clean seed, and two of the three seeds this probe
called clean are not; **20260722 is the only one that survives the longer
ladder.**

Seed 12345 was carried out to a 14-ring context and 529 delivered chunks and
**is never zero at any of the twelve widths**, while its centre chunk has been
still since depth 9. That pair of facts is the section: the interior settles,
the delivered square does not, and a gate that watches the interior watches
the half that settles.

Read the 309 and the 11 and the 5,514 side by side before drawing a trend from
any of them: the effect is **sparse and localised**, so the aggregate is noisy.
At the 10-ring rung the movers are chunks (3,-7), (2,-7) and (4,-7) — all three
on the rim, all at depth 2 — while the rung before it moved 17,194 across 49
chunks reaching four deep. What is not noisy is the sign. At 12345 and 0 the
aggregate is never zero at any width tested; at 20260722 it is zero at every
one.

**Seed 20260722 is genuinely stable, and that is the good news in this
section.** Every parity figure this project publishes at that seed is
measured on a square whose delivered chunks do not move when the square grows
— checked out to a 10-ring context here, where before this it had been
checked at one chunk of one radius.

### 31.3 Why no width converges, which is a property of the shape and not of the number

A tile of radius *r* with margin *m* decorates out to *r + m* and delivers out
to *r*. Its rim is therefore at depth *m* below the outermost decorated ring
**whatever m is**: widening the margin moves the rim outward with it and
leaves the distance unchanged. The outermost decorated ring itself decorates
against a world that does not exist beyond it, so it is wrong, and it writes
that wrongness inward — which is the reasoning `pipeline.rs` already gives for
why one ring is not enough. What it does not say is that the same argument
does not stop at two, and the ladder above is what it costs at each depth.

So there is no width to find. A margin of *m* buys a delivered interior that
is correct from depth *m* inward — and the tile's own rim is never in it.
Chasing convergence by raising the constant is chasing a fixed point that the
construction does not have; **the fix is generate-once status-based
generation over one canvas**, which is what `crates/rustmc-server/src/tiles.rs`
and `probe_margin_context.rs` both already name and what this measurement now
supports with a number instead of an argument.

What the ladder *does* buy is a price list, and the prices are bad. To make
chunk (0,0) at seed 12345 stable against one more ring takes depth 9, and a
tile of radius *r* at margin *m* generates `(2r + 1 + 2m)^2` chunks to deliver
`(2r + 1)^2`:

```text
  m    generated for a radius-2 tile (25 delivered)   for radius-10 (441)
  2                 81   (3.2x)                        625   (1.4x)
  9                361  (14.4x)                       1369   (3.1x)
```

And depth 9 is one seed, one chunk, one rung of zero after 40, 56, 41, 29, 33,
33, 8 — not a convergence. Buying 14.4x for that would be buying a number that
happened to come up.

### 31.4 The gate was under-powered on two axes, and only one of them had to be fixed

`a_chunk_does_not_depend_on_how_big_the_area_around_it_was` compared **chunk
(0,0), at seed 20260722, at tile radius 0, 1 and 2** and reported 0
differences over 196,608 blocks. Both halves of that scope were load-bearing:

* **One seed.** 20260722 is the one seed of the three where the property
  holds. Changing nothing but the seed makes the same comparison fail.
* **One chunk, and the interior one.** §30.3 put the movement in the rim of
  the delivered square; chunk (0,0) at radius 2 is at depth 4, and the rim it
  never looked at is at depth 2.

The strengthened test runs four cases — three seeds, and at seed 12345 a
second centre — and compares every delivered chunk at each rung:

```text
  seed       centre     r0 -> r1    r1 -> r2
  20260722   (0,0)             0           0
  12345      (0,0)            40        1397
  12345      (48,54)          23         658
  0          (0,0)             2          81
                                   total 2,201
```

**The 23 in that table is §30.3's 23.** Same chunk, same first position
`(768,63,865)`, same `grass_block[snowy=false] -> grass_block[snowy=true]`,
same eight-way breakdown of snow, ice, spruce leaves and a snowy grass block.
§30.3 got it from a 37x37-chunk dump against a 41x41 one; this gets it from a
5x5 generation against a 7x7, in the time it takes to generate 74 chunks. The
effect is short-range enough to be a unit test, and the only reason it was not
one is that nothing had ever pointed a tile at that chunk.

### 31.5 The injection, and the sharper version of "a gate that cannot fail"

The strengthened gate failing today is not by itself evidence that the
strengthening is what did it. So the constant was moved and both gates — the
one that shipped, kept verbatim under a second target name, and the new one —
were run at each width. `RUSTMC_DECORATE_REACH` was set to match the margin so
the parallel decoration grouping stayed at least as conservative as the reach
it is derived from.

```text
  DECORATION_MARGIN     the gate that shipped     the strengthened gate
     1                  2 passed, 0 failed        FAILED   5,753 blocks
     2  (today)         2 passed, 0 failed        FAILED   2,201 blocks
     4                  2 passed, 0 failed        FAILED   1,130 blocks
```

**Read the left column first.** A margin of 1 is not a hypothetical: it is
what this tree shipped until 2026-07-27, and `pipeline.rs` records it being
raised because one ring was measurably wrong by 604 blocks on a 25-chunk
window at seed 20260722 — *more than that window's entire disagreement with
vanilla*. The gate named for the margin passes at that value. It passes at 1,
at 2 and at 4 alike. **It does not respond to the margin at all**, which is a
sharper statement than "it could not fail": it is not a weak gate on the
right quantity, it is a gate on a different quantity that reads as one on
this.

The strengthened gate does respond, and it responds in both directions. At
margin 1 it catches the parity seed as well — `seed 20260722 centre (0,0):
radius 1 -> 2 moves 4 blocks`, at chunk (0,-1), which is a rim chunk that the
old scope never looked at. So the two axes pay separately: the seed axis
catches 12345 and 0 at today's margin, the every-delivered-chunk axis catches
20260722 at the old one.

And the right column is §31.3 again, from the other end. Widening the margin
does not remove the movement, it **redistributes** it: at margin 4 the
(48,54) case falls to 0 and 0, seed 12345 at the origin barely moves (41 and
1,088 against 40 and 1,397), and seed 0 acquires a mover it did not have at
margin 2. 5,753 -> 2,201 -> 1,130 is a decreasing sequence with no zero in it,
bought at `(2r + 1 + 2m)^2` chunks generated per tile.

### 31.6 What this branch did NOT do, so the next one does not have to re-derive it

* **The generator is untouched.** No file under `crates/rustmc-worldgen/`
  changed, `DECORATION_MARGIN` is still 2, and the injections above were made
  on the remote copy and reverted in the same script. Any adjudication in
  flight against a dump of `d049ef5a` is still valid against this branch's
  tip.
* **The margin was not raised, and raising it is not the open item.** §31.3 is
  why. The open item is generate-once status-based generation over one canvas,
  which `crates/rustmc-server/src/tiles.rs` and `probe_margin_context.rs`
  already name and which this section now supports with a ladder instead of an
  argument.
* **`tiled_world` is a T1 target, not a T0 one** (`ci/checks/test-targets.toml`,
  bucket `heavy-integration`), so the strengthened gate does not sit in every
  branch's inner loop. It costs 27 s at margin 2 against the old target's 7 s.
* **A third `ci/checks/t1-known-red.toml` entry was considered and not taken.**
  Asserting the true requirement (0 everywhere) and allowlisting the signature
  is the house pattern for a known red, and it would keep the tree stating what
  tiling actually needs. It was not taken because the requirement is not one
  ring short of true, it is unreachable by construction (§31.3) — an allowlist
  entry would be recording a permanent state as an exception — and because the
  characterisation form asserts the 20260722 zeroes *and* the 12345 non-zeroes,
  which the allowlist form cannot: an allowlisted red that quietly went green
  would still be a red the adjudicator reports, but a red that went green for
  the wrong reason is exactly the case this project keeps paying for.

### 31.7 A second instrument on the same defect, and what "exactly two rings" is and is not

Reported to this branch by the orchestrator while it was running, measured by
the `worldgen-sulfur-r18` agent at `8443f466` — **base `d049ef5a`, the same sha
this branch is based on, so it is this generator.** It is recorded here as a
reading taken elsewhere, checked against this branch's own numbers rather than
adopted:

```text
    ours r=18 vs ours r=20, changed blocks by ring
      x=46   2,020        x=48   0
      x=47     690        x=49   0
```

**It reconciles to the block with §31.1.** §30.3 put the r=18 vs r=20 residual
"in chunks x=46 and x=47, plus 3 at (79,46)", and §31.1 re-diffed that pair
here and got **2,713**. 2,020 + 690 + 3 = 2,713. Two agents, two branches, one
number.

Now the question it was passed on for: is "exactly two rings" the margin
working as designed at the dump boundary, or a coincidence of the dump extent?

**Neither, and the confusion is that two different quantities both read 2.**
Express the profile in the variable §31.2 says governs it — a chunk's depth
below the outermost *decorated* ring. `worldgen-dump full 12345 18` decorates
44..84, so ring 46 is at depth 2 and ring 48 at depth 4; the r=20 dump
decorates 42..86, so the comparison moves each ring from depth *d* to depth
*d + 2*:

```text
    ring   depth in r=18 -> in r=20    changed
     46          2 -> 4                  2,020
     47          3 -> 5                    690
     48          4 -> 6                      0
     49          5 -> 7                      0
```

* **It is not the margin working.** `DECORATION_MARGIN` of 2 *is* the claim
  that a chunk at depth 2 is finished. Depth 2 carries 2,020 blocks. A margin
  that worked would put a zero on that row, which is the orchestrator's second
  point and it is right.
* **And the 2 in "two contaminated rings" is not the 2 in the margin.** One is
  a count of rings that moved, the other is a depth, and they coincide here by
  arithmetic accident. The tile ladder in §31.2 is the counter-example: at
  seed 12345 the delivered chunk at the origin still moves at depth 7 (33
  blocks) and depth 8 (8 blocks), seven rings in. Contamination reaches depth
  3 and stops in *this* window at (64,64) and reaches depth 8 in a tile at the
  origin, at the same seed, on the same tip.

So the profile is a decay — 2,020, 690, 0, 0 — and the decay *length* is a
property of where you are standing, not of the constant. That is the same
conclusion §31.2 draws from the noise in the aggregate, reached from the
region-dump side instead of the tile side, and it is why §31.3's argument is
about the shape of the construction rather than about how far contamination
happens to travel: the rim is at depth `m` for every `m`, and depth `m`
demonstrably is not clean.

**On the silent drop, independently seen twice.** That agent reported
`region-differ` returning `chunks=1089 mismatches=10650` on an honest window
and the identical pair of numbers on a window 54% larger; §31.1 has the same
shape at `chunks=1089 mismatches=23`, unchanged from `48 48 80 80` to
`46 46 82 82`. Two different residuals, two different windows, one failure
mode. The `ebed533a` coverage refusal was run on this branch's own three
windows before any figure above was believed: exit 3 with `280 of 1369 chunks
are not minecraft:full` on the under-covered one, exit 0 on the five covered
ones.

## 32. Generate-once over one canvas: the extent half is the jar's, the read half zeroes the gate and is measurably further from vanilla (2026-08-12)

Branch `worldgen-generate-once`, off `main` **d049ef5a** with
`worldgen-decoration-margin` **3eeed9b5** underneath it, Threadripper.
§31 measured that no `DECORATION_MARGIN` converges and named the fix:
generate-once, status-based generation over one canvas, closing when
`tiled_world.rs::a_chunk_does_not_depend_on_how_big_the_area_around_it_was`
can assert `Some(0)` on all four cases.

**It can, and this section is why that is not the good news it sounds like.**
The model that produces the zeroes is measurably further from vanilla than
the one it replaces — 8,712 to 42,873 mismatches on the window below, and a
published 100.000000% falling to 99.998861% — so it ships switched off and
**99.998861% is not the model this server runs**, which is why it is
registered in `ci/figures.toml` as unquotable rather than as a fidelity
level. The half of it that comes straight out of the jar ships on, and moves
50 blocks.

Every figure below carries its floor or its control beside it, and every
window was run through `ci/ref-chunk-extent.py` before any number from it was
believed.

### 32.1 What the jar actually says, which is not a margin at all

`net/minecraft/world/level/chunk/status/ChunkPyramid`, `ChunkStep`,
`ChunkDependencies` and `net/minecraft/server/level/ChunkGenerationTask`,
`javap -p -c -constants`. `ChunkStep$Builder.addRequirement(status, radius)`
sets `direct[i] = max(direct[i], status)` for every `i <= radius`. The
`GENERATION_PYRAMID`'s twelve steps, with their direct requirements and
block-state write radius:

```text
  EMPTY                 -                                        -
  STRUCTURE_STARTS      -                                        -
  STRUCTURE_REFERENCES  STRUCTURE_STARTS @ 8                     -
  BIOMES                STRUCTURE_STARTS @ 8                     -
  NOISE                 STRUCTURE_STARTS @ 8, BIOMES @ 1         write 0
  SURFACE               STRUCTURE_STARTS @ 8, BIOMES @ 1         write 0
  CARVERS               STRUCTURE_STARTS @ 8                     write 0
  FEATURES              STRUCTURE_STARTS @ 8, CARVERS @ 1        write 1
  INITIALIZE_LIGHT      -                                        -
  LIGHT                 INITIALIZE_LIGHT @ 1                     -
  SPAWN                 BIOMES @ 1                               -
  FULL                  -                                        -
```

**The `FEATURES` row is the whole section.** A chunk may decorate once its
eight neighbours are **carved**. Vanilla does not require a decorated
neighbour of a decorating chunk at any distance, and there is no step in the
pyramid that does.

`ChunkStep$Builder.buildAccumulatedDependencies` composes a step's direct
requirements with its parent's accumulated ones, offset by
`getRadiusOfParent`, and `ChunkGenerationTask.getRadiusForLayer` reads the
layer radius for a status straight off the target step's accumulated
dependencies. `scheduleLayer` then walks that square **x-then-z** — the same
order `Area::chunks` already promises — and `scheduleChunkInLayer` skips a
chunk already at that status, which is the generate-once property. Computed
from the port and pinned in `crates/rustmc-worldgen/src/status.rs`, for a
chunk reaching `FULL`:

```text
  FEATURES @ 1     CARVERS @ 2     BIOMES @ 3     STRUCTURE_STARTS @ 11
```

So delivering a square of radius `r` needs **decoration over `r + 1` and
terrain over `r + 2`** — one ring *fewer* decorated than
`DECORATION_MARGIN = 2` decorates, over the same terrain. Our uniform margin
decorates every generated chunk including the outermost ring, and that ring
has nothing beyond it: it is §31.3's rim, and under the pyramid it does not
decorate at all.

`ChunkDependencies`'s lookup table is `first().getIndex() + 1` long, so
`getRadiusOf` throws for a status later than any neighbour requirement — for
the `FULL` step that is `LIGHT` and `SPAWN`. The port answers 0 there and
says so in its module docs rather than reproducing a throw nothing calls.

### 32.2 The one thing the jar does not decide, and vanilla is not a function of the seed there

`WorldGenRegion.getChunk(x, z, status, required)` computes the chessboard
distance from the chunk being generated, caps the status a neighbour may be
**asked** for at `generatingStep.directDependencies().get(distance)` —
`CARVERS` at distance 1 for `FEATURES` — and then returns
`GenerationChunkHolder.getChunkIfPresentUnchecked(status)`, which reads the
live `CompletableFuture` array and hands back **the same mutable
`ProtoChunk`** every other status holds. So the cap is on the request and not
on the content: what a feature observes in a neighbour is whatever the
scheduler happened to have finished. `warnIfReadOutsideWriteZone` logs a read
past the write radius, so Mojang treats reaching further as a defect, but
within the 3x3 the interleaving is undecided.

It is undecided in the measurements too, and that was already on the record
before this branch: two unmodified vanilla runs of one seed and window differ
by **32,127** blocks in the same chunk order and **146,169** across orders
(AGENT-BRIEF, ORACLE rule 29). Vanilla's world is not a function of its seed
at this seam. A port therefore has to choose, and the choice is what §32.3
prices.

### 32.3 The price of choosing the cap, measured on three arms of one binary

`RUSTMC_DECORATE_ISOLATED` selects the model, so all three arms come off one
build and one compiler:

* **`shared`** (`=0`) — the uniform `DECORATION_MARGIN`, every generated
  chunk decorating. What the tree ran until 2026-08-12.
* **`extent`** (unset — the shipped default) — the pyramid extent, decoration
  stopping one isolation reach inside the generated area, reads still live.
* **`isolated`** (`=1`) — the pyramid extent, and each chunk decorating
  against a copy of the carved canvas no other chunk has written into, its
  write list replayed onto the one canvas in the canonical order. Reads
  honour the `getChunk` cap exactly.

Seed 20260722, r=7 at centre (64,64), 225 chunks, 22,118,400 blocks,
`ci/canvas-model-price.sh`. **VANILLA FIRST.** `ci/ref-chunk-extent.py`
returned exit 0 on both references and on all three of our dumps at this
window before any of it was believed — and it earned that line: the same
check **refused** `oracle-work/van-c6458` here, `171 of 225 chunks are not
minecraft:full`, after `region-differ` had already returned a confident
83.77% for it. That figure is not quoted anywhere and is not a figure.

```text
                       van-rows-c64   van-s1-c62   vs ours(shared)
  ours(shared)              18,779        8,712        —
  ours(extent)              18,729        8,762           50
  ours(isolated)            42,085       42,873       39,796
```

Both models are byte-identical to themselves: two dumps of each, same
binary, **0 mismatches over 22,118,400 blocks**. **THE `isolated` HALF OF
THAT SENTENCE IS FALSE AND THE SAMPLE SIZE IS WHY — §33, 2026-08-12.** Two
dumps of that arm land in the same class about a third of the time; nine runs
of one binary produce **three different worlds**, and every parallel run
differs from the sequential one. The `extent` reading below is unaffected and
was re-taken at N=9 over three thread counts: still 0 on all 36 pairs. The
shipped model is also
byte-identical **at `RUSTMC_WORKERS=1` and at twelve** on that window, same
0 of 22,118,400, which is the control the shared-canvas parallel pass has
always needed and nearly lost: the isolated arm's chunks are independent by
construction so its driver is a flat work queue, and replacing the old
driver with it deleted the level barriers **the shipped model still needs**.
That left decoration running sequentially on a stage that is 47% of the
pipeline's wall at twelve workers, with every block still correct and every
gate still green. Both paths are present. The `shared` and `extent`
columns also reproduced exactly across three builds of this branch.
**The `isolated` column did not, and it is recorded rather than smoothed
over:** three builds gave 42,108 / 42,898, then 42,191 / 42,783, then the
42,085 / 42,873 above, against code changes that touched only the *other*
arm's extent. About 200 blocks in 42,000 — 0.5%, nowhere near the effect
being measured — but it is an unexplained build-sensitivity in an arm whose
whole claim is that a chunk's writes are a pure function of the seed and its
position. It is not chased here because the arm ships switched off and
nothing quotes it to better than "about 42,000"; anyone who reopens
`RUSTMC_DECORATE_ISOLATED=1` should start by explaining it.

> **EXPLAINED, AND IT IS NOT BUILD-SENSITIVITY — §33, 2026-08-12.** Those
> three readings are three draws from a run-to-run distribution, not three
> builds' answers, and no code change between them has to explain anything.
> The arm was not a function of its inputs: `spider_placed` is shared across
> the chunks one mineshaft corridor reaches, its own comment states that it is
> only safe because the parallel driver *orders* any two chunks within
> `2 * DECORATE_REACH`, and the isolated driver's flat work queue is exactly
> the deletion of that ordering. Everything in the table above survives — the
> distance is ~42k against **both** references against a reference spread of
> 10,067, so rule 6 fires either way — but the arm is now per-chunk in its
> spider memory and reproduces itself.

**Read the bottom row against both references, because that is what makes it
a finding rather than reference noise.** The two references disagree about
our `shared` output by 10,067 blocks — they are independently generated
vanilla worlds and that spread is their own floor on this window. The
`isolated` arm is +23,412 against one and +34,071 against the other, and it
lands at ~42k against **both**: rule 6 fires and the regression is real.

The `extent` arm moves **50 blocks** from `shared` on this window and scores
−50 and +50 against the two references. It is a wash, which is the answer
that lets it ship: it is the jar's declared shape rather than a uniform
margin, and it costs nothing to adopt.

**The End city window says the same thing in the dimension where we had an
exact number.** Seed 20260722, centre chunk (83,62), r=7, y 0..255,
denominator 14,745,600, vanilla first, against
`van-end-20260722-c8362` **and** its `-b` twin — the standing must-not-regress
figure of 100.000000%:

```text
  ours(extent)      100.000000%   against BOTH twins — the standing figure holds
  ours(isolated)     99.998861%   against BOTH twins, identically
```

168 blocks, and every one of them is `chorus_plant` or `chorus_flower`. The
End driver's own comment named that feature in advance — it reads the world
back on every block it writes and spreads up to 8 blocks horizontally — so
the isolated model's cost showed up exactly where the code said it would.
Both twins agreeing is rule 6 again: it is ours, not the reference's. And the
`extent` row is what makes this a *comparison*: the same re-run of the same
window under the shipped model is still exact, so 99.998861% is the read
half's price and not the branch's.

**One control on the extent arm, because ORACLE rule 14 asks for it.** The
terrain extent under `extent` is the pyramid's `CARVERS` radius of 2, which
is exactly what `DECORATION_MARGIN` generated. An earlier arm of this branch
generated terrain two rings wider than that (`decorated.grown(2)`, an extra
ring beyond what the pyramid asks for), and the widening gate reads
**0 / 0 / 66 / 608 / 23 / 986 / 12 / 2,364 under both** — identical, rung for
rung. Widening the context did not change the answer, which is what says the
narrower one is the converged extent rather than the cheaper guess. It also
re-confirms from a third direction what `docs/BENCH.md` measured over reaches
1, 2, 3, 4, 6 and 8: a decorating chunk's long reads decide nothing.

### 32.4 So the gate can be closed and closing it would be a lie

Under `isolated` the strengthened gate reads **0 on all eight rungs**:

```text
  seed       centre     r0 -> r1    r1 -> r2
  20260722   (0,0)             0           0
  12345      (0,0)             0           0
  12345      (48,54)           0           0
  0          (0,0)             0           0
```

and the same binary with `RUSTMC_DECORATE_ISOLATED=0` reproduces §31.4's
**0 / 0 / 40 / 1,397 / 23 / 658 / 2 / 81 — 2,201** to the block. The zeroes
are the model, not the instrument going quiet, and the control is one
environment variable in one build.

Under the shipped `extent` model the same eight rungs read
**0 / 0 / 66 / 608 / 23 / 986 / 12 / 2,364 — 4,059**, so all three arms are
on the record:

```text
  arm         gate total     seed 12345 (48,54) r0 -> r1     vs van-s1-c62
  shared           2,201                            23             8,712
  extent           4,059                            23             8,762
  isolated             0                             0            42,873
```

**Note what the `extent` column does and does not buy.** It removes the ring
that decorated with a void beside it — an operation `WorldGenRegion.getChunk`
answers with `Requested chunk unavailable during world generation` rather
than performing — and it leaves parity where it found it. It does **not**
reduce the extent dependence, and it should not be expected to: a delivered
chunk now misses the writes of an undecorated ring where before it received
the wrong writes of a badly decorated one, and §32.4's chain says neither is
closer to a full-world generation than the other. 4,059 against 2,201 is that
swap, not a regression in anything anyone quotes.

**And §30.3's 23 survives all of it.** Seed 12345, chunk (48,54), radius
0 -> 1, `(768,63,865)`, `grass_block[snowy=false] <-> grass_block[snowy=true]`
— exactly 23 under `shared` and exactly 23 under `extent`, which is the tie
between this gate and a 37x37-chunk region dump holding across a change to
which chunks decorate. It is 0 under `isolated`, along with everything else.

**And they are bought with §32.3.** A delivered chunk stops depending on how
big the area around it was because a chunk's feature writes stop depending on
any other chunk's feature writes — which is precisely the dependence vanilla
has and exercises. The two properties are not both available:

* a generator whose delivered chunks are extent-independent cannot let a
  feature read a neighbour's features, because the read chains outward
  without bound — chunk `c` reads the 3x3 around it, whose content is decided
  by chunks within radius 2 of `c`, whose content is decided by chunks within
  radius 4, and the finite square never closes. §31.3 measured the shadow of
  that chain and read it as a margin problem; it is not one;
* vanilla lets features read neighbours' features, and pays by not being a
  function of its own seed at that seam (32,127 blocks between two runs of
  one seed in one chunk order).

So **"a tile is exactly the slice of one pass it claims to be" is not a
property vanilla has**, and a port that acquires it has left vanilla to do
so. That retires §31's named fix rather than completing it.

### 32.5 What ships, and the trap the zeroes would have set

The `extent` arm is the default. It is a faithful port of the pyramid, it
removes the ring that decorated against a void, and it moves 50 blocks. The
`isolated` arm ships behind `RUSTMC_DECORATE_ISOLATED=1` with §32.3 attached,
because the measurement is worth keeping runnable and because the next reader
who has this idea should find it already priced.

**`tiled_world.rs` is unchanged.** Not "changed carefully" — untouched. Its
four cases were `Some(0)`, `[None, None]`, `[Some(23), None]`, `[None, None]`
and every one of those assertions still holds under the shipped model,
including the exact 23. That is worth stating because the licence to rewrite
it was on the table and did not have to be used. **What it must not become is
four `Some(0)`s**, and the reason is worth more than the decision. Eight zeroes is
also what a generator that decorated *nothing* would print — and until
2026-08-12 the gate's non-zeroes were themselves the evidence that decoration
ran. Pinning every case to zero spends that evidence and leaves a gate whose
entire content is the absence of a number, which is rule 7's shape exactly.
`tiled_world.rs::the_delivered_square_is_the_decorated_world_and_not_the_carved_one`
is the replacement: it generates one area through `Through::Carvers` and
through `Through::Decoration` and counts what decoration put there, so a
composition that dropped every write list comes back 0 and red. It exists
because the zeroes were briefly real, not as a hypothetical.

### 32.6 The next item, and what would close it

**Not a bigger margin, not read isolation, and not tiling.** All three are
now measured dead ends: §31.3, §32.3, and this section's second bullet in
§32.4.

What is left is the server-side half, and it is a different property from the
one the gate has been asking for. A player must not be able to change the
world behind them by walking; that does **not** require a delivered chunk to
be independent of the generated extent, it requires the *canvas to be one
canvas* and a chunk, once delivered, never to be written again. Under the
pyramid that is reachable and cheap: hold one canvas per world rather than
one per tile, give every chunk a status, upgrade it at most once, decorate in
a **world-anchored** order rather than a window-relative one, and deliver a
chunk only when its own 3x3 has decorated — after which nothing in the
pyramid can write into it, because `blockStateWriteRadius` is 1.

**It closes when a canvas grown from radius `r` to radius `r + 1` leaves
every chunk it had already delivered byte-identical, and the delivered set is
what `ChunkStep.getAccumulatedRadiusOf` says it may be.** That is checkable,
it can fail — deliver one ring too early and it does — and unlike the current
gate it does not ask for a property vanilla does not have. `tiles.rs` is
where it lands and `crates/rustmc-worldgen/src/status.rs` is already the
half of it that had to come out of the jar.

## 35. A chunk, once delivered, is never written again — one canvas per world, grown a ring at a time (2026-08-12)

Branch `worldgen-deliver-once`, stacked on `worldgen-generate-once`
(**dd6f029d**), Threadripper, remote dir `rustmc-once`.

§32.6 named this as the successor to the property §32.4 retired, and the
distinction is the whole section. **Extent-independence — "a tile is exactly
the slice of one pass it claims to be" — is not a property vanilla has**, and
a port that acquires it is 8,712 -> 42,873 mismatches further from vanilla on
the seed-20260722 r=7 window at (64,64) (§32.3). What a player can actually
feel is weaker: the world must not change behind them as they walk. That does
not need a delivered chunk to be independent of the generated extent. It needs
the canvas to be **one canvas** and a chunk, once handed out, never to be
written into again.

### 35.1 The two arms, one binary, one instrument

`crates/rustmc-server/tests/growing_world.rs`. Both arms take a square from
radius 0 to 1 to 2 at four seed/centre pairs, and both are scored block by
block over the **same** delivered chunks — every block of every column, y
-64..319, read through the same comparison:

* **`regenerate`** — what the tree does today. `crates/rustmc-server/src/tiles.rs`
  gives every tile its own generation of its own square, so growing means
  generating the bigger square from scratch. **This is the before.**
* **`grow`** — `crates/rustmc-server/src/growing.rs`: one canvas, a
  `ChunkStatus` per chunk, each chunk raised at most once, and a chunk
  delivered only once its own 3x3 has decorated.

The seeds and centres are `tiled_world.rs`'s, deliberately, so that the
regenerate arm has to reproduce that file's eight numbers. It does, rung for
rung:

```text
  seed       centre     r0 -> r1   r1 -> r2      arm
  20260722   (0,0)             0          0      regenerate
  12345      (0,0)            66        608      regenerate
  12345      (48,54)          23        986      regenerate
  0          (0,0)            12      2,364      regenerate
                                                 ------------
                                        4,059    total

  20260722   (0,0)             0          0      grow
  12345      (0,0)             0          0      grow
  12345      (48,54)           0          0      grow
  0          (0,0)             0          0      grow
                                                 ------------
                                            0    total
```

**4,059 -> 0**, and the 4,059 is not a figure this branch introduced: it is
§32.4's own table for the shipped `extent` model, arrived at from a different
file by a different loop. Two instruments agreeing is what says this one is
measuring the thing that one measures.

**The floor beside those numbers is zero, and it is measured rather than
assumed.** Every figure here is our output against our output, one binary, one
build, so the floor is whether this generator reproduces itself — and §32.3
took two dumps of the shipped model on the seed-20260722 r=7 window at (64,64)
and got **0 mismatches over 22,118,400 blocks**, at `RUSTMC_WORKERS=1` and at
twelve alike. No vanilla reference is involved in 33.1 to 33.4, so rule 6 has
nothing to fire on; the one figure here that *is* against vanilla is the End
city window in §35.6, and it carries its own floor (252 across six references,
§5) beside a residual of zero.

### 35.2 The zero is not the evidence, and this is the part that was nearly got wrong

Eight zeroes is also what a generator that had stopped generating would print.
§32.5 is the most recent time that nearly happened here and AGENT-BRIEF's rule
7 has paid out fourteen times, so **the injection is inside the same
measurement rather than beside it**.

Every rung snapshots the delivered square *and the ring one chunk outside it* —
which is exactly the set `GrowingWorld::delivering_early(1)` hands out, the
chunks whose own 3x3 has **not** finished decorating — and scores the two
separately off the same grown bytes:

```text
  seed       centre     r0 -> r1   r1 -> r2     what it is
  20260722   (0,0)         5,032      6,151     delivered one ring early
  12345      (0,0)         6,243      7,509
  12345      (48,54)       3,236      7,767
  0          (0,0)         6,118      8,322
                                     -------
                                      50,378    total
```

**50,378 blocks.** That is the failure the property is built to have: a world
that hands out a chunk before its 3x3 has decorated hands out a chunk the next
ring then writes into, and a player standing in it watches it change. It is
also, read the other way, the proof that growing the canvas writes anything at
all — the same growth that moved 0 blocks inside the delivered square moved
50,378 one ring out, so the zero above is the delivery rule and not a pass that
had stopped looking.

The witness is per-block and not a total: the first one at seed 20260722 is
chunk (-1,-1) at `(-16, -39, -16)`, growing radius 0 to 1.

**And it was witnessed red, twice, rather than argued.** Both injections were
applied to the tree that produces the zeroes above, run, and reverted:

```text
  injection                                   delivered-chunk blocks moved
  score the ring one chunk early as                                 50,378
    delivered (§32.6's own named failure)
  drop the generate-once filter on the                               2,971
    decoration targets, so a decorated
    chunk decorates again on the next growth
```

`a chunk that had been delivered was written again` in both, all eight rungs.
**The second is the sharper one and it is worth the paragraph.** It is a defect
in the *mechanism* rather than in the delivery rule, and it reddens at **seed
20260722 — 62 blocks at `(0,49,5)` growing radius 0 to 1, and 361 at
`(-16,55,-10)` growing 1 to 2** — where the regenerate arm reads 0 on both
rungs. So this gate is not a re-spelling of `tiled_world.rs`'s: at the seed
every parity figure in this project is taken at, there is a class of defect
that file cannot see and this one convicts.

### 35.3 Why it holds, out of the jar rather than out of the measurement

`ChunkPyramid.GENERATION_PYRAMID`'s `FEATURES` step is the only step in the
pyramid with a block-state write radius above zero, and its radius is **1**
(§32.1's table; `crates/rustmc-worldgen/src/status.rs` pins it, and
`features_is_the_only_step_that_writes_outside_its_own_chunk` is the assertion).
`NOISE`, `SURFACE` and `CARVERS` write 0 — their own chunk only — and every
step after `FEATURES` writes nothing.

So once every chunk of a chunk's own 3x3 has decorated, **nothing left in the
pyramid can reach it**: there is no later writing step, and every chunk that
could have written into it has already had its one turn. The delivery rule is
therefore not a margin and not a guess. It is
`ChunkStep.getAccumulatedRadiusOf(FEATURES)` for a chunk reaching `FULL`, read
off the ported pyramid at run time rather than written down as a 1 —
`GrowingWorld::delivery_reach`.

The generate-once half is `ChunkGenerationTask.scheduleChunkInLayer`, which
skips a chunk that has already reached the status being scheduled.
`pipeline::Resumed` carries the canvas and the status map back into
`generate_resuming`, and the noise fill, the biome containers, the surface
rule, the carvers and the decoration targets are each filtered by it. A
from-scratch pass passes `None` and every one of those filters is a test
against an empty set.

### 35.4 What it costs, and it is a saving

Each ladder was asked for its carve count. A growing world carves each chunk
once, so reaching radius 2 costs the widest square it ever holds and nothing
more:

```text
  arm          chunks carved to deliver radius 2
  grow                                        81   = the 9x9 it ends up holding
  regenerate                                 155   = 25 + 49 + 81
```

`81` is asserted rather than reported: it is
`extents_for(Area::square(c, 2)).1.chunk_count()`, so a chunk carved twice
reddens the gate. `tiles.rs`'s own note that a 5x5 tile generates 81 chunks to
deliver 25 — 69% thrown away, and thrown away again by the tile next door —
is the same arithmetic seen from the other side.

### 35.5 What this is NOT, said plainly

**A grown world is not the world one pass at the final radius would have
produced, and it cannot be.** That is §32.4 and it is not softened here: the
regenerate arm's 4,059 is the size of the difference at these four ladders, and
it does not go to zero under any margin (§31.3) or under any model that keeps
vanilla's cross-chunk feature reads (§32.3). What this section claims is
strictly weaker and strictly checkable — the chunks delivered at radius `r`
are the chunks a pass at radius `r` produced, and they stay that way.

Three specific things a reader should not take from the zero:

* **The biome and surface stages' shared `ParameterList` sees a different query
  sequence in a growing pass.** `find_value` takes `&mut self` because the
  previous result seeds the next search *and breaks its ties*, so a fresh chunk
  can get a different biome on an exact tie than a from-scratch pass would have
  given it. That is a difference between growing and regenerating; it is not a
  write into anything delivered, and it is inside the 4,059.
* **Structure starts are computed over the requested square.** A start
  discovered only once the square is bigger never reaches a chunk that has
  already decorated, because that chunk never decorates again. Again inside the
  4,059, and again not a violation.
* **The decoration order is growth-anchored, not world-anchored.** Chunks
  decorate in raster order within each growth ring, and rings in the order they
  were grown. §32.6 asked for a world-anchored order; what is built is one that
  is a function of the growth history rather than of the window, which is
  enough for this property and is **not** enough to make two differently-grown
  worlds agree. That is the next thing anyone reopening this should measure.

### 35.6 Standing state

`ci/checks/test-targets.toml` gains a `growing-world` bucket at `t1`, beside
`tiled_world`, on cost. The from-scratch path is unchanged by construction —
every filter this branch adds is a test against a set that is empty unless a
caller passed `Resumed` — and the evidence is measured rather than argued:

* `cargo test --release -p rustmc-server --test tiled_world` is **3 passed, 0
  failed** with all four cases unmoved (0 / 0 / 66 / 608 / 23 / 986 / 12 /
  2,364, and 122,560 decoration blocks over the 25 delivered chunks);
* `ci/end-city-window.sh` re-run on this branch's tip is **100.000000%**
  against `van-end-20260722-c8362` **and** its `-b` twin —
  `chunks=225 blocks=14745600 mismatches=0` on both, VANILLA FIRST. That
  window is re-run because this branch touches
  `crates/rustmc-worldgen/src/pipeline.rs`, which the End path builds its
  `Pipeline` through, and AGENT-BRIEF's standing instruction is to re-run it
  before handing off rather than to reason about whether it could have moved.
  `ci/ref-chunk-extent.py --window 76 55 90 69` exits 0 on both twins and on
  our dump, so the 225 chunks `region-differ` scored are 225 chunks that are
  `minecraft:full` on every side and not 225 it kept after silently dropping
  the rest.

`Tiles` itself is **not rewired onto `GrowingWorld` in this branch**, and that
is a scope decision rather than an oversight. §32.6's closing criterion is
about the canvas, and it is closed above; replacing the tile map with a growing
canvas changes retirement, the memory bound `docs/BENCH.md` measures, the
per-tile overlay and `MAX_CONCURRENT_GENERATIONS`, none of which this
measurement touches. That is the next item.

### The poison control on the item this hands over — it fires

The by-chunk cut names a 3,209-block tree-and-snow lump in the NW corner, and
a number is not evidence that the code a brief would go and edit is the code
that produced it. `ci/nw-corner-poison-control.sh` poisons
`TrunkPlacer#getTreeHeight` with `base + 1` — no draw moves, so the RNG stream
is untouched and only a spruce trunk's geometry changes — and re-scores the
five chunks holding the lump against `gen-wide-12345-r16-ow-c` at N=1:

