Vanilla's placement for it is `rarity_filter chance=700`, then `in_square`,
`heightmap MOTION_BLOCKING`, a `random_offset` and finally a `biome` filter to
`end_highlands`. One attempt per 700 chunks, surviving only where the position
lands in that biome. Over all three windows — 1,539 chunks, whose
end_highlands share is about 398 chunk-equivalents — the expected number of
return gateways is

```text
  (1089 x 0.2580  +  225 x 0.5200  +  225 x 0.0000) / 700  =  0.57
```

and the observed number is 0. So the End's 100.000000% is not evidence about
`end_gateway_return` in either direction: the port could place them perfectly
or not at all and every figure in this file would read the same. Measuring it
needs a window carrying roughly 1,750 end_highlands chunks, which is about
6.2x the r=16 window's end_highlands content, and no such window has been
harvested.

**The same test applied to the four features that ARE ported passes**, which is
what makes the gap a gap rather than a general complaint about coverage:

```text
  end_spike             the_end            c(0,0) r=7    40,524 obsidian,
                                                         146 iron_bars,
                                                         10 bedrock, 10 fire
  end_platform          the_end            c(0,0) r=7    fixed_placement at
                                                         exactly (100,49,0),
                                                         which is chunk (6,0)
                                                         and inside this window
                                                         and no other
  chorus_plant          end_highlands      c(64,64) r=16 12,497 chorus_plant,
                                                         1,571 chorus_flower
  end_island_decorated  small_end_islands  c(64,64) r=16 19,464,192 blocks of
                                                         the biome, 27.27%
  end_city (structure)  end_highlands      c(83,62) r=7  3,183 purpur_block,
                                                         1,164 purpur_pillar,
                                                         114 end_rod, 3 chest,
                                                         1 ender_chest
```

Every one of those is exact. `end_platform`'s is the strongest of them because
it is not statistical: the feature has a `fixed_placement` at one position in
the entire dimension, that position is inside the c(0,0) window, and it is
therefore either measured or absent with no third possibility.

**And the r=16 window measures two block families and nothing else.**
`region-differ starts` over it returns `starts=0 pieces=0`, and its whole census
is `air`, `end_stone`, `chorus_plant`, `chorus_flower`. The widest End window
this project has — the one behind `end-r16-n6-exact` — contains no structure,
no spike, no platform and no gateway. That is not an argument against the
figure, which is a true statement about 71.3 million blocks; it is the reason
the figure needed the other two windows beside it before it could be read as a
statement about the dimension.

## 36. The t2-regiondiff window has a floor, and its an exact match was a lucky pair (2026-08-16)

`README.md` sells `ci/t2-regiondiff.sh` as the **"Anvil parser+differ
determinism proof"** and `docs/ORACLE.md` records it as *"same seed generated
twice by vanilla, inner 3x3 diffed, gate demands bit-exact an exact match — **and
passes**"*. It does pass. The number is real. **It is also one draw from a
distribution nobody had sampled, and the distribution does not have its mass at
zero.**

### What was measured

Eight independent vanilla harvests of the identical window — seed **20260722**,
r=2, centre chunk **(64,64)**, overworld, `GEN_ORDER=square`, pinned jar sha1
`823e2250d24b3ddac457a60c92a6a941943fcd6a` — diffed pairwise over the gate's own
inner 3x3 square, `--bbox 63 63 65 65`, denominator **884,736** blocks. Two of
the eight are the frozen fixture `oracle-work/t2-regiondiff/{a,b}` harvested
2026-07-22 19:55/19:56; six (`h1`..`h6`) were taken 2026-08-16 13:52–13:54 on
sayer-desktop. All 28 pairs, vanilla first, lower index first:

```text
   mismatches of 884,736     pairs
        0                        4
       15                        2
       79                        6
       94                        3
      129                        3
      208                        2
      223                        1
      407                        3
      486                        2
      501                        1
      536                        1
```

(Given in blocks deliberately. Rendering each row as a percentage would put
eleven six-decimal figures into this document that a reader could mistake for
eleven results; there is one result and it is the spread.)

**Floor 0..536 blocks at N=8, median 94, mean 180.1** — i.e. exactness on this
window runs **99.939417%** at worst to an exact match at best, median
**99.989375%**. Neither endpoint nor the median is a reading anyone may quote: each is **not a
level** but one draw of a spread, and all three are registered unquotable on
exactly that ground. The finding is the spread, not any row of it. The eight runs fall into
**five distinct worlds**:

```text
  { frozen-a, frozen-b, h4 }   { h1 }   { h2 }   { h3, h6 }   { h5 }
```

### Which of the two numbers is right: neither, as a fixed value

The item that opened this asked whether **an exact match** (the frozen pair) or
**99.998079%** (a fresh harvest) is correct. 99.998079% of 884,736 is **17
blocks**. Seventeen is not in the table above, and that is the answer: it is
simply another draw. Seventeen blocks is **not a level** either; no row of this ensemble is. There
is no single true reading of this window, because
*two unmodified vanilla runs of one seed, one window and one chunk order do not
agree*. Every figure of the form "vanilla-vs-vanilla on the t2-regiondiff window
is X%" carries a floor of 0..536 at N=8 and must quote it.

### It is NOT a stale fixture, and the control is decisive

**`h4` reproduces the frozen pair bit-for-bit — 0 of 884,736 against both `a`
and `b`.** A harvest taken today, with today's tree, lands exactly on a pair
taken 25 days ago. So nothing changed underneath the fixture: not the jar (same
sha1, mtime 2026-07-22 19:29, i.e. 26 minutes *before* the fixture itself), not
the dumper, not the reference set. The frozen pair is a reachable outcome of the
current harvest path. It is not stale; it is lucky.

### It is NOT chunk order, and that was checked first

This repo has withdrawn a headline over exactly this axis — an x-major dump
against z-major references, which the admissible instrument prices at **117,628
blocks on a candidate that is vanilla** (§15, §16). So it was ruled out before
anything else was concluded:

* all eight runs carry `gen_order=square` in their own `HARVEST-ORDER` stamp
  (the six fresh ones) or were produced by the same default path (the frozen
  pair, harvested before stamping existed 2026-08-10);
* **both sides of every pair are vanilla harvests through one code path**, so
  there is no dump order to mismatch — unlike a rustmc-against-reference
  comparison, this instrument has no second traversal to get wrong;
* and the `h4`-reproduces-frozen result closes it: a same-order run reaching the
  frozen pair exactly means the frozen pair's order is the order the fresh runs
  use.

### What it IS: `GEN_ORDER=square` leaves chunk arrival to the scheduler

`ci/gen-vanilla-region.sh`'s own header claims of the single-shot square path
that *"that order is deterministic for a given extent — two runs at one centre
are bit-identical"*. **That sentence is false and this section refutes it.** One
`forceload add` over the whole square hands 25 chunks to `ChunkMap`, which
dispatches every status upgrade onto `Util.backgroundExecutor()` — a thread pool
sized `availableProcessors() - 1`. Which chunk reaches FEATURES first is a race,
and features write one chunk out.

The confusion table for the widest pair (`h1` -> `h5`, 536 blocks) names the
mechanism, and it is the one §11 already identified for the r=16 floor —
features that **read the world before they write it**:

```text
  229  minecraft:sulfur    ->  minecraft:cinnabar    y -10..31
  115  minecraft:cinnabar  ->  minecraft:sulfur      y  -7..24
   25  minecraft:seagrass  ->  minecraft:water       y  45..50
   24  minecraft:water     ->  minecraft:seagrass    y  45..49
   20  minecraft:tall_seagrass[half=upper] -> water  y  47..51
   15  minecraft:sulfur_spike[...]         -> water  y  26..28
   11  minecraft:granite   ->  minecraft:andesite    y  10..17
```

344 of 536 are the order-inverted stone-variant ore pair `sulfur`/`cinnabar` —
the same row §21 adjudicated on the r=16 window — plus `granite`/`andesite`,
speleothem `sulfur_spike`, and seagrass on a sea floor. Mismatches concentrate in
the window's **edge** chunks (`(65,64)`: 382 of 536; `(65,65)`: 83; `(65,63)`:
66; `(64,64)`: 5), which is what a one-chunk-out feature write looks like: the
inner margin of 1 the gate applies protects against features from *outside* the
generated square, not against the race between chunks *inside* it.

### What this costs the project

Nothing that was measured against a **fixed** reference set moves: a diff against
a pinned reference is still a deterministic function of the candidate, and every
delta taken that way stands. What moves is any claim of the form *"the harvest is
reproducible"*, and there is exactly one load-bearing instance: the sentence in
`ci/gen-vanilla-region.sh` quoted above, which this section corrects, and the
`README.md` line calling `ci/t2-regiondiff.sh` a determinism proof.

**And the gate itself was never a parser+differ proof.** Its green is produced by
two harvests agreeing; a differ that returns zero mismatches for every input, or
a parser that reads every chunk as empty, passes it unchanged. That is this
project's central defect class sitting inside the instrument the parity figures
are produced with. §37 is the repair.

## 37. The harvest floor is concurrency, and one system property removes it (2026-08-16)

§36 established that the `ci/t2-regiondiff.sh` window has a floor — eight
harvests, five distinct worlds, 0..536 blocks of 884,736. This section says
**what causes it**, and the answer is not the one §11 gives for the r=16 floor.

### Two arms, on top of §36's eight square-path runs

Same seed, radius, centre and window throughout; sayer-desktop, 2026-08-16.

**Arm P — `GEN_ORDER=perchunk`**, four runs. One `forceload add` per chunk, in
fixed row-major order, the next not sent until the previous chunk is FULL on
disk. Six pairs:

```text
  p1 p2   79      p2 p3    0
  p1 p3   79      p2 p4    0
  p1 p4   79      p3 p4    0
```

Floor **0..79 at N=4** — much tighter than the square path's 0..536, and **not
zero**. Serialising the *request* order does not serialise generation. This is
consistent with §11's finding that `perchunk` does not remove the overworld
floor, and it means the ordering knobs are not the mechanism here.

**Arm T — `-Dmax.bg.threads=1`**, four runs, square path otherwise unchanged.
All six pairs:

```text
  t1 t2   0      t1 t3   0      t1 t4   0
  t2 t3   0      t2 t4   0      t3 t4   0
```

**Floor exactly 0 at N=4, an exact match on every pair.** And the arm is not
trivially agreeing with everything — it produces a *specific* world, distinct
from the square-path ones: `t1` against `h1` is 136, against `h5` 414, against
`h4` 7, against `p1` 156.

### The mechanism, out of the bytecode

`net/minecraft/util/Util.maxAllowedExecutorThreads()` is
`Mth.clamp(availableProcessors() - 1, 1, getMaxThreads())`, and `getMaxThreads()`
is `Integer.parseInt(System.getProperty("max.bg.threads"))` accepted in 1..255,
defaulting to 255. `ChunkMap` dispatches every chunk status upgrade onto
`Util.backgroundExecutor()`. On a 24-thread box that is 23 workers racing to
carry 25 chunks to FEATURES, and features write one chunk out — so whichever
chunk decorates first wins the overlap. Clamped to one worker there is no race
and the order is a function of the request sequence alone.

`max.bg.threads` is the only system property in the server jar that touches
worldgen (`ci/vanilla-server.sh` header, read 2026-08-06). Setting it patches
nothing: it is a property the unmodified server reads for itself, so the
clean-room position is unchanged.

### The correction this forces

`ci/gen-vanilla-region.sh`'s header claimed of the single-shot square path that
*"that order is deterministic for a given extent — two runs at one centre are
bit-identical"*. **That is false and is corrected in this commit.** It is true
only under the clamp. Every reference in `oracle-work/` harvested without it is
one draw from a distribution rather than *the* world of its seed and window —
which does not invalidate any delta taken against a fixed reference set, because
such a delta is still a deterministic function of the candidate, but it does
mean a reference is not reproducible from its stamp alone. References should be
taken under the clamp from here on; `HARVEST-ORDER` should record it.

### What it buys immediately

`ci/t2-regiondiff.sh`'s live half can honestly demand `an exact match` from two
fresh harvests — a determinism proof over input that genuinely changes, rather
than over a fossil. Its red-when-broken evidence is one word: `ci/regiondiff-
falsify.sh` arm 4 removes the clamp and re-harvests, and measured **79
mismatches** on the run of 2026-08-16. §36's table is the population that arm
samples: 24 of 28 unclamped pairs are non-zero, so roughly one run in seven
lands on zero anyway, and the arm says so rather than treating a lucky pair as a
failure of the gate.

### The nulls, so nobody re-runs them

* **`GEN_ORDER=perchunk` is not a fix.** 0..79 at N=4. Killed here.
* **The jar did not move.** sha1 `823e2250d24b3ddac457a60c92a6a941943fcd6a`,
  mtime 2026-07-22 19:29 — 26 minutes before the frozen fixture was taken.
* **It is not chunk order.** All eight §36 runs are `gen_order=square`, both
  sides of every pair come from one harvest path, and a fresh square run (`h4`)
  reproduces the 2026-07-22 fixture at 0 of 884,736.
* **It is not a stale fixture.** Same evidence: `h4` reaches it exactly.

## 38. The clamp does not hold at r=16 — it removes 45% of the harvest floor and none of its non-zero-ness (2026-08-17)

§37 clamped `-Dmax.bg.threads=1` over a 25-chunk generation and got an exact
match on every pair: floor exactly 0 at N=4. That result was taken as the
opening of a door. **Every floor this project publishes is an unclamped floor** —
overworld 3,006 and 4,105, nether 48, the End 252, and the N=5 admissible floor
that `docs/PARITY.md` prints beside its one quotable worldgen exactness — and if
the clamp zeroed the floor at the scale those figures are taken at, then the
r=16 overworld window would carry a quotable exactness for the first time in
this project's history.

**It does not. The measurement is below and the answer is no.**

### The window, and why the arms are 4 and 6

Seed 12345, r=16, centre chunk (64,64), 1,089 chunks, bbox `48 48 80 80`,
107,053,056 blocks, `GEN_ORDER=rows` on both arms, sayer-desktop, 2026-08-17.
This is the same window `docs/PARITY.md` publishes 69,288 / 99.935277% on, so

(That headline is registered **superseded** in `ci/figures.toml` — the matched-order retake replaced it — and is quoted here only to name the window.)
whatever this says lands directly on the project's headline rather than beside
it.

The clamped arm is six references rather than four because the property under
test is a **scheduling** one. `k1..k4` were taken back to back on a quiet box;
`k5` and `k6` hours later under a load average near 15. Four runs sampled from
one scheduler state is the weakest evidence a scheduling claim can rest on.

### ARM 1 — CONTROL, 4 unclamped references, 6 pairs

```text
  c d  17779    c e  16320    c f  15869
  d e  16712    d f  16285    e f  13317
```

Range **13,317..17,779**, median **16,302.5**.

This arm runs first and the script refuses if any pair of it comes back zero.
That is not a courtesy. The result arm 2 was looking for is a row of zeros, and
a row of zeros is also exactly what a wrong bbox, a differ that stopped
counting, a reference path resolving four times to one directory, or a short
harvest all print. These four references are known to disagree, they are read by
the same differ through the same bbox in the same run, and if they go quiet then
the instrument is what is quiet. That is the whole falsification argument and it
needs no injection harness: the two arms differ in one flag and one of them is a
known non-zero.

### ARM 2 — CLAMPED, 6 references, all 15 pairs

```text
  k1 k2  9433   k1 k3  9511   k1 k4  8678   k1 k5  9488   k1 k6  8951
  k2 k3  7790   k2 k4  6829   k2 k5  9245   k2 k6  9255   k3 k4  7149
  k3 k5  7585   k3 k6  9307   k4 k5  8118   k4 k6  8526   k5 k6  9738
```

Range **6,829..9,738**, median **8,951**, and **15 of 15 pairs non-zero**. That
range is a floor and a spread; it is **not a level** and nothing may be scored
against it.

Every reference carries `bg_threads=1` in its own `HARVEST-ORDER`, read back off
the artefact by the script rather than asserted by it — ORACLE rule 30, an
unrecognised knob is not a turned one. The clamp reached the JVM. It simply did
not do at 1,089 chunks what it did at 25.

**Median floor 16,302.5 → 8,951 is a 45.1% reduction.** Quote the medians rather
than the ranges when comparing the arms: the clamped arm has 15 draws and the
control 6, so its max is the max of a larger sample and its range is wider for a
reason that has nothing to do with the clamp.

### ARM 3 — the clamp under the other request order

`kr-a` vs `kr-b`, both clamped, both `GEN_ORDER=rows_rev`: **13,311** at N=2, one
pair. Not zero either, and sitting inside the *unclamped* range rather than the
clamped one — but it is a single pair and nothing should be built on it beyond
"the clamp does not zero this order either".

### ARM 4 — cross-order, the positive control on the harness

```text
  k1 kr-a  87120    k1 kr-b  88888    k2 kr-a  85750    k2 kr-b  87352
```

Roughly **ten times** the clamped floor. The request order still reaches the
world under the clamp, exactly as §11 and §15 require, so the instrument
distinguishes things it should distinguish and arm 2's numbers are not small
because the differ has stopped looking.

### ARM 5 — is the clamped world somewhere else entirely?

Vanilla first, unclamped against clamped `k1`:

```text
  c k1  12375    d k1  13250    e k1  13316    f k1  12493
```

**12,375..13,316 — at or just below the bottom of the unclamped-vs-unclamped
range.** The clamped world is an ordinary member of the population the unclamped
runs draw from, not a different world reached by a different code path. That
matters for the choice `docs/PARITY.md` now has to make: a clamped reference is
not a laboratory artefact that our server would have to be re-aimed at.

### The first explanation, and the ladder that killed it

The obvious reading of §37-zero against §38-non-zero is that the clamp holds
locally and only fails once enough of the world is in view at once — 25 chunks
racing on one worker is fine, 1,089 is not. `ci/clamped-floor-ladder.sh` tests
that directly and it is free: it re-reads **the same six clamped r=16
references** through progressively narrower concentric bounding boxes. Nothing
is regenerated, so the seed, the clamp, the harvest order, the generated extent
and the decoration margin are all held fixed by construction and the only thing
that varies is how much of the world is being *looked at*. A narrow rung is not
a narrow generation — features from outside it were already written into it by
the harvest that produced both sides — so ORACLE rule 14's margin question does
not arise. Restricting a comparison and shrinking a window are different
operations and only the first is happening here.

```text
  r    chunks   blocks       UNCLAMPED N=4 (6 pairs)   CLAMPED N=6 (15 pairs)
  1    9        884,736      0..55      (5/6 nz)       0..32     (12/15 nz)
  2    25       2,457,600    25..278    (6/6 nz)       6..308    (15/15 nz)
  4    81       7,962,624    835..1343  (6/6 nz)       214..889  (15/15 nz)
  8    289      28,409,856   3760..5019 (6/6 nz)       1579..3456 (15/15 nz)
```

**Read through a 9-chunk box the clamped references still disagree**, on 12 of
15 pairs. So the failure is not a threshold that trips somewhere between 25
chunks and 1,089 — it is present at low density everywhere, and the r=16 figure
is that density summed over 1,089 chunks rather than a new phenomenon. The
unclamped arm is non-zero at every rung, so no rung's zeros are a quiet bbox.

Note also that the clamp buys nothing measurable at the smallest rungs and about
half at the largest. That is what a low-density difference looks like when the
count is small: at r=1 the whole floor is tens of blocks and the arms are not
separable; by r=8 they plainly are.

### What is left, and it is a real open question

The ladder holds fixed the two things §37 and this section actually differ in:
the **seed** (20260722 there, 12345 here) and the **generated extent** (25 chunks
there, 1,089 here). One of those is why §37 saw zero. `ci/clamp-extent-control.sh`
separates them by generating at seed 12345 with §37's extent — four clamped and
three unclamped harvests at r=2 — and is running as this section is written. Its
answer is recorded in its own section rather than predicted here.

### What this does to the headline, and what it does not

**The r=16 overworld window does not become quotable through the clamp**, and
that was the single most valuable thing this branch could have found tonight, in
whichever direction it came out. A clamped ensemble is still a floor of thousands
of blocks on this window and there is no exact-match route to a level here.

What is real and does transfer:

* **The clamp is worth 45% of the harvest floor at r=16**, on this window, at
  these Ns. That is a large and cheap improvement in resolving power and it costs
  one system property on the reference side only.
* **Nothing about our generator changed and nothing needs to.** Both arms are
  vanilla against vanilla. This is a statement about how reproducible the
  *reference* is, which is the denominator of every parity claim here.
* **Every floor in this project is still an unclamped floor**, and each one is
  now known to be an over-estimate of the irreducible floor by an unmeasured
  amount that is 45% on the one window where it has been measured. That is not a
  correction to any published figure — a floor quoted too wide makes a figure
  *less* quotable, never falsely more — but it does mean a re-take is worth real
  resolving power wherever a figure is currently blocked by its floor.

`ci/clamped-floor-r16.sh`, `ci/clamped-floor-ladder.sh`. Neither is in `ci/t0.sh`
and the reason is in their headers: both reference roots hold ~22 MB per
reference, neither is in the repository, and a check pinned to a path the gate
cannot see passes on the box that made the fixture and refuses everywhere else.
What *is* gated is the differ underneath them — `ci/regiondiff-falsify.sh` runs
in T0 and injects a parser fault, a differ that stops counting and a differ that
drops a chunk. Every number above rests on that.

## 39. The clamp is exact over the interior of a small harvest, and what breaks it is how many chunks were generated at once (2026-08-17)

§38 left one question open and got it wrong on the first pass. **An earlier
draft of this section, committed and pushed, claimed that §37's exact match was
"the mode drawn four times" and that the clamp only ever narrows the harvest to
a few discrete worlds. The measurement below refutes that, and it is my own
follow-up that refuted it rather than anyone else's.** What follows is the
corrected finding; the wrong version is recorded here because it was published,
however briefly, and because the way it went wrong is the more useful half.

### The confound, and the arms that resolve it

§37 clamped `-Dmax.bg.threads=1` over a small harvest at seed 20260722 and got
an exact match on all six pairs. §38 clamped it over a 1,089-chunk harvest at
seed 12345 and got 15 of 15 pairs non-zero. Two variables moved: the **seed**
and the **generated extent**. `ci/clamped-floor-ladder.sh` had already shown
that how much of the world you *look at* is not a third one.

`ci/clamp-extent-control.sh` generates four clamped and three unclamped
harvests at r=2, at either seed, and the references are then read through two
bounding boxes: the **inner 9 chunks** (`63 63 65 65`, 884,736 blocks — the
window §37 and `ci/t2-regiondiff.sh` actually use) and the **25-chunk box**
(`62 62 66 66`). sayer-desktop, 2026-08-17, `GEN_ORDER=rows` throughout.

```text
  seed 20260722, r=2 harvest
    inner 9 chunks    CLAMPED  0 0 0 0 0 0            all six pairs exact
                      control  10 / 17 / 27           (instrument live)
    25-chunk box      CLAMPED  e1 = e2 = e4; e3 off all three by 187
                      control  17 / 141 / 158

  seed 12345, r=2 harvest
    inner 9 chunks    CLAMPED  0 0 0 0 0 0            all six pairs exact
                      control  0 / 89 / 89
    25-chunk box      CLAMPED  e1 = e3 = e4; e2 off all three by 190
                      control  5 / 143 / 148

  seed 12345, r=16 harvest  (§38's ensemble, read through the same boxes)
    inner 9 chunks    CLAMPED  0..32, 12 of 15 pairs non-zero
    full 1,089        CLAMPED  6,829..9,738, 15 of 15 non-zero
```

### What that says, in three regimes

1. **Over the interior of a small harvest the clamp is exact, at both seeds.**
   Six of six pairs at zero, twice, against a control that is not. §37's result
   is reproduced at a second seed and **stands as written for its own window**.
2. **At the periphery of that same harvest it is not.** Widen the box from 9
   chunks to 25 and a discrete second world appears, at both seeds and with the
   same shape: three harvests identical, one off all three by a single constant
   (187 and 190). That is the boundary ring, where a chunk's decoration depends
   on neighbours at the edge of what was requested.
3. **At a large generated extent the clamp is exact nowhere**, the interior
   included — 12 of 15 pairs disagree over the very same 9 chunks that are
   exact when only a small region was generated.

**So the variable is the number of chunks generated in one harvest, not the
seed and not the size of the window you read.** The seed is dead as an
explanation: the two seeds behave identically in all three regimes.

### Why the first draft got it backwards, and it is worth keeping

The earlier draft compared §37's six zeros against a 25-chunk reading of a
25-chunk harvest, saw three non-zero pairs, and concluded §37 had drawn a mode.
**The two readings were of different boxes.** §37's window is the inner 9
chunks, 884,736 blocks; the control script's default box is 25 chunks. Nothing
about the clamp changed between those two numbers — only the `--bbox` did, and
the constant 187 lives entirely outside the smaller one.

That is ORACLE rule 14 in the form that actually bites: **the answer was still
moving when the width changed, and the first draft quoted it before it
converged.** The published caveat everywhere else in this project is to widen
the context until the answer stops changing. Here widening changed a *negative*
result into a positive one, which is the direction nobody checks.

It is also rule 7 paying out again: the first draft's measurement cleared a
candidate — "§37 was under-powered" — a little too cleanly, and the thing to
suspect was the measurement.

### `ci/t2-regiondiff.sh` is sound, and this section retracts the doubt

The earlier draft warned that the gate's LIVE part, which harvests two fresh
clamped references and asserts an exact match, was asserting a
coincidence with an unmeasured failure rate. **It is now measured on the gate's
own window and the rate is zero over six pairs**: four independent clamped
harvests of seed 20260722 at r=2 agree exactly over `63 63 65 65`, while three
unclamped ones disagree by 10, 17 and 27 over the same box. The gate is
operating in regime 1, which is the regime where the clamp is exact. Its header
and its failure message are restored accordingly in this commit — the warning
they briefly carried was wrong and a wrong warning on a live gate is worse than
none, because it tells an operator to re-run rather than investigate.

### What still stands from §38

Everything. §38's arms are all at r=16, which is regime 3, and nothing here
touches them: the clamped floor there is 6,829..9,738 over 15 of 15 non-zero
pairs, a 45.1% median reduction and not an elimination, and **the r=16
overworld window still does not become quotable through the clamp**.

### What this changes about taking references

* **A clamped reference is reproducible only if it was harvested small.** That
  is a usable property and a new one: the `ci/t2-regiondiff.sh` fixture regime —
  a small extent, read through its interior — genuinely has a floor of zero, and
  a gate built that way can assert an exact match honestly.
* **A clamped r=16 reference is not reproducible**, and no stamp on it says so.
  `ci/gen-vanilla-region.sh`'s header is corrected in this commit to state the
  extent condition rather than to promise reproducibility outright.
* **Never quote a floor without the box it was read through.** Two numbers from
  one ensemble, 9 chunks and 25 chunks, differ here between "exactly zero" and
  "a second world exists". A floor belongs to the pair of runs that produced it,
  and equally to the bbox that read it.

`ci/clamp-extent-control.sh`, `EXTENT_SEED`/`EXTENT_PORTBASE`. It writes only
under `$EXTENT_DEST`, touches no existing reference, and uses ports
25971..25977 and 25981..25987 once, ever.

---
